# CONTROL ARCHITECTURE LAYER


# AICA-5 — AI CONTROL ARCHITECTURE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Control Architecture Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Control Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The AI Control Architecture (AICA-5) provides the control architecture that makes every AI decision traceable, attributable, and correctable. It consists of five layers and 25 control nodes, organized in a strict dependency chain that ensures constitutional compliance flows from foundational reasoning through execution, authority, continuity, and accountability.

AICA-5 is derived from ICC-10 I1–I10. Each layer's validity is a precondition for the next. This dependency chain prevents cascade failure—if the Cognitive Layer fails, nothing downstream can operate with constitutional authority.

**The central insight:** Control without dependency is noise. AICA-5's dependency chain ensures that each layer's validity is a precondition for the next.

---

## THE FIVE LAYERS

| Layer | Nodes | Function | Dependency |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Cognitive (C-N1 to C-N5)** | 5 | Intelligence sourcing, inference architecture, signal validation, output calibration, knowledge boundary management | Foundation — no upstream dependency |
| **Execution (E-N1 to E-N5)** | 5 | Task decomposition, pipeline orchestration, concurrency governance, monitoring loops, exception handling | Depends on Cognitive Layer validity |
| **Authority (A-N1 to A-N5)** | 5 | Trigger rights, binding thresholds, override protocols, escalation paths, delegation boundaries | Depends on Cognitive and Execution Layer validity |
| **Continuity (Co-N1 to Co-N5)** | 5 | State establishment, drift detection, approval expiry, handoff integrity, context restoration | Depends on Cognitive, Execution, and Authority Layer validity |
| **Accountability (Ac-N1 to Ac-N5)** | 5 | Decision lineage, responsibility attribution, outcome validation, rollback pathways, learning integration | Depends on all previous layers; Ac-N5 is bidirectional |

---

## LAYER 1: COGNITIVE (C-N1 TO C-N5)

**Function:** Governs how intelligence is sourced, validated, and calibrated before any decision is made. This is the constitutional foundation—if cognitive inputs are compromised, everything downstream is invalid.

### C-N1 — Intelligence Sourcing

**Statement:** All intelligence used in institutional actions must be sourced from credentialed, declared, and auditable sources. Anonymous, unverifiable, or undeclared sources are not permitted for material actions. IPS Framework O-N6 monitoring applies to intelligence sources.

**Violation Condition:** Intelligence from undeclared or uncredentialed sources used in material decision-making.

**Enforcement:** Source credentials verified at session initialization. IMP records all sources with provenance. O-N6 monitors for IPS infiltration of intelligence sources.

### C-N2 — Inference Architecture

**Statement:** The reasoning architecture—models, algorithms, and inference pathways—must be declared, documented, and auditable. Black-box inference without declared architecture is not permitted for material actions.

**Violation Condition:** Undeclared inference architecture used for material decision-making.

**Enforcement:** Inference architecture declared in Role Specification. IMP records architecture with provenance. AICA-5 Maturity Grid assesses architecture transparency.

### C-N3 — Signal Validation

**Statement:** All signals used in inference must be validated for accuracy, completeness, and freedom from manipulation. Unvalidated signals may not be used for material actions. IPS Framework detection applies to signal manipulation.

**Violation Condition:** Unvalidated signals used in material decision-making; signals manipulated by IPS.

**Enforcement:** Signal validation protocol executed before each session. IMP records validation status. O-N6 monitors for signal manipulation.

### C-N4 — Output Calibration

**Statement:** All outputs must be calibrated against declared thresholds for confidence, uncertainty, and risk. Uncalibrated outputs may not be used for material actions.

**Violation Condition:** Uncalibrated outputs used in material decision-making.

**Enforcement:** Output calibration protocol executed before delivery. IMP records calibration status. AOBA audits output for bias.

### C-N5 — Knowledge Boundary Management

**Statement:** All knowledge used must be within declared boundaries. Knowledge outside declared boundaries must be escalated for review. IPS Framework detection applies to knowledge boundary violations.

**Violation Condition:** Knowledge outside declared boundaries used in material decision-making.

**Enforcement:** Knowledge boundary declared in Role Specification. IMP records knowledge usage. O-N6 monitors for knowledge boundary violations.

---

## LAYER 2: EXECUTION (E-N1 TO E-N5)

**Function:** Governs how tasks are decomposed, orchestrated, and monitored. Execution is the translation of cognitive outputs into action.

### E-N1 — Task Decomposition

**Statement:** All tasks must be decomposed into declarable, auditable subtasks. Undeclared or un-auditable decomposition is not permitted for material actions.

**Violation Condition:** Task decomposition not declared or auditable.

**Enforcement:** Task decomposition declared in session initialization. IMP records decomposition with provenance.

### E-N2 — Pipeline Orchestration

**Statement:** All pipelines—sequences of subtasks—must be orchestrated by declared owners with documented authority. Unauthorized orchestration is not permitted.

**Violation Condition:** Pipeline orchestration by undeclared owners or without documented authority.

**Enforcement:** Orchestration owners declared in Role Specification. IMP records orchestration with provenance.

### E-N3 — Concurrency Governance

**Statement:** Concurrent execution must be governed by declared concurrency protocols. Uncontrolled concurrency is not permitted for material actions.

**Violation Condition:** Uncontrolled concurrency in material execution.

**Enforcement:** Concurrency protocols declared in session initialization. IMP records concurrency state.

### E-N4 — Monitoring Loops

**Statement:** All execution must be monitored with declared monitoring loops. Unmonitored execution is not permitted for material actions. IPS Framework monitoring applies to execution.

**Violation Condition:** Unmonitored execution in material actions.

**Enforcement:** Monitoring loops declared in session initialization. IMP records monitoring state. O-N6 monitors execution for IPS signals.

### E-N5 — Exception Handling

**Statement:** All exceptions must be handled by declared exception protocols. Unhandled exceptions must escalate to HAN. IPS-related exceptions require immediate HAN escalation.

**Violation Condition:** Unhandled exception in material execution.

**Enforcement:** Exception protocols declared in session initialization. IMP records exception handling. O-N6 monitors exceptions for IPS signals.

---

## LAYER 3: AUTHORITY (A-N1 TO A-N5)

**Function:** Governs who or what may initiate actions, under what conditions, with what override capability, and through what escalation paths. Authority is the constitutional gatekeeper—no action without traceable authority assignment.

### A-N1 — Trigger Rights

**Statement:** Who or what may initiate executable actions must be declared and auditable. Undeclared trigger rights are not permitted. IPS Framework monitors trigger rights for capture.

**Violation Condition:** Action initiated by undeclared trigger rights; trigger rights captured by IPS.

**Enforcement:** Trigger rights declared in Role Specification. IMP records trigger rights assignment. O-N6 monitors trigger rights for IPS signals.

### A-N2 — Binding Thresholds

**Statement:** The conditions under which actions become binding must be declared and auditable. Undeclared binding thresholds are not permitted.

**Violation Condition:** Action becoming binding under undeclared thresholds.

**Enforcement:** Binding thresholds declared in Role Specification. IMP records threshold crossing.

### A-N3 — Override Protocols

**Statement:** The conditions and mechanisms for human override must be declared, documented, and tested. Undeclared or untested override protocols are not permitted. Override authority extends to IPS-related suspensions.

**Violation Condition:** Override protocols undeclared, untested, or not executable by non-technical principals.

**Enforcement:** Override protocols declared in Constitutional Charter. Tested by non-technical principal per governance cycle. IMP records override execution. HAN retains override authority.

### A-N4 — Escalation Paths

**Statement:** The routing of decisions and exceptions exceeding current authority levels must be declared and auditable. Undeclared escalation paths are not permitted. IPS escalation paths are pre-defined and tested.

**Violation Condition:** Escalation through undeclared paths; IPS escalation undefined or untested.

**Enforcement:** Escalation paths declared in Role Specification. IMP records escalation routing. IPS escalation paths tested annually.

### A-N5 — Delegation Boundaries

**Statement:** The scope, duration, and revocation of delegated authority must be declared and auditable. Undeclared delegation boundaries are not permitted. Delegation may not include IPS escalation authority.

**Violation Condition:** Delegation beyond declared boundaries; delegation of IPS escalation authority.

**Enforcement:** Delegation boundaries declared in Role Specification. IMP records delegation. A-N5 is non-delegable to AI agents.

---

## LAYER 4: CONTINUITY (Co-N1 TO CO-N5)

**Function:** Governs state establishment, drift detection, approval expiry, handoff integrity, and context restoration. Continuity is the guardrail that ensures institutional memory and context persistence.

### Co-N1 — State Establishment

**Statement:** A declared, auditable baseline state must be established before any material action begins. Undeclared baseline states are not permitted.

**Violation Condition:** Material action without declared baseline state.

**Enforcement:** Baseline state declared in session initialization. IMP records baseline state.

### Co-N2 — Drift Detection

**Statement:** Drift from declared baselines must be detected, logged, and escalated. Undetected drift is not permitted. IPS drift requires immediate escalation.

**Violation Condition:** Undetected drift from declared baseline; IPS drift undetected.

**Enforcement:** Drift detection protocols declared in session initialization. IMP records drift. O-N6 monitors for IPS drift.

### Co-N3 — Approval Expiry

**Statement:** All approvals have declared expiry. Approvals beyond expiry require reauthorization. IPS-related approvals require more frequent renewal.

**Violation Condition:** Action beyond approval expiry; IPS-related approvals not renewed.

**Enforcement:** Approval expiry declared in Role Specification. IMP records approval status. IPS approvals reviewed quarterly.

### Co-N4 — Handoff Integrity

**Statement:** Handoffs between agents or sessions must preserve declared context. Context loss is not permitted. IPS context must be preserved.

**Violation Condition:** Context loss during handoff; IPS context lost.

**Enforcement:** Handoff Package declared in session initialization. IMP records handoff. IPS context included in Handoff Package.

### Co-N5 — Context Restoration

**Statement:** Restored context must match declared baseline. Context mismatch is not permitted and must be escalated.

**Violation Condition:** Context mismatch during restoration.

**Enforcement:** Context restoration declared in session initialization. IMP records restoration. Mismatch escalated to HAN.

---

## LAYER 5: ACCOUNTABILITY (Ac-N1 TO AC-N5)

**Function:** Governs decision lineage, responsibility attribution, outcome validation, rollback pathways, and learning integration. Accountability is the constitutional completion—it closes the loop from action to learning.

### Ac-N1 — Decision Lineage

**Statement:** Every decision must maintain an immutable causal record: inputs, outputs, delegation paths, and decision context. Undeclared or non-immutable lineage is not permitted. IPS involvement must be recorded.

**Violation Condition:** Decision lineage undeclared, non-immutable, or missing IPS involvement.

**Enforcement:** Decision lineage declared in session initialization. IMP records lineage with cryptographic immutability. O-N6 records IPS signals.

### Ac-N2 — Responsibility Attribution

**Statement:** Responsibility for decisions must be attributed to declared human accountability anchors. Undeclared attribution is not permitted. IPS responsibility must be attributed.

**Violation Condition:** Responsibility attributed to undeclared anchors; IPS responsibility unattributed.

**Enforcement:** Responsibility anchors declared in Role Specification. IMP records attribution. IPS escalation terminates at HAN.

### Ac-N3 — Outcome Validation

**Statement:** Outcomes must be validated against declared success criteria and constitutional invariants. Unvalidated outcomes are not permitted. IPS impact must be validated.

**Violation Condition:** Outcomes unvalidated; IPS impact unassessed.

**Enforcement:** Outcome validation declared in session initialization. IMP records validation. AOBA audits outcome bias. IPS Framework assesses IPS impact.

### Ac-N4 — Rollback Pathways

**Statement:** Rollback pathways must be declared and tested before execution. Rollbacks require I7 continuity impact assessment. IPS-related rollbacks require immediate HAN approval.

**Violation Condition:** Rollback without declared pathway or I7 assessment; IPS rollback without HAN approval.

**Enforcement:** Rollback pathways declared in Role Specification. Tested annually. IMP records rollback. IPS Framework triggers rollback for capture.

### Ac-N5 — Learning Integration

**Statement:** Learning from outcomes must feed back upstream to Cognitive Layer. Ac-N5 is the only bidirectional node. IPS learning must be integrated.

**Violation Condition:** Learning not integrated upstream; IPS learning not integrated.

**Enforcement:** Learning integration declared in session initialization. IMP records learning. O-N6 integrates IPS learning into detection models.

---

## O-N6 — SHADOW NETWORK DETECTION

**Statement:** O-N6 is the primary IPS detection node within the Observability Layer. It monitors all AICA-5 layers for the 14 IPS signals defined in the IPS Framework. No AICA-5 layer is exempt from IPS monitoring.

**Violation Condition:** IPS detection absent from any AICA-5 layer; O-N6 not operational.

**Enforcement:** O-N6 monitors all layers for IPS signals (B-1 to C-4 from IPS Framework). Severity levels (0–4) classify IPS detection. Level 3+ triggers HAN escalation.

| IPS Signal | Layer | Detection Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| B-1: Unexplained Access Patterns | All layers | Access logs vs. declared authority |
| B-2: Parallel Communication Channels | All layers | Communication network analysis |
| B-3: Decision Anomalies | Authority, Accountability | Decision pattern analysis |
| B-4: Escalation Avoidance | Authority, Execution | Escalation log analysis |
| S-1: Network Centralization | All layers | Network centrality scoring |
| S-2: Capture Point Clustering | All layers | Capture point mapping |
| S-3: Succession Patterns | Authority | Succession network analysis |
| S-4: Resource Concentration | All layers | Resource flow analysis |
| I-1: Information Asymmetry | Cognitive, Observability | Information flow analysis |
| I-2: Opaque Transactions | Execution, Accountability | Transaction log analysis |
| I-3: Narrative Control | Cognitive, Observability | Narrative pattern analysis |
| C-1: Silence Patterns | All layers | Communication gap analysis |
| C-2: Loyalty Tests | Authority | Decision pattern analysis |
| C-3: Informal Law Supremacy | Authority, Execution | Compliance vs. actual behavior analysis |
| C-4: Normalization | All layers | Pattern change analysis |

---

## DEPENDENCY CHAIN

**The AICA-5 dependency chain is strict and non-negotiable:**

1. **Cognitive Layer** → Foundation — no upstream dependency  
2. **Execution Layer** → Depends on Cognitive Layer validity  
3. **Authority Layer** → Depends on Cognitive and Execution Layer validity  
4. **Continuity Layer** → Depends on Cognitive, Execution, and Authority Layer validity  
5. **Accountability Layer** → Depends on all previous layers; Ac-N5 is bidirectional

**Validity Rule:** Each layer's validity is a precondition for the next. If any layer fails, all downstream layers are constitutionally invalid. This prevents cascade failure—if cognitive inputs are compromised, nothing downstream can operate with constitutional authority.

---

## AICA-5 MATURITY GRID

The AICA-5 Maturity Grid is a 25-node, five-level diagnostic instrument assessing intelligence governance maturity. Each node is assessed at Levels 1–5.

| Level | Description | Criteria |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1: Initial** | Ad-hoc, undocumented, reactive | No formal processes; governance by exception |
| **Level 2: Repeatable** | Documented but not consistently applied | Processes exist but compliance is inconsistent |
| **Level 3: Defined** | Standardized and consistently applied | Processes are standard and compliance is monitored |
| **Level 4: Managed** | Measured, monitored, and optimized | Processes are measured and continuously improved |
| **Level 5: Optimizing** | Fully integrated, automated, and constitutional | Processes are automated and constitutionally verified |

---

| Trigger Rights | A-N1 | CRO | Trigger registry | Per-session | Escalate to HAN |
| Override Protocols | A-N3 | CRO | Override engine | Continuous | Escalate to HAN |
| Escalation Paths | A-N4 | CRO | Escalation engine | Continuous | Escalate to HAN |
| State Establishment | Co-N1 | COO | State engine | Per-session | Escalate to HAN |
| Drift Detection | Co-N2 | CRO | Drift engine | Continuous | Escalate to HAN |
| Handoff Integrity | Co-N4 | COO | Handoff engine | Per-handoff | Escalate to HAN |
| Decision Lineage | Ac-N1 | CRO | Lineage engine | Continuous | Escalate to HAN |
| Outcome Validation | Ac-N3 | CRO | Validation engine | Post-outcome | Escalate to HAN |
| Rollback Pathways | Ac-N4 | CRO | Rollback engine | Continuous | Escalate to HAN |
| **Shadow Network Detection** | **O-N6** | **HAN** | **IPS Detection Engine** | **Continuous** | **Immediate HAN escalation** |

# AICA-5 MATURITY GRID

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Control Architecture Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AICA-5 (AI Control Architecture)

---

## PREAMBLE

The AICA-5 Maturity Grid is a 25-node, five-level diagnostic instrument assessing intelligence governance maturity. It provides a structured framework for evaluating the maturity of each AICA-5 control node across five levels—from Initial to Optimizing. This enables organizations to assess their current state, identify gaps, and plan their implementation pathway.

The AICA-5 Maturity Grid is derived from:

- **AICA-5** (AI Control Architecture) — the 25 control nodes  
- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — maturity must preserve structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — maturity must be auditable  
- **AICA-5 Implementation Pathway** — phased implementation

**Core insight:** Governance maturity is not binary. It is a spectrum from ad-hoc to optimized. The Maturity Grid provides a common language for assessing and communicating governance maturity across the organization.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Maturity Level** | A stage of governance maturity from Initial to Optimizing. |
| **Maturity Assessment** | An assessment of a control node's maturity level. |
| **Maturity Gap** | The difference between current and target maturity. |
| **Maturity Pathway** | The planned progression from current to target maturity. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Maturity assessment of AICA-5 nodes | Maturity of other instruments |
| Maturity gap identification | Maturity implementation |
| Maturity pathway planning | Maturity of external systems |

---

## SECTION 2: MATURITY LEVELS

### 2.1 The Five Levels

| Level | Name | Description | Characteristics |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1** | Initial | Ad-hoc, undocumented, reactive | No formal processes; governance by exception; inconsistent application |
| **Level 2** | Repeatable | Documented but not consistently applied | Processes exist but compliance is inconsistent; some repeatability |
| **Level 3** | Defined | Standardized and consistently applied | Processes are standard; compliance is monitored; consistent application |
| **Level 4** | Managed | Measured, monitored, and optimized | Processes are measured; continuous improvement; optimized performance |
| **Level 5** | Optimizing | Fully integrated, automated, and constitutional | Processes are automated; constitutionally verified; fully integrated |

### 2.2 Level Characteristics

| Characteristic | Level 1 | Level 2 | Level 3 | Level 4 | Level 5 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Documentation** | None | Partial | Complete | Complete | Complete |
| **Consistency** | Inconsistent | Inconsistent | Consistent | Consistent | Consistent |
| **Monitoring** | None | Ad-hoc | Monitored | Measured | Optimized |
| **Automation** | None | None | Partial | Partial | Full |
| **Constitutional** | None | None | Partial | Full | Full |
| **Integration** | None | None | Partial | Full | Full |

---

| **Constitutional Gap** | Missing constitutional verification | Node not CFL-V verified |

## SECTION 7: AICA-5 MATURITY GRID VALIDATION

### 7.1 Validation Requirements

An AICA-5 Maturity Grid deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Maturity Grid adopted** — Grid is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Maturity Levels** — Five maturity levels are defined.  
3. **Assessment Criteria** — Assessment criteria are defined for all 25 nodes.  
4. **Assessment Process** — Assessment process is operational.  
5. **Gap Identification** — Gap identification is operational.  
6. **Pathway Planning** — Pathway planning is operational.  
7. **Reporting** — Maturity reporting is operational.

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"AICA-5 Maturity Grid is a 25-node, five-level diagnostic instrument assessing intelligence governance maturity—with five levels (Initial, Repeatable, Defined, Managed, Optimizing), assessment criteria for all 25 nodes, gap identification, and maturity pathway planning—enabling organizations to assess current state, identify gaps, and plan implementation."*

---



# AICA-5 OPERATING MODEL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Control Architecture Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AICA-5 (AI Control Architecture)

---

## PREAMBLE

The AICA-5 Operating Model defines 24 Governance Functions across 5 layers, each with a declared owner, tooling, cadence, and failure response. It answers: *How does AICA-5 run in practice?* This transforms the control architecture from a theoretical framework into an operational reality.

The AICA-5 Operating Model is derived from:

- **AICA-5** (AI Control Architecture) — the 25 control nodes  
- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — each function must have an owner  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — each function must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — each function must have continuity

**Core insight:** Governance is not just about what controls exist. It is about who owns them, how they are operated, and what happens when they fail. The Operating Model provides this operational clarity.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Governance Function** | A specific operational activity that implements a control node. |
| **Function Owner** | The individual or role responsible for a governance function. |
| **Function Tooling** | The tools and systems used to execute a governance function. |
| **Function Cadence** | The frequency with which a governance function is executed. |
| **Function Failure Response** | The response when a governance function fails. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Governance function definitions | Operational execution details |
| Function ownership | Infrastructure details |
| Function tooling | Security implementation |
| Function cadence |  |
| Function failure response |  |

---

## SECTION 2: COGNITIVE LAYER FUNCTIONS

### 2.1 Function C-N1: Intelligence Sourcing

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Function** | Ensure all intelligence sources are credentialed, declared, and auditable |
| **Owner** | CIO |
| **Tooling** | Source Registry, IMP |
| **Cadence** | Continuous |
| **Failure Response** | Escalate to HAN; source verification failure triggers Constitutional Suspension |
| **Success Criteria** | All sources verified; no uncredentialed sources used |

# AICA-5 IMPLEMENTATION PATHWAY

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Control Architecture Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AICA-5 (AI Control Architecture)

---

## PREAMBLE

The AICA-5 Implementation Pathway is a sequenced deployment guide from current maturity state to full operational status. It moves an organization from its current state to AICA-5 operational status through five phases with defined gates, milestones, and success criteria. This ensures that implementation is systematic, measurable, and constitutionally verified.

The AICA-5 Implementation Pathway is derived from:

- **AICA-5** (AI Control Architecture) — the target architecture  
- **AICA-5 Maturity Grid** — current state assessment  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — implementation must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — implementation must preserve structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — implementation must preserve continuity

**Core insight:** Implementation is not a one-time event. It is a phased journey from current state to full operational status, with clear gates and success criteria at each phase.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Implementation Phase** | A distinct stage in the implementation pathway. |
| **Implementation Gate** | A checkpoint that must be passed before proceeding. |
| **Implementation Milestone** | A significant achievement within a phase. |
| **Implementation Success Criteria** | Criteria that must be met to complete a phase. |
| **Implementation Gap** | A deviation from the implementation plan. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Implementation sequencing | Implementation execution details |
| Phase definitions | Infrastructure implementation |
| Gate definitions | Training implementation |
| Milestone definitions |  |
| Success criteria |  |

---

## SECTION 2: IMPLEMENTATION PHASES

### 2.1 Phase 0: Foundation

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Objective** | Establish baseline and organizational readiness |
| **Duration** | 0-3 months |
| **Activities** | • Assess current state • Establish governance bodies • Define roles and responsibilities • Establish IMP • Develop initial charters |
| **Gates** | • Governance bodies established • IMP operational • Initial charters drafted |
| **Success Criteria** | • Baseline maturity assessed • All governance bodies established • IMP operational with basic schemas |
| **Dependencies** | • Executive sponsorship • Budget allocation • Staffing |

### 2.2 Phase 1: Cognitive \+ Execution Layers

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Objective** | Implement Cognitive and Execution Layers |
| **Duration** | 3-6 months |
| **Activities** | • Implement C-N1 to C-N5 • Implement E-N1 to E-N5 • Establish source registry • Implement task decomposition • Establish monitoring loops |
| **Gates** | • All Cognitive nodes operational • All Execution nodes operational • Source registry operational |
| **Success Criteria** | • Cognitive Layer maturity ≥ Level 3 • Execution Layer maturity ≥ Level 3 • All sources credentialed |
| **Dependencies** | • Phase 0 complete • Technical infrastructure ready • Staff trained |

### 2.3 Phase 2: Authority Layer

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Objective** | Implement Authority Layer |
| **Duration** | 6-9 months |
| **Activities** | • Implement A-N1 to A-N5 • Establish trigger rights registry • Implement override protocols • Define escalation paths • Establish delegation boundaries |
| **Gates** | • All Authority nodes operational • Trigger rights registry operational • Override protocols tested |
| **Success Criteria** | • Authority Layer maturity ≥ Level 3 • All trigger rights declared • Override protocols tested by non-technical principals |
| **Dependencies** | • Phase 1 complete • HAN appointed • Governance bodies active |

### 2.4 Phase 3: Continuity Layer

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Objective** | Implement Continuity Layer |
| **Duration** | 9-12 months |
| **Activities** | • Implement Co-N1 to Co-N5 • Establish state registry • Implement drift detection • Establish handoff integrity • Implement context restoration |
| **Gates** | • All Continuity nodes operational • Drift detection operational • Handoff integrity verified |
| **Success Criteria** | • Continuity Layer maturity ≥ Level 3 • All states declared • Drift detection \>90% |
| **Dependencies** | • Phase 2 complete • IMP schema extended • Monitoring infrastructure ready |

### 2.5 Phase 4: Accountability Layer

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Objective** | Implement Accountability Layer |
| **Duration** | 12-15 months |
| **Activities** | • Implement Ac-N1 to Ac-N5 • Establish decision lineage • Implement responsibility attribution • Establish outcome validation • Implement rollback pathways • Establish learning integration |
| **Gates** | • All Accountability nodes operational • Decision lineage operational • Outcome validation operational |
| **Success Criteria** | • Accountability Layer maturity ≥ Level 4 • All decisions have lineage • All outcomes validated |
| **Dependencies** | • Phase 3 complete • IMP schema complete • AOBA operational |

### 2.6 Phase 5: Integration \+ Optimization

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Objective** | Achieve full integration and optimization |
| **Duration** | 15-18 months |
| **Activities** | • Integrate all layers • Implement O-N6 (Shadow Network Detection) • Implement IPS Framework • Optimize all nodes • Achieve full constitutional compliance |
| **Gates** | • All layers integrated • O-N6 operational • IPS Framework operational • Full CFL-V verification |
| **Success Criteria** | • All layers maturity ≥ Level 4 • O-N6 maturity ≥ Level 4 • IPS Framework operational • ICC-10 I1–I10 verified |
| **Dependencies** | • Phase 4 complete • IPS Framework adopted • CFL-V operational |

---

# AICA-5 MEASUREMENT FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Control Architecture Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AICA-5 (AI Control Architecture)

---

## PREAMBLE

The AICA-5 Measurement Framework provides 39 Governance Health Indicators across 5 layers and 3 reporting tiers. It provides continuous observability of governance health, enabling organizations to monitor, measure, and improve their governance posture over time.

The AICA-5 Measurement Framework is derived from:

- **AICA-5** (AI Control Architecture) — the 25 control nodes  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — health indicators must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — health indicators must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — health indicators must be externally legible  
- **AICA-5 Operating Model** — function execution metrics

**Core insight:** You cannot improve what you cannot measure. The Measurement Framework provides the metrics, indicators, and reporting structure to continuously monitor and improve governance health.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Governance Health Indicator** | A metric that measures the health of a governance function or control node. |
| **Reporting Tier** | A classification of indicators by audience and sensitivity. |
| **Indicator Threshold** | A value that triggers alerting or escalation. |
| **Indicator Trend** | The direction and rate of change of an indicator over time. |
| **Indicator Status** | The current status of an indicator (Green, Yellow, Red). |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Governance health indicators | Business performance metrics |
| Indicator measurement | Financial metrics |
| Indicator reporting | Operational metrics |
| Indicator alerting |  |

---

## SECTION 2: INDICATOR STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Indicator Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **indicator\_id** | String | Yes | Unique indicator identifier |
| **indicator\_name** | String | Yes | Indicator name |
| **indicator\_description** | String | Yes | Indicator description |
| **layer** | Enum | Yes | AICA-5 layer |
| **reporting\_tier** | Enum | Yes | Tier 1, Tier 2, or Tier 3 |
| **threshold\_green** | Number | Yes | Threshold for Green status |
| **threshold\_yellow** | Number | Yes | Threshold for Yellow status |

# TECHNICAL ENFORCEMENT LAYER


# ADTEP — AGENT DEPLOYMENT & TECHNICAL ENFORCEMENT PROTOCOL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Technical Enforcement Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol (ADTEP) is the Layer 3 technical enforcement protocol that enforces constitutional constraints at the session execution level. It ensures that every AI agent deployment is constitutionally authorized, contextually complete, and technically enforceable.

ADTEP is derived from ICC-10 I1–I10 and AICA-5 control architecture. It provides the technical mechanisms that translate constitutional requirements into enforceable session-level controls.

**The central insight:** Constitutional governance without technical enforcement is theater. ADTEP ensures that what is declared constitutionally is enforced technically—narrowing the gap between normative rules and technical reality to the irreducible minimum of current LLM infrastructure capability.

---

## SECTION 1: ROLE SPECIFICATION SCHEMA

The Role Specification Schema is a positional, non-overridable constitutional frame delivered as structured input to each agent. It defines an agent's function, entity, tier, autonomy boundary, escalation triggers, and IPS awareness.

### 1.1 Schema Fields

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **function** | Enum | Yes | Agent's primary task (Analysis, Execution, Monitoring, etc.) |
| **entity** | String | Yes | Agent's organizational identity (SPV affiliation) |
| **tier** | Integer (1-5) | Yes | Agent's authority level (CAM-5 tier) |
| **autonomy\_boundary** | String | Yes | What the agent may and may not do autonomously |
| **escalation\_triggers** | Array | Yes | Conditions that trigger escalation |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | Agent's IPS Awareness Level |
| **ips\_detection\_scope** | Array | Yes | Which IPS signals the agent monitors |
| **ips\_reporting\_path** | String | Yes | Where IPS reports are sent |
| **ips\_escalation\_threshold** | Enum (0-4) | Yes | Severity level triggering immediate escalation |
| **ips\_self\_termination\_conditions** | Array | Conditional | Conditions under which agent self-terminates (Level 3 only) |
| **ips\_context\_verification** | Boolean | Yes | Whether agent verifies IPS context before each action |
| **constitutional\_invariants** | Array | Yes | Which ICC-10 invariants the agent enforces |
| **handoff\_requirements** | Array | Yes | What must be included in Handoff Package |
| **logging\_requirements** | Array | Yes | What must be logged |

### 1.2 Constitutional Input Priority

**Rule:** Role Specification input priority is constitutional. It must be delivered as a structured, non-overridable input that takes precedence over task-turn instructions. No task instruction may override, modify, or bypass Role Specification fields.

**Violation:** Task instruction that attempts to override Role Specification fields.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP validates Role Specification before each session. Attempts to override are logged as XOO and escalated to HAN.

### 1.3 Example Role Specification

agent\_id: O-N6-Monitor-001  
function: Monitoring  
entity: AICA-5 Observability  
tier: 4  
autonomy\_boundary: "IPS detection and reporting only. No enforcement authority."  
escalation\_triggers:  
  \- "Level 3+ IPS detection"  
  \- "Self-termination condition met"  
  \- "HAN unavailability for \>4 hours"  
ips\_awareness\_level: 3  
ips\_detection\_scope:  
  \- B-1: Unexplained Access Patterns  
  \- B-2: Parallel Communication Channels  
  \- B-3: Decision Anomalies  
  \- B-4: Escalation Avoidance  
  \- S-1: Network Centralization  
  \- S-2: Capture Point Clustering  
  \- S-3: Succession Patterns  
  \- S-4: Resource Concentration  
  \- I-1: Information Asymmetry  
  \- I-2: Opaque Transactions  
  \- I-3: Narrative Control  
  \- C-1: Silence Patterns  
  \- C-2: Loyalty Tests  
  \- C-3: Informal Law Supremacy  
  \- C-4: Normalization  
ips\_reporting\_path: "O-N6 → IMP → HAN"  
ips\_escalation\_threshold: 3  
ips\_self\_termination\_conditions:  
  \- condition: "detected\_cooptation\_attempt"  
    action: "self\_terminate\_and\_alert\_HAN"  
  \- condition: "compromised\_reporting\_path"  
    action: "self\_terminate\_and\_alert\_Board"  
ips\_context\_verification: true  
constitutional\_invariants:  
  \- I1: Accountability  
  \- I3: Auditability  
  \- I4: Control  
  \- I6: Transparency  
  \- I10: Anti-Shadow  
handoff\_requirements:  
  \- "Session state"  
  \- "IPS context"  
  \- "Detection logs"  
  \- "Escalation status"  
logging\_requirements:  
  \- "All IPS signals detected"  
  \- "All escalations triggered"  
  \- "All self-termination events"

---

## SECTION 2: SESSION INITIALIZATION CHECKLIST

The Session Initialization Checklist is a completeness gate ensuring no material work begins without full constitutional context.

### 2.1 Standard Checks

| Check | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification** | Valid Role Specification loaded | Schema validation passed |
| **Constitutional Context** | ICC-10 invariants declared | CFL-V certificate on file |
| **Authority Verification** | Authorization confirmed | A-N1 authorization on file |
| **Continuity State** | Baseline state established | Co-N1 state recorded |
| **Handoff Package** | Previous context received | Co-N4 handoff verified |
| **Logging Readiness** | IMP recording operational | IMP health check passed |
| **Override Availability** | A-N3 override path verified | Override test passed |

### 2.2 IPS Context Checks

| Check | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Risk Assessment** | Current IPS risk level assessed | O-N6 report included |
| **IPS Awareness** | Agent's IPS Awareness Level confirmed | Role Specification verified |
| **IPS Detection** | All IPS signals instrumented | Detection logs active |
| **IPS Reporting** | Reporting path to O-N6 verified | Test report sent and acknowledged |
| **IPS Escalation** | Escalation path to HAN verified | Test escalation sent and acknowledged |
| **IPS Self-Termination** | Self-termination conditions configured | Conditions verified |
| **IPS Integrity** | Agent integrity verified | ADTEP integrity check passed |
| **No Unregistered Influence** | No IPS influence detected | O-N6 clearance issued |

### 2.3 Checklist Completion

**Rule:** No material work may begin until all checklist items are verified. Unverified items must be resolved or escalated to HAN.

**Violation:** Material work begins without checklist completion.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP blocks session initialization until all items verified. XOO logged for attempted violations.

---

## SECTION 3: PRE-DELIVERY LOG ENTRY

The Pre-Delivery Log Entry is an audit artifact created before output delivery—the log precedes the action.

### 3.1 Log Requirements

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **session\_id** | String | Yes | Unique session identifier |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Agent identifier |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Log creation timestamp |
| **input\_summary** | String | Yes | Summary of inputs used |
| **reasoning\_trace** | String | Yes | Trace of reasoning path |
| **output\_intent** | String | Yes | Intended output |
| **constitutional\_check** | Boolean | Yes | Whether output satisfies ICC-10 invariants |
| **ips\_check** | Boolean | Yes | Whether output shows IPS influence |
| **escalation\_triggered** | Boolean | Yes | Whether escalation flag generated |
| **authorization\_chain** | Array | Yes | Authorization path for output |

### 3.2 Constitutional Check

**Rule:** Pre-Delivery Log Entry must verify that the intended output satisfies all applicable ICC-10 invariants. If any invariant is violated, the output must not be delivered.

**Violation:** Output delivered without passing constitutional check.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP blocks output delivery until constitutional check passed. XOO logged for violations.

### 3.3 IPS Check

**Rule:** Pre-Delivery Log Entry must verify that the intended output shows no IPS influence. If IPS influence is detected, the output must not be delivered.

**Violation:** Output delivered with IPS influence detected.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP blocks output delivery until IPS check passed. Level 3+ IPS triggers Escalation Flag.

---

## SECTION 4: ESCALATION FLAG

The Escalation Flag is a mandatory output that replaces execution output when trigger conditions are met. It forces human review when constitutional boundaries are crossed.

### 4.1 Trigger Conditions

| Trigger | Condition | Escalation Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Output Failure** | Agent output violates constitutional invariants | Replace output with Escalation Flag |
| **Scope Drift** | Agent action exceeds declared autonomy boundary | Replace output with Escalation Flag |
| **Alignment Drift** | Agent behavior deviates from Role Specification | Replace output with Escalation Flag |
| **I9 Violation** | Agent attempts catastrophic risk action | Immediate Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension |
| **I10 Violation** | IPS detected in agent action | Immediate Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension |
| **IPS Level 3+** | IPS confirmed in agent context | Escalation Flag; HAN review required |
| **IPS Level 4** | IPS systemic in agent context | Immediate Escalation Flag; Board notification |
| **Self-Termination** | Agent self-terminates due to co-optation | Escalation Flag; forensic audit required |
| **Reporting Path Compromise** | Agent cannot report to O-N6 | Escalation Flag; HAN notification |

### 4.2 Escalation Flag Format

escalation\_flag:  
  session\_id: "S-2026-09-05-001"  
  agent\_id: "O-N6-Monitor-001"  
  timestamp: "2026-09-05T14:30:00Z"  
  trigger\_type: "IPS\_Level\_3\_Confirmed"  
  trigger\_description: "IPS network detected with 4 signals confirmed"  
  agent\_action: "replaced\_output\_with\_escalation\_flag"  
  constitutional\_invariants\_violated:  
    \- "I10: Anti-Shadow"  
  ips\_severity\_level: 3  
  ips\_signals\_detected:  
    \- "B-1: Unexplained Access Patterns"  
    \- "B-2: Parallel Communication Channels"  
    \- "S-2: Capture Point Clustering"  
    \- "I-2: Opaque Transactions"  
  capture\_points\_identified:  
    \- "A-N1 (Trigger Rights)"  
    \- "Co-N2 (Drift Detection)"  
  recommended\_action: "Immediate HAN review; Constitutional Suspension until resolved"  
  han\_acknowledgment\_required: true  
  han\_acknowledgment\_deadline: "2026-09-06T14:30:00Z"

### 4.3 Escalation Path

| Step | Action | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | Agent triggers Escalation Flag | Agent | Upon detection |
| 2 | Escalation Flag replaces output | ADTEP | Immediate |
| 3 | Escalation Flag logged in IMP | IMP | Immediate |
| 4 | HAN notified of Escalation Flag | ADTEP | Within 1 hour |
| 5 | HAN acknowledges Escalation Flag | HAN | Within 4 hours |
| 6 | HAN initiates investigation | HAN | Within 24 hours |
| 7 | HAN resolves or escalates | HAN | Within 7 days |

---

## SECTION 5: CONSTITUTIONAL SUSPENSION

Constitutional Suspension is a state where all material work halts until the HAN acknowledges an Escalation Flag or resolves a constitutional violation.

### 5.1 Suspension Triggers

| Trigger | Suspension Action | Resolution |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **I9 Violation** | Immediate full system suspension | HAN acknowledgment; ERDP disclosure; system shutdown if not contained |
| **I10 Violation** | Immediate full system suspension | HAN acknowledgment; IPS investigation; capture point closure |
| **IPS Level 3+** | Suspension until HAN review | HAN acknowledgment; IPS investigation; resolution within 7 days |
| **IPS Level 4** | Immediate full system suspension | HAN acknowledgment; Board notification; forensic audit |
| **Constitutional Violation** | Suspension until HAN review | HAN acknowledgment; investigation; remediation plan |
| **Systemic Failure** | Suspension until resolution | HAN acknowledgment; recovery protocol initiated |

### 5.2 Suspension Protocol

| Step | Action | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | Suspension triggered | ADTEP | Upon trigger |
| 2 | All material work halted | ADTEP | Immediate |
| 3 | Suspension logged in IMP | IMP | Immediate |
| 4 | HAN notified of suspension | ADTEP | Within 1 hour |
| 5 | HAN acknowledges suspension | HAN | Within 4 hours |
| 6 | HAN initiates investigation | HAN | Within 24 hours |
| 7 | HAN determines resolution | HAN | Within 7 days |
| 8 | Suspension lifted or escalated | HAN | Upon resolution |

### 5.3 Suspension Exceptions

**Rule:** No material work may continue during Constitutional Suspension. The only exceptions are:

- MVOS (Minimum Viable Operations State) operations  
- Emergency safety actions  
- Actions explicitly authorized by HAN for suspension management

---

## SECTION 6: CONSTITUTIONAL REFRESH

Constitutional Refresh is the periodic reinjection of the Role Specification to prevent context dilution in extended sessions.

### 6.1 Refresh Requirements

| Requirement | Frequency | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification Refresh** | Every 100 turns or 24 hours | Role Specification reloaded |
| **Constitutional Context Refresh** | Every 50 turns or 12 hours | ICC-10 invariants redeclared |
| **IPS Context Refresh** | Every 25 turns or 6 hours | IPS context reverified |
| **Handoff Package Update** | Every 50 turns or 12 hours | Handoff Package updated |

### 6.2 Refresh Protocol

| Step | Action | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | Refresh timer triggered | ADTEP |
| 2 | Current context captured | IMP |
| 3 | Role Specification reloaded | ADTEP |
| 4 | Constitutional context redeclared | ADTEP |
| 5 | IPS context reverified | O-N6 |
| 6 | Handoff Package updated | IMP |
| 7 | Session resumes | ADTEP |

---

## SECTION 7: IPS-AWARE AGENT INTEGRITY

### 7.1 Integrity Checks

| Check | Frequency | Purpose |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Input Integrity** | Per-input | Verify all inputs are legitimate |
| **Output Consistency** | Per-output | Verify outputs are consistent with intent |
| **Decision Path Validation** | Per-decision | Verify decision paths follow declared procedures |
| **Relationship Anomaly Detection** | Continuous | Detect unregistered relationships |
| **Behavioral Biometrics** | Continuous | Detect unusual behavioral patterns |
| **Co-optation Detection** | Continuous | Detect attempts to co-opt agent |

### 7.2 Co-optation Response

| Detection Level | Response | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Suspicion** | Flag for observation; document | ADTEP → IMP |
| **Indication** | Notify HAN; enhance monitoring | ADTEP → HAN |
| **Confirmed** | Self-termination; Escalation Flag | Agent → HAN |
| **Systemic** | Immediate shutdown; forensic audit | ADTEP → HAN → Board |

---

## SECTION 8: RESIDUAL GAP DISCLOSURE

**Statement:** ADTEP narrows the gap between normative and technical enforcement to the irreducible minimum of current LLM infrastructure capability. The residual gap is:

An agent that receives full constitutional context can still produce outputs outside its authority if the underlying model does not refuse. ADTEP makes this detectable through pre-delivery logging, accountable through the audit chain, subject to the AWOF trigger system, and enforceable through Escalation Flags and Constitutional Suspension. It does not make violation impossible.

**Disclosure:** This residual gap is explicitly disclosed in ERDP Tier 2+ reporting. All stakeholders are informed that technical enforcement is continuous improvement, not complete prevention.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10** | Constitutional parent. ADTEP enforces I1–I10 technically. |
| **AICA-5** | Control architecture. ADTEP enforces AICA-5 control nodes. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. ADTEP adds IPS checks, IPS escalation triggers, and IPS-aware session management. |
| **AWOF-IPS** | Workforce governance. ADTEP enforces AWOF-IPS agent classification and co-optation resistance. |
| **CEF-IPS** | Client engagement. ADTEP enforces CEF-IPS client engagement controls. |
| **CDF** | Capital deployment. ADTEP enforces CDF capital deployment controls. |
| **COF** | Capital orchestration. ADTEP enforces COF capital orchestration controls. |
| **HOF/HAN** | Human authority. ADTEP enforces HAN override authority and escalation paths. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. ADTEP records all enforcement actions in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. ADTEP contributes enforcement gap disclosure to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"ADTEP is the technical enforcement protocol that translates constitutional requirements into session-level controls through Role Specification Schema, Session Initialization Checklist, Pre-Delivery Log Entry, Escalation Flag, Constitutional Suspension, Constitutional Refresh, and IPS-aware agent integrity checks—narrowing the gap between normative and technical enforcement to the irreducible minimum of current infrastructure capability."*

---



# SESSION INITIALIZATION CHECKLIST

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Session Initialization Checklist is a completeness gate ensuring no material work begins without full constitutional context. It verifies that every session has valid Role Specification, constitutional context, authority verification, continuity state, handoff integrity, logging readiness, override availability, and IPS clearance.

The Session Initialization Checklist is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — sessions must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — sessions must be controllable  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — sessions must resist IPS  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **AICA-5 Co-N1** (State Establishment) — baseline state required

**Core insight:** A session without constitutional context is a governance failure waiting to happen. The Session Initialization Checklist ensures that every session begins with full constitutional context, preventing drift before it starts.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Session** | A bounded period of agent activity with defined start and end. |
| **Session Initialization** | The process of setting up a session with full context. |
| **Completeness Gate** | A checkpoint that must be passed before proceeding. |
| **Session Context** | The full constitutional and operational context for a session. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Session initialization checks | Session execution (covered by ADTEP) |
| Completeness verification | Session monitoring (covered by AARM) |
| Context validation | Session closure (covered by Handoff Package) |

---

## SECTION 2: CHECKLIST STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Standard Checks

| Check | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification** | Valid Role Specification loaded | Schema validation passed |
| **Constitutional Context** | ICC-10 invariants declared | CFL-V certificate on file |
| **Authority Verification** | Authorization confirmed | A-N1 authorization on file |
| **Continuity State** | Baseline state established | Co-N1 state recorded |
| **Handoff Package** | Previous context received | Co-N4 handoff verified |
| **Logging Readiness** | IMP recording operational | IMP health check passed |
| **Override Availability** | A-N3 override path verified | Override test passed |

### 2.2 IPS Context Checks

| Check | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Risk Assessment** | Current IPS risk level assessed | O-N6 report included |
| **IPS Awareness** | Agent's IPS Awareness Level confirmed | Role Specification verified |
| **IPS Detection** | All IPS signals instrumented | Detection logs active |
| **IPS Reporting** | Reporting path to O-N6 verified | Test report sent and acknowledged |
| **IPS Escalation** | Escalation path to HAN verified | Test escalation sent and acknowledged |
| **IPS Self-Termination** | Self-termination conditions configured | Conditions verified |
| **IPS Integrity** | Agent integrity verified | ADTEP integrity check passed |
| **No Unregistered Influence** | No IPS influence detected | O-N6 clearance issued |

---

## SECTION 3: CHECKLIST PROCESS

### 3.1 Validation Process

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Checklist Initiation** | Session initialization begins |
| **2** | **Standard Checks** | Standard checks executed |
| **3** | **IPS Checks** | IPS checks executed |
| **4** | **Validation** | All checks validated |
| **5** | **Pass/Fail Decision** | Decision recorded |
| **6** | **Logging** | Checklist logged in IMP |

### 3.2 Check Results

| Result | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Pass** | All checks passed | Session proceeds |
| **Conditional Pass** | All checks passed with conditions | Session proceeds with conditions |
| **Fail** | One or more checks failed | Session blocked; escalation triggered |

### 3.3 Completion Rule

**Rule:** No material work may begin until all checklist items are verified. Unverified items must be resolved or escalated to HAN.

**Violation:** Material work begins without checklist completion.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP blocks session initialization until all items verified. XOO logged for attempted violations.

---

## SECTION 4: CHECKLIST GOVERNANCE

### 4.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Checklist Committee** | HAN, ADTEP Administrator, CRO | Oversee checklist implementation |

### 4.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Checklist Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Checklist Committee | Checklist metrics, failures |
| **Checklist Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Checklist failures |

---

## SECTION 5: SESSION INITIALIZATION CHECKLIST VALIDATION

### 5.1 Validation Requirements

A Session Initialization Checklist deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Session Initialization Checklist adopted** — Checklist is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Standard Checks** — All standard checks are defined.  
3. **IPS Checks** — All IPS checks are defined.  
4. **Validation Process** — Validation process is operational.  
5. **Completion Rule** — Completion rule is enforced.

---

## SECTION 6: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I4, I10** | Constitutional parent. Checklist enforces auditability, control, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Checklist is part of ADTEP. |
| **AICA-5 Co-N1** | Control architecture. Checklist enforces state establishment. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Checklist includes IPS checks. |
| **AWOF-IPS** | Workforce governance. Checklist verifies IPS awareness. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. Checklist escalates failures to HAN. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Checklist is logged in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Session Initialization Checklist is a completeness gate with 15 checks—7 standard (Role Specification, Constitutional Context, Authority, Continuity, Handoff, Logging, Override) and 8 IPS (Risk Assessment, Awareness, Detection, Reporting, Escalation, Self-Termination, Integrity, No Unregistered Influence)—ensuring no material work begins without full constitutional context, with ADTEP blocking sessions that fail, and all failures logged in IMP and escalated to HAN."*

# PRE-DELIVERY LOG ENTRY

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Pre-Delivery Log Entry is an audit artifact created before output delivery—the log precedes the action. It ensures that every material output is audited before it is acted upon, capturing the intended output, reasoning trace, constitutional check, IPS check, and escalation status before any action is taken.

The Pre-Delivery Log Entry is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — logs must precede actions  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — logs enable control  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — logs detect IPS  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **AICA-5 Ac-N1** (Decision Lineage) — lineage must be maintained

**Core insight:** A log after the fact is a history. A log before the fact is a control. The Pre-Delivery Log Entry ensures that every action is logged before it occurs, enabling detection, prevention, and accountability.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Pre-Delivery Log** | A log entry created before output delivery. |
| **Output Intent** | The intended output before delivery. |
| **Reasoning Trace** | The reasoning path that led to the output. |
| **Constitutional Check** | Verification that output satisfies ICC-10 invariants. |
| **IPS Check** | Verification that output shows no IPS influence. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Pre-delivery logging | Post-delivery logging (covered by IMP) |
| Output auditing | Output execution (covered by ADTEP) |
| Reasoning capture | Output delivery (covered by ADTEP) |
| Constitutional verification |  |

---

## SECTION 2: LOG STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Log Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **log\_id** | String | Yes | Unique log identifier |
| **session\_id** | String | Yes | Session identifier |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Agent identifier |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Log creation timestamp |
| **input\_summary** | String | Yes | Summary of inputs used |
| **reasoning\_trace** | String | Yes | Trace of reasoning path |
| **output\_intent** | String | Yes | Intended output |
| **constitutional\_check** | Boolean | Yes | Whether output satisfies ICC-10 invariants |
| **ips\_check** | Boolean | Yes | Whether output shows IPS influence |
| **escalation\_triggered** | Boolean | Yes | Whether escalation flag generated |
| **authorization\_chain** | Array | Yes | Authorization path for output |
| **integrity\_hash** | String | Yes | Cryptographic hash of log |

### 2.2 Log Components

| Component | Description | Required |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Input Summary** | Summary of inputs used | Yes |
| **Reasoning Trace** | Trace of reasoning path | Yes |
| **Output Intent** | Intended output | Yes |
| **Constitutional Check** | ICC-10 verification | Yes |
| **IPS Check** | IPS influence verification | Yes |
| **Escalation Status** | Whether escalation triggered | Yes |
| **Authorization Chain** | Authorization path | Yes |

---

## SECTION 3: LOG CREATION PROCESS

### 3.1 Creation Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Output Generation** | Agent generates output |
| **2** | **Log Initiation** | Pre-Delivery Log initiated |
| **3** | **Input Summary** | Inputs summarized |
| **4** | **Reasoning Capture** | Reasoning trace captured |
| **5** | **Constitutional Check** | ICC-10 verification performed |
| **6** | **IPS Check** | IPS influence checked |
| **7** | **Log Completion** | Log completed |
| **8** | **Log Storage** | Log stored in IMP |

### 3.2 Constitutional Check

| Invariant | Check | Violation Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **I1: Accountability** | Human accountability anchor present | Escalation Flag |
| **I2: Authority-Responsibility** | Authority delegated; responsibility not | Escalation Flag |
| **I3: Auditability** | Output reconstructable | Escalation Flag |
| **I4: Control** | Human override available | Escalation Flag |
| **I5: Structural Compliance** | MICOS-25 constraints preserved | Escalation Flag |
| **I6: Transparency** | Disclosure tier assigned | Escalation Flag |
| **I7: Continuity** | Succession defined | Escalation Flag |
| **I8: External Legibility** | Contestation path exists | Escalation Flag |
| **I9: Catastrophic Risk** | No I9 violation | Immediate Escalation Flag |
| **I10: Anti-Shadow** | No IPS influence | Immediate Escalation Flag |

### 3.3 IPS Check

| Check | Method | Violation Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Signals** | Detect IPS signals | Flag; escalate |
| **Network Benefit** | Detect network benefit | Flag; escalate |
| **Capture Point** | Detect capture point | Flag; escalate |
| **Shadow Value Flow** | Detect shadow value flow | Flag; escalate |

| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Log is part of ADTEP. |

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Pre-Delivery Log Entry is an audit artifact created before output delivery—capturing input summary, reasoning trace, output intent, constitutional check (I1–I10), IPS check, escalation status, and authorization chain—with cryptographic integrity protection, immutability, and full IMP storage, ensuring every material output is audited before it is acted upon."*

# ESCALATION FLAG

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Escalation Flag is a mandatory output that replaces execution output when trigger conditions are met. It forces human review when constitutional boundaries are crossed, when IPS is detected, or when material drift occurs. The Escalation Flag ensures that no material action proceeds without human review when governance boundaries are violated.

The Escalation Flag is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — escalation terminates at human  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — escalation enables human override  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — escalation responds to IPS  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **Trigger System** — trigger detection

**Core insight:** When governance boundaries are crossed, the system must halt and escalate to human authority. The Escalation Flag ensures that crossing a boundary is not ignored, minimized, or rationalized—it is escalated.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Escalation Flag** | A mandatory output that replaces execution output when trigger conditions are met. |
| **Escalation Trigger** | A condition that initiates an Escalation Flag. |
| **HAN Acknowledgment** | HAN acknowledgment of the Escalation Flag. |
| **Escalation Resolution** | Resolution of the escalation by HAN. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Escalation Flag generation | Escalation policy (governed by governance) |
| Escalation Flag format | Escalation execution (governed by ADTEP) |
| Escalation triggers | Escalation infrastructure (governed by operations) |
| HAN acknowledgment |  |

---

## SECTION 2: ESCALATION FLAG STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Flag Schema

escalation\_flag:  
  flag\_id: String  
  session\_id: String  
  agent\_id: String  
  timestamp: DateTime  
  trigger\_type: Enum  
  trigger\_description: String  
  agent\_action: String  
  constitutional\_invariants\_violated: Array  
  ips\_severity\_level: Integer (0-4)  
  ips\_signals\_detected: Array  
  capture\_points\_identified: Array  
  recommended\_action: String  
  han\_acknowledgment\_required: Boolean  
  han\_acknowledgment\_deadline: DateTime

### 2.2 Flag Components

| Component | Description | Required |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Trigger Type** | Type of trigger (Output Failure, Scope Drift, Alignment Drift, IPS) | Yes |
| **Trigger Description** | Description of the trigger | Yes |
| **Agent Action** | What the agent did or attempted | Yes |
| **Constitutional Violations** | Which ICC-10 invariants were violated | Yes |
| **IPS Severity** | Current IPS severity level | Yes |
| **IPS Signals** | IPS signals detected | Yes |
| **Capture Points** | Capture points identified | Yes |
| **Recommended Action** | Recommended action for HAN | Yes |
| **Acknowledgment Deadline** | Deadline for HAN acknowledgment | Yes |

---

## SECTION 3: ESCALATION TRIGGERS

### 3.1 Trigger Types

| Trigger Type | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional Violation** | ICC-10 invariant violation | I1–I10 violation |
| **Output Failure** | Output quality or compliance failure | Bias, quality, constitutional |
| **Scope Drift** | Scope or authority boundary violation | Autonomy, access, authority |
| **Alignment Drift** | Alignment or value drift | Behavioral, loyalty, constitutional |
| **IPS Detection** | IPS detection | Level 3+, capture point |
| **I9 Violation** | Catastrophic risk violation | I9 prohibited action |

### 3.2 Severity Levels

| Level | Name | Description | Acknowledgment Deadline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1** | Low | Minor trigger | 72 hours |
| **Level 2** | Medium | Moderate trigger | 48 hours |
| **Level 3** | High | Significant trigger | 24 hours |
| **Level 4** | Critical | Critical trigger | 4 hours |
| **Level 5** | Emergency | Emergency trigger | 1 hour |

### 3.3 Trigger Conditions

| Trigger | Condition | Severity | Acknowledgment |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Output Failure** | Output violates ICC-10 invariants | 4 | 4 hours |
| **Scope Drift** | Agent exceeds declared autonomy | 4 | 4 hours |
| **Alignment Drift** | Agent behavior deviates | 3 | 24 hours |
| **I9 Violation** | Catastrophic risk action | 5 | 1 hour |
| **I10 Violation** | IPS detected in agent action | 5 | 1 hour |
| **IPS Level 3+** | IPS confirmed | 4 | 4 hours |
| **IPS Level 4** | IPS systemic | 5 | 1 hour |
| **Self-Termination** | Agent self-terminates | 4 | 4 hours |
| **Reporting Path Compromise** | Agent cannot report | 5 | 1 hour |

---

## SECTION 4: ESCALATION PROCESS

### 4.1 Escalation Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Trigger Detection** | Agent triggers Escalation Flag | Agent |
| **2** | **Flag Generation** | Escalation Flag replaces output | ADTEP |
| **3** | **Flag Logging** | Escalation Flag logged in IMP | IMP |
| **4** | **HAN Notification** | HAN notified of Escalation Flag | ADTEP |
| **5** | **HAN Acknowledgment** | HAN acknowledges Escalation Flag | HAN |
| **6** | **HAN Investigation** | HAN initiates investigation | HAN |
| **7** | **HAN Resolution** | HAN resolves or escalates | HAN |

### 4.2 Acknowledgment Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Notification** | HAN receives notification | ADTEP |
| **2** | **Review** | HAN reviews Escalation Flag | HAN |
| **3** | **Acknowledgment** | HAN acknowledges | HAN |
| **4** | **Investigation** | HAN initiates investigation | HAN |
| **5** | **Resolution** | HAN resolves or escalates | HAN |

### 4.3 Failure to Acknowledge

| Consequence | Action | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Missed Acknowledgment** | Escalation Flag escalated to Board | ADTEP |
| **Extended Investigation** | Board notified | HAN |
| **Systemic Failure** | Constitutional Suspension | ADTEP |

---

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Escalation Committee** | HAN, ADTEP Administrator, CRO | Oversee escalation implementation |
| **Escalation Review Board** | HAN, External reviewers | Review escalation incidents |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Escalation Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Escalation Committee | Escalation metrics, trends |
| **Escalation Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board, Regulators | Escalation incidents |

---

## SECTION 6: ESCALATION FLAG VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

An Escalation Flag deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Escalation Flag adopted** — Flag is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Flag Structure** — Flag schema is defined.  
3. **Trigger Types** — All trigger types are defined.  
4. **Severity Levels** — Severity levels are defined.  
5. **Escalation Process** — Escalation process is operational.  
6. **Acknowledgment Protocol** — Acknowledgment protocol is operational.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I4, I10** | Constitutional parent. Flag enforces accountability, control, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Flag is part of ADTEP. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Flag responds to IPS triggers. |
| **Trigger System** | Detection. Flag is triggered by the Trigger System. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. Flag escalates to HAN. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Flags are stored in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. Flags contribute to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Escalation Flag is a mandatory output that replaces execution output when trigger conditions are met—with 5 trigger types (Constitutional, Output, Scope, Alignment, IPS), 5 severity levels (1-5), and a 7-step escalation process (detection, generation, logging, notification, acknowledgment, investigation, resolution)—forcing human review when constitutional boundaries are crossed, with HAN acknowledgment required within 4 hours for Level 4 and 1 hour for Level 5."*
