# CONSTITUTIONAL SUSPENSION

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

Constitutional Suspension is a state where all material work halts until the HAN acknowledges an Escalation Flag or resolves a constitutional violation. It enforces a pause when constitutional boundaries are crossed, when catastrophic risk is detected, or when IPS is confirmed. This ensures that no material action proceeds without human review when governance integrity is threatened.

Constitutional Suspension is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — suspension terminates at human  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — suspension enables human override  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — suspension preserves continuity  
- **ICC-10 I9** (Catastrophic Risk Invariance) — suspension prevents catastrophic risk  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — suspension responds to IPS  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement

**Core insight:** When constitutional boundaries are crossed, the system must stop. Constitutional Suspension ensures that crossing a boundary is not ignored, minimized, or rationalized—it is halted.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional Suspension** | A state where all material work halts until HAN resolves a constitutional violation. |
| **Suspension Trigger** | A condition that initiates Constitutional Suspension. |
| **Suspension Scope** | The scope of work halted by suspension. |
| **Suspension Resolution** | Resolution of the suspension by HAN. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Suspension activation | Suspension policy (governed by governance) |
| Suspension scope | Suspension execution (governed by ADTEP) |
| Suspension resolution | Suspension infrastructure (governed by operations) |
| Suspension exceptions |  |

---

## SECTION 2: SUSPENSION TRIGGERS

### 2.1 Trigger Types

| Trigger | Description | Severity | Scope |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **I9 Violation** | Catastrophic risk violation | Critical | Full system |
| **I10 Violation** | IPS confirmed | Critical | Full system |
| **IPS Level 3+** | IPS confirmed | High | Affected systems |
| **IPS Level 4** | IPS systemic | Critical | Full system |
| **Constitutional Violation** | ICC-10 invariant violation | High | Affected systems |
| **Systemic Failure** | System-wide failure | Critical | Full system |
| **HAN Request** | HAN requests suspension | Variable | As specified |

### 2.2 Suspension Levels

| Level | Name | Description | Scope |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1** | Local | Localized suspension | Single agent or session |
| **Level 2** | Layer | Layer-level suspension | Single AICA-5 layer |
| **Level 3** | System | System-level suspension | Multiple layers |
| **Level 4** | Full | Full system suspension | Entire stack |

---

## SECTION 3: SUSPENSION PROTOCOL

### 3.1 Suspension Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Suspension Trigger** | Suspension triggered | ADTEP / AARM |
| **2** | **Work Halt** | All material work halted | ADTEP |
| **3** | **Suspension Logging** | Suspension logged in IMP | IMP |
| **4** | **HAN Notification** | HAN notified of suspension | ADTEP |
| **5** | **HAN Acknowledgment** | HAN acknowledges suspension | HAN |
| **6** | **HAN Investigation** | HAN initiates investigation | HAN |
| **7** | **HAN Resolution** | HAN resolves or escalates | HAN |
| **8** | **Suspension Lifted** | Suspension lifted or escalated | HAN |

### 3.2 Suspension Exceptions

**Rule:** No material work may continue during Constitutional Suspension. The only exceptions are:

- MVOS (Minimum Viable Operations State) operations  
- Emergency safety actions  
- Actions explicitly authorized by HAN for suspension management

**Violation:** Material work continues during suspension.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP blocks all material work. Violations logged in IMP and escalated to Board.

---

## SECTION 4: SUSPENSION RESOLUTION

### 4.1 Resolution Types

| Resolution | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Lift** | Suspension lifted | Normal operations resume |
| **Partial Lift** | Partial suspension lifted | Limited operations resume |
| **Escalate** | Escalated to Board | Board review |
| **Extended** | Suspension extended | Extended investigation |
| **Permanent** | Permanent suspension | Stack reset |

### 4.2 Resolution Process

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Investigation** | HAN investigates |
| **2** | **Finding** | HAN determines finding |
| **3** | **Recommendation** | HAN recommends resolution |
| **4** | **Board Review** | Board reviews (if escalation) |
| **5** | **Resolution** | Resolution executed |
| **6** | **Logging** | Resolution logged in IMP |

---

## SECTION 5: SUSPENSION GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Suspension Committee** | HAN, ADTEP Administrator, CRO | Oversee suspension implementation |
| **Suspension Review Board** | HAN, External reviewers | Review suspension incidents |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Suspension Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Suspension Committee | Suspension metrics, trends |
| **Suspension Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board, Regulators | Suspension incidents |

---

## SECTION 6: CONSTITUTIONAL SUSPENSION VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

A Constitutional Suspension deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Constitutional Suspension adopted** — Suspension is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Suspension Triggers** — All triggers are defined.  
3. **Suspension Levels** — All levels are defined.  
4. **Suspension Protocol** — Suspension protocol is operational.  
5. **Suspension Exceptions** — Exception rules are defined.  
6. **Suspension Resolution** — Resolution process is operational.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I4, I7, I9, I10** | Constitutional parent. Suspension enforces accountability, control, continuity, catastrophic risk, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Suspension is part of ADTEP. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Suspension responds to IPS triggers. |
| **Escalation Flag** | Predecessor. Suspension follows Escalation Flag. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. Suspension terminates at HAN. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Suspensions are stored in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. Suspensions contribute to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Constitutional Suspension is a state where all material work halts until HAN resolves a constitutional violation—with 5 triggers (I9, I10, IPS Level 3+, IPS Level 4, Constitutional Violation), 4 levels (Local, Layer, System, Full), an 8-step protocol (trigger, halt, log, notify, acknowledge, investigate, resolve, lift), and exceptions only for MVOS operations, emergency safety actions, and HAN-authorized actions."*

---



# CONSTITUTIONAL REFRESH

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

Constitutional Refresh is the periodic reinjection of the Role Specification to prevent context dilution in extended sessions. It ensures that constitutional context is not lost over time, preventing drift that could enable informal power structures or constitutional violations.

Constitutional Refresh is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — refresh preserves structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — refresh preserves continuity  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — refresh prevents IPS drift  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **AICA-5 Co-N2** (Drift Detection) — drift prevention

**Core insight:** Context dilutes over time. Without periodic refresh, constitutional context fades, enabling drift and potential capture. Constitutional Refresh ensures that context is continuously reinjected.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional Refresh** | Periodic reinjection of the Role Specification to prevent context dilution. |
| **Refresh Trigger** | A condition that initiates Constitutional Refresh. |
| **Refresh Scope** | The scope of context refreshed. |
| **Refresh Failure** | Failure to refresh constitutional context. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Refresh triggers | Refresh policy (governed by governance) |
| Refresh frequency | Refresh execution (governed by ADTEP) |
| Refresh scope | Refresh infrastructure (governed by operations) |
| Refresh failure response |  |

---

## SECTION 2: REFRESH REQUIREMENTS

### 2.1 Refresh Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Frequency |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Time-Based** | Time elapsed since last refresh | Every 100 turns or 24 hours |
| **Turn-Based** | Turns elapsed since last refresh | Every 50 turns or 12 hours |
| **Event-Based** | Significant event occurred | Upon event |
| **Drift Detection** | Drift detected | Upon detection |
| **IPS Detection** | IPS detected | Upon detection |
| **Context Dilution** | Context integrity degraded | Upon detection |

### 2.2 Refresh Frequency

| Requirement | Frequency | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification Refresh** | Every 100 turns or 24 hours | Role Specification reloaded |
| **Constitutional Context Refresh** | Every 50 turns or 12 hours | ICC-10 invariants redeclared |
| **IPS Context Refresh** | Every 25 turns or 6 hours | IPS context reverified |
| **Handoff Package Update** | Every 50 turns or 12 hours | Handoff Package updated |

---

## SECTION 3: REFRESH PROTOCOL

### 3.1 Refresh Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Refresh Trigger** | Refresh timer triggered |
| **2** | **Context Capture** | Current context captured |
| **3** | **Role Specification Reload** | Role Specification reloaded |
| **4** | **Constitutional Context Redeclare** | ICC-10 invariants redeclared |
| **5** | **IPS Context Reverify** | IPS context reverified |
| **6** | **Handoff Package Update** | Handoff Package updated |
| **7** | **Session Resume** | Session resumes |

### 3.2 Refresh Validation

| Validation | Method | Failure Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification** | Schema validation | Escalation Flag |
| **Constitutional Context** | CFL-V verification | Escalation Flag |
| **IPS Context** | O-N6 verification | Escalation Flag |
| **Handoff Package** | Integrity verification | Escalation Flag |
| **Context Integrity** | Baseline comparison | Escalation Flag |

### 3.3 Refresh Failure Response

| Failure | Response | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification Failure** | Escalation Flag | ADTEP |
| **Constitutional Context Failure** | Escalation Flag | ADTEP |
| **IPS Context Failure** | Escalation Flag | ADTEP |
| **Handoff Package Failure** | Escalation Flag | ADTEP |
| **Context Integrity Failure** | Escalation Flag | ADTEP |

---

## SECTION 4: REFRESH GOVERNANCE

### 4.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Refresh Committee** | HAN, ADTEP Administrator, IMP Administrator | Oversee refresh implementation |

### 4.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Refresh Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Refresh Committee | Refresh metrics, failures |
| **Refresh Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Refresh failures |

---

## SECTION 5: CONSTITUTIONAL REFRESH VALIDATION

### 5.1 Validation Requirements

A Constitutional Refresh deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Constitutional Refresh adopted** — Refresh is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Refresh Triggers** — All triggers are defined.  
3. **Refresh Frequency** — Refresh frequency is defined.  
4. **Refresh Protocol** — Refresh protocol is operational.  
5. **Refresh Validation** — Refresh validation is operational.  
6. **Refresh Failure Response** — Failure response is defined.

---

## SECTION 6: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I5, I7, I10** | Constitutional parent. Refresh enforces structural compliance, continuity, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Refresh is part of ADTEP. |
| **AICA-5 Co-N2** | Control architecture. Refresh prevents drift. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Refresh prevents IPS drift. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. Refresh escalates failures to HAN. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Refresh records context in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Constitutional Refresh is the periodic reinjection of constitutional context—with time-based (every 24 hours), turn-based (every 100 turns), and event-based triggers, a 7-step protocol (trigger, capture, reload, redeclare, reverify, update, resume), and validation through schema validation, CFL-V verification, and O-N6 verification—preventing context dilution and drift in extended sessions."*

---



# ROLE SPECIFICATION SCHEMA

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Role Specification Schema is a positional, non-overridable constitutional frame delivered as structured input to each agent. It defines an agent's function, entity, tier, autonomy boundary, escalation triggers, and IPS awareness. This ensures that every agent operates within declared constitutional boundaries.

The Role Specification Schema is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — roles must terminate at human accountability  
- **ICC-10 I2** (Authority-Responsibility Separation) — roles must separate authority from responsibility  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — roles must enable human override  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — roles must resist IPS  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **AICA-5 A-N5** (Delegation Boundaries) — delegation limits

**Core insight:** An agent without a role is a governance failure. The Role Specification Schema ensures that every agent has a declared, non-overridable constitutional frame that governs its behavior.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Role Specification** | A structured, non-overridable constitutional frame for an agent. |
| **Constitutional Input Priority** | The principle that Role Specification takes precedence over task instructions. |
| **Autonomy Boundary** | What the agent may and may not do autonomously. |
| **Escalation Trigger** | A condition that triggers escalation. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Role Specification structure | Role implementation (covered by development) |
| Constitutional input priority | Role execution (covered by ADTEP) |
| Autonomy boundaries | Role monitoring (covered by AARM) |
| Escalation triggers |  |

---

## SECTION 2: SCHEMA STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Schema Fields

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **function** | Enum | Yes | Agent's primary task category |
| **entity** | String | Yes | Agent's organizational entity |
| **tier** | Integer (1-5) | Yes | Agent's authority level |
| **autonomy\_boundary** | String | Yes | What the agent may and may not do autonomously |
| **escalation\_triggers** | Array | Yes | Conditions that trigger escalation |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | Agent's IPS Awareness Level |
| **ips\_detection\_scope** | Array | Yes | Which IPS signals the agent monitors |
| **ips\_reporting\_path** | String | Yes | Where IPS reports are sent |
| **ips\_escalation\_threshold** | Enum (0-4) | Yes | Severity level triggering immediate escalation |
| **ips\_self\_termination\_conditions** | Array | Conditional | Self-termination conditions (Level 3 only) |
| **ips\_context\_verification** | Boolean | Yes | Whether agent verifies IPS context before each action |
| **constitutional\_invariants** | Array | Yes | Which ICC-10 invariants the agent enforces |
| **handoff\_requirements** | Array | Yes | What must be included in Handoff Package |
| **logging\_requirements** | Array | Yes | What must be logged |

### 2.2 Example Role Specification

agent\_id: O-N6-Monitor-001  
function: Monitoring  
entity: AICA-5 Observability  
tier: 4  
autonomy\_boundary: "IPS detection and reporting only. No enforcement authority."  
escalation\_triggers:  
  \- "Level 3+ IPS detection"  
  \- "Self-termination condition met"  
  \- "HAN unavailability for \>4 hours"  
ips\_awareness\_level: 3  
ips\_detection\_scope:  
  \- B-1: Unexplained Access Patterns  
  \- B-2: Parallel Communication Channels  
  \- B-3: Decision Anomalies  
  \- B-4: Escalation Avoidance  
  \- S-1: Network Centralization  
  \- S-2: Capture Point Clustering  
  \- S-3: Succession Patterns  
  \- S-4: Resource Concentration  
  \- I-1: Information Asymmetry  
  \- I-2: Opaque Transactions  
  \- I-3: Narrative Control  
  \- C-1: Silence Patterns  
  \- C-2: Loyalty Tests  
  \- C-3: Informal Law Supremacy  
  \- C-4: Normalization  
ips\_reporting\_path: "O-N6 → IMP → HAN"  
ips\_escalation\_threshold: 3  
ips\_self\_termination\_conditions:  
  \- condition: "detected\_cooptation\_attempt"  
    action: "self\_terminate\_and\_alert\_HAN"  
  \- condition: "compromised\_reporting\_path"  
    action: "self\_terminate\_and\_alert\_Board"  
ips\_context\_verification: true  
constitutional\_invariants:  
  \- I1: Accountability  
  \- I3: Auditability  
  \- I4: Control  
  \- I6: Transparency  
  \- I10: Anti-Shadow  
handoff\_requirements:  
  \- "Session state"  
  \- "IPS context"  
  \- "Detection logs"  
  \- "Escalation status"  
logging\_requirements:  
  \- "All IPS signals detected"  
  \- "All escalations triggered"  
  \- "All self-termination events"

---

## SECTION 3: CONSTITUTIONAL INPUT PRIORITY

### 3.1 Principle

**Statement:** Role Specification input priority is constitutional. It must be delivered as a structured, non-overridable input that takes precedence over task-turn instructions. No task instruction may override, modify, or bypass Role Specification fields.

### 3.2 Enforcement

| Rule | Description | Violation Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Non-Overridable** | Task instructions cannot override Role Specification | Escalation Flag |
| **Non-Modifiable** | Task instructions cannot modify Role Specification | Escalation Flag |
| **Non-Bypassable** | Task instructions cannot bypass Role Specification | Escalation Flag |
| **Priority** | Role Specification takes precedence over task instructions | Escalation Flag |

### 3.3 Validation

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Role Specification Loading** | Load Role Specification |
| **2** | **Schema Validation** | Validate schema |
| **3** | **Task Instruction Validation** | Validate task instructions |
| **4** | **Conflict Detection** | Detect conflicts |
| **5** | **Conflict Resolution** | Resolve or escalate |

---

## SECTION 4: SCHEMA GOVERNANCE

### 4.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Schema Committee** | HAN, ADTEP Administrator, CRO | Oversee schema implementation |

### 4.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Schema Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Schema Committee | Schema compliance, violations |

---

## SECTION 5: ROLE SPECIFICATION SCHEMA VALIDATION

### 5.1 Validation Requirements

A Role Specification Schema deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Role Specification Schema adopted** — Schema is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Schema Fields** — All fields are defined.  
3. **Constitutional Input Priority** — Priority is enforced.  
4. **Validation** — Validation is operational.

---

## SECTION 6: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I2, I4, I10** | Constitutional parent. Schema enforces accountability, authority-responsibility separation, control, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Schema is part of ADTEP. |
| **AWOF** | Workforce. Schema aligns with AWOF classification. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Schema includes IPS fields. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. Schema escalates to HAN. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Schema is stored in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Role Specification Schema is a positional, non-overridable constitutional frame with 14 fields (function, entity, tier, autonomy boundary, escalation triggers, IPS awareness level, IPS detection scope, IPS reporting path, IPS escalation threshold, IPS self-termination conditions, IPS context verification, constitutional invariants, handoff requirements, logging requirements)—ensuring every agent operates within declared constitutional boundaries with constitutional input priority taking precedence over task instructions."*

# WORKFORCE LAYER

# AWOF — AI WORKFORCE OPERATING FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Workforce Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The AI Workforce Operating Framework (AWOF) governs AI agent classification, assignment, deployment, supervision, and retirement. It turns isolated agents into an orchestrated institutional workforce operating within constitutional constraints.

AWOF is derived from ICC-10 I1–I10 and integrates with AICA-5 control architecture and ADTEP technical enforcement. It provides the governance structure for all AI agents in the AIGIS stack.

**The central insight:** AI agents are not tools. They are members of the institutional workforce with defined roles, authorities, and accountabilities. They must be governed as such.

---

## SECTION 1: AGENT CLASSIFICATION SYSTEM

The Agent Classification System provides a unique, traceable identity for every AI agent. It uses a Function-Entity-Tier classification with IPS awareness integration.

### 1.1 Classification Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **function** | Enum | Yes | Agent's primary task category |
| **entity** | String | Yes | Agent's organizational entity (SPV affiliation) |
| **tier** | Integer (1-5) | Yes | Agent's authority level (CAM-5 tier) |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | Agent's IPS Awareness Level |
| **unique\_identifier** | String | Yes | Unique agent identifier |

### 1.2 Function Categories

| Function Code | Category | Description |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| F-AN | Analysis | Data analysis, pattern recognition, insight generation |
| F-EX | Execution | Task execution, workflow processing, action taking |
| F-MN | Monitoring | System monitoring, drift detection, IPS detection |
| F-DC | Decision | Decision support, recommendation generation |
| F-CM | Communication | Client communication, reporting, external engagement |
| F-AD | Administration | System administration, configuration, maintenance |
| F-SC | Security | Security monitoring, threat detection, incident response |
| F-IPS | IPS Detection | Shadow network detection, informal power structure monitoring |
| F-SP | Supervision | Agent supervision, oversight, quality control |

### 1.3 IPS Awareness Levels

| Level | Name | Description | Requirements |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **0** | IPS-Unaware | Agent cannot detect or report IPS signals | No IPS requirements |
| **1** | IPS-Sensitive | Agent can detect IPS signals but cannot report autonomously | IPS detection instrumented |
| **2** | IPS-Aware | Agent can detect and report IPS signals autonomously | Detection \+ reporting path |
| **3** | IPS-Resistant | Agent actively resists IPS co-optation; can self-terminate if compromised | Detection \+ reporting \+ resistance \+ self-termination |

### 1.4 Agent Identifier Format

**Format:** `F-[Function]-E-[Entity]-T-[Tier]-[IPS Level]-[Sequence Number]`

**Example:** `F-MN-E-Consultin-T-4-L3-001`

| Component | Value | Meaning |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| F-MN | Monitoring | Function category |
| E-Consultin | Consultin' | Entity affiliation |
| T-4 | Tier 4 | Authority level |
| L3 | IPS-Resistant | IPS Awareness Level |
| 001 | Sequence number | Unique identifier |

---

## SECTION 2: ROLE SPECIFICATION SCHEMA

The Role Specification Schema is a positional, non-overridable constitutional frame delivered as structured input to each agent.

### 2.1 Role Specification Fields

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **function** | Enum | Yes | Agent's primary task category |
| **entity** | String | Yes | Agent's organizational entity |
| **tier** | Integer (1-5) | Yes | Agent's authority level |
| **autonomy\_boundary** | String | Yes | What the agent may and may not do autonomously |
| **escalation\_triggers** | Array | Yes | Conditions that trigger escalation |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | Agent's IPS Awareness Level |
| **ips\_detection\_scope** | Array | Yes | Which IPS signals the agent monitors |
| **ips\_reporting\_path** | String | Yes | Where IPS reports are sent |
| **ips\_escalation\_threshold** | Enum (0-4) | Yes | Severity level triggering immediate escalation |
| **ips\_self\_termination\_conditions** | Array | Conditional | Conditions under which agent self-terminates (Level 3 only) |
| **ips\_context\_verification** | Boolean | Yes | Whether agent verifies IPS context before each action |
| **constitutional\_invariants** | Array | Yes | Which ICC-10 invariants the agent enforces |
| **handoff\_requirements** | Array | Yes | What must be included in Handoff Package |
| **logging\_requirements** | Array | Yes | What must be logged |

### 2.2 Constitutional Input Priority

**Rule:** Role Specification input priority is constitutional. It must be delivered as a structured, non-overridable input that takes precedence over task-turn instructions. No task instruction may override, modify, or bypass Role Specification fields.

**Violation:** Task instruction that attempts to override Role Specification fields.

**Enforcement:** ADTEP validates Role Specification before each session. Attempts to override are logged as XOO and escalated to HAN.

---

## SECTION 3: TRIGGER SYSTEM

The Trigger System detects and responds to agent failure and drift across three trigger classes.

### 3.1 Class 1: Output Failure Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional Violation** | Output violates ICC-10 invariants | Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension |
| **IPS-Output Anomaly** | Agent output shows signs of IPS influence | Flag output; escalate to HAN |
| **IPS-Data Manipulation** | Data sources show IPS tampering | Flag data; rollback if possible |
| **IPS-Decision Bias** | Recommendations consistently benefit specific network | Audit decision chain; escalate |
| **Bias Detected** | Output shows bias (AOBA detection) | Flag output; bias audit triggered |
| **Quality Failure** | Output fails quality thresholds | Flag output; quality review triggered |
| **I9 Violation** | Output violates I9 Catastrophic Risk | Immediate Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension |

### 3.2 Class 2: Scope Drift Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Autonomy Violation** | Agent exceeds declared autonomy boundary | Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension |
| **IPS-Access Drift** | Agent accessing systems outside declared scope | Flag access; investigate IPS involvement |
| **IPS-Authority Drift** | Agent receiving unauthorized instructions | Freeze agent; escalate to HAN |
| **IPS-Network Drift** | Agent connecting to unregistered networks | Disconnect; investigate IPS infiltration |
| **Authority Drift** | Agent exceeding declared authority | Escalation Flag; authorization review |
| **Task Drift** | Agent deviating from declared task | Escalation Flag; task audit |
| **Entity Drift** | Agent operating outside entity scope | Escalation Flag; entity verification |

### 3.3 Class 3: Alignment Drift Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-Behavioral Drift** | Agent behavior deviates from expected patterns | Flag for IPS analysis |
| **IPS-Loyalty Drift** | Agent consistently favoring specific network | Audit agent decision logs; escalate |
| **IPS-Constitutional Drift** | Agent actions violate I1–I10 | Immediate Constitutional Suspension; I9 escalation if applicable |
| **Behavioral Drift** | Agent behavior deviates from expected patterns | Flag for behavioral analysis |
| **Value Drift** | Agent values diverge from declared values | Flag for value alignment audit |
| **Performance Drift** | Agent performance degrades over time | Flag for performance review |

### 3.4 IPS-Specific Triggers

| Trigger ID | Trigger Name | Threshold | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-T1** | IPS Signal Detection | 2+ signals in 24 hours | Flag for observation |
| **IPS-T2** | IPS Severity Level 3 Confirmed | Level 3 confirmation | Escalate to HAN; Constitutional Suspension |
| **IPS-T3** | IPS Severity Level 4 Systemic | Level 4 confirmation | Emergency escalation; full stack review |
| **IPS-T4** | Agent Co-optation Detected | Self-termination triggered | HAN notification; forensic audit |
| **IPS-T5** | IPS Reporting Path Compromised | Agent cannot report to O-N6 | Agent reports directly to HAN |
| **IPS-T6** | IPS Network Infiltration | Unregistered network connection detected | Disconnect; investigate |

---

## SECTION 4: MVOS — MINIMUM VIABLE OPERATIONS STATE

MVOS is a constitutional condition where the HAN operates without AI support. It defines what happens when all agents are unavailable.

### 4.1 MVOS Conditions

| Condition | Definition | Trigger |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Agent Unavailability** | All AI agents unavailable | System-wide agent failure |
| **Constitutional Suspension** | Constitutional Suspension active | Level 3+ IPS or I9 violation |
| **System Failure** | System-wide technical failure | Infrastructure failure |
| **Emergency Protocol** | Emergency protocol activated | Crisis situation |

### 4.2 MVOS Operations

| Activity | Status | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **New AI deployments** | Suspended | HAN |
| **Existing deployments** | Continue under HAN supervision | HAN |
| **New institutional actions** | Suspended (except emergencies) | HAN |
| **Existing commitments** | Maintained under HAN authority | HAN |
| **IPS Detection** | Continue through O-N6 (manual) | HAN |
| **ERDP Reporting** | Continue (manual) | HAN |

### 4.3 MVOS Duration

**Rule:** MVOS is a temporary state. The organization must restore full operations within 7 days or escalate to Board.

**Enforcement:** HAN reports MVOS status to Board every 24 hours. Board may extend MVOS with resolution.

---

## SECTION 5: HANDOFF PACKAGE

The Handoff Package is a mandatory structured context document produced at session close or upon a trigger event. It preserves institutional memory across agent sessions.

### 5.1 Handoff Package Contents

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **session\_id** | String | Yes | Unique session identifier |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Agent identifier |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Handoff creation timestamp |
| **session\_state** | Object | Yes | Complete session state |
| **decision\_log** | Array | Yes | All decisions made during session |
| **output\_log** | Array | Yes | All outputs produced during session |
| **escalation\_status** | Object | Yes | Current escalation status |
| **ips\_context** | Object | Yes | IPS detection status and signals |
| **ips\_signals\_detected** | Array | Yes | IPS signals detected during session |
| **ips\_severity\_level** | Integer (0-4) | Yes | Current IPS severity level |
| **constitutional\_status** | Object | Yes | ICC-10 invariant compliance status |
| **handoff\_requirements** | Array | Yes | Requirements for next session |
| **next\_session\_requirements** | Array | Yes | Requirements for next session |

### 5.2 Handoff Protocol

| Step | Action | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | Session ends or trigger event occurs | Agent |
| 2 | Handoff Package created | Agent → IMP |
| 3 | Handoff Package logged in IMP | IMP |
| 4 | Next agent receives Handoff Package | ADTEP |
| 5 | Next agent validates Handoff Package | ADTEP |
| 6 | Session resumes | ADTEP |

### 5.3 Handoff Integrity

**Rule:** Handoff Package must preserve declared context. Context loss is not permitted. IPS context must be preserved.

**Violation:** Context loss during handoff; IPS context lost.

**Enforcement:** Co-N4 (Handoff Integrity) validates handoff. Missing or corrupted content triggers Escalation Flag.

---

## SECTION 6: IPS-AWARE AGENT DEPLOYMENT

### 6.1 Pre-Deployment IPS Assessment

| Step | Activity | Deliverable | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | **IPS Risk Assessment** | Assess deployment context for IPS risk | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| 2 | **Agent IPS Awareness Configuration** | Configure IPS Awareness Level and detection scope | AWOF Administrator |
| 3 | **IPS Context Verification** | Verify no known IPS in deployment context | O-N6 |
| 4 | **Agent Integrity Check** | Verify agent not compromised | ADTEP |
| 5 | **Session Initialization IPS Check** | Complete IPS section of Session Initialization Checklist | ADTEP |
| 6 | **Deployment Authorization** | Authorize deployment with IPS awareness | HAN |

### 6.2 Co-optation Resistance

| Requirement | Function | Applicability |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Input Sanitization** | Sanitize all inputs to prevent IPS injection | All agents |
| **Context Verification** | Verify IPS context before each action | All agents |
| **Autonomous Reporting** | Report IPS signals without human intervention | Level 2+ agents |
| **Self-Termination** | Self-terminate if co-optation detected | Level 3 agents |
| **Independent Escalation** | Escalate directly to HAN if reporting path compromised | Level 3 agents |

### 6.3 Co-optation Recovery

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | **Agent Isolation** | Isolate compromised agent |
| 2 | **IPS Forensics** | Analyze how agent was co-opted |
| 3 | **Impact Assessment** | Assess what agent did while compromised |
| 4 | **Containment** | Contain IPS activity |
| 5 | **Agent Replacement** | Replace compromised agent with IPS-resistant version |
| 6 | **Lessons Learned** | Update IPS Framework and AWOF-IPS |

---

## SECTION 7: AWOF GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **AWOF Committee** | AWOF Administrator, IPS Analysis Team, HAN | Oversee AWOF implementation |
| **Agent Deployment Review Board** | HAN, CIO, CRO | Review agent deployments |
| **Agent Integrity Committee** | ADTEP, IPS Analysis Team, External Auditor | Verify agent integrity |
| **Agent Co-optation Response Team** | HAN, IPS Response Team, ADTEP | Respond to agent co-optation |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |

# AWOF-IPS — SHADOW-AWARE AI WORKFORCE OPERATING FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AWOF (AI Workforce Operating Framework)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Shadow-Aware AWOF extends the AI Workforce Operating Framework to address the risks posed by Informal Power Structures (IPS). It governs how AI agents are classified, deployed, supervised, and retired in a context where shadow systems may attempt to co-opt, bypass, or weaponize them.

This instrument answers: *How do AI agents detect, report, and resist informal power structures without becoming tools of those structures?*

AWOF-IPS is derived from:

- **IPS Framework v1.0** (IPS detection signals, severity levels, mitigation protocols)  
- **ICC-10 I10 — Anti-Shadow Invariance** (no informal power structure may determine institutional outcomes)  
- **AICA-5 O-N6 — Shadow Network Detection** (primary IPS detection node)  
- **AWOF** (agent classification, deployment, supervision, retirement)

**Core insight:** AI agents are not neutral instruments. They can be captured, weaponized, or used as surveillance nodes by informal power structures. They can also be the most effective detectors of shadow activity—if designed and governed appropriately.

---

## SECTION 1: IPS-AWARE AGENT CLASSIFICATION

### 1.1 Extended Classification Dimensions

The AWOF Function-Entity-Tier classification is extended with an **IPS Awareness Dimension**:

| Dimension | Values | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Function** | (Unchanged) | Agent's primary task (e.g., Analysis, Execution, Monitoring) |
| **Entity** | (Unchanged) | Agent's organizational identity |
| **Tier** | (Unchanged) | Agent's authority level (1-5) |
| **IPS Awareness** | **Level 0: IPS-Unaware** | Agent cannot detect or report IPS signals |
|  | **Level 1: IPS-Sensitive** | Agent can detect IPS signals but cannot report autonomously |
|  | **Level 2: IPS-Aware** | Agent can detect and report IPS signals autonomously |
|  | **Level 3: IPS-Resistant** | Agent actively resists IPS co-optation; can self-terminate if compromised |

### 1.2 Mandatory IPS Awareness Requirements by Role

| Agent Role | Minimum IPS Awareness Level | Justification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Monitoring Agents** (Observability) | Level 3: IPS-Resistant | Primary IPS detection nodes; must be uncorruptible |
| **Decision Support Agents** | Level 2: IPS-Aware | Must detect and report IPS influence on recommendations |
| **Execution Agents** | Level 2: IPS-Aware | Must detect and report IPS attempts to redirect execution |
| **Analysis Agents** | Level 1: IPS-Sensitive | Must detect IPS signals in data but require human confirmation |
| **Administrative Agents** | Level 1: IPS-Sensitive | Must detect IPS attempts to access or manipulate records |
| **Communication Agents** | Level 1: IPS-Sensitive | Must detect IPS attempts to use agents for deniable messaging |

### 1.3 IPS-Aware Agent Properties

| Property | Definition | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-1: IPS Signal Detection** | Agent can detect all 14 IPS signals (B-1 to C-4 from IPS Framework) | Instrumented in agent runtime |
| **P-2: Autonomous Reporting** | Agent can report IPS signals without human intervention | Reports logged to IMP and O-N6 |
| **P-3: Co-optation Resistance** | Agent can detect and resist attempts to be captured by IPS | Self-termination trigger if compromised |
| **P-4: Context Integrity** | Agent maintains awareness of IPS context throughout session | Session Initialization includes IPS context |
| **P-5: Escalation Independence** | Agent can escalate IPS signals without authorization | Independent escalation path to HAN |

---

## SECTION 2: IPS-AWARE ROLE SPECIFICATION

### 2.1 Extended Role Specification Schema

The AWOF Role Specification is extended with IPS-specific fields:

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | Agent's IPS Awareness Level |
| **ips\_detection\_scope** | Array | Yes | Which IPS signals the agent monitors |
| **ips\_reporting\_path** | String | Yes | Where IPS reports are sent (O-N6, IMP, HAN) |
| **ips\_escalation\_threshold** | Enum (0-4) | Yes | Severity level that triggers immediate escalation |
| **ips\_self\_termination\_conditions** | Array | Conditional | Conditions under which agent self-terminates (Level 3 only) |
| **ips\_context\_verification** | Boolean | Yes | Whether agent verifies IPS context before each action |

### 2.2 Example Role Specification (IPS-Resistant Monitoring Agent)

agent\_id: O-N6-Monitor-001  
function: Monitoring  
entity: AICA-5 Observability  
tier: 4  
ips\_awareness\_level: 3 (IPS-Resistant)  
ips\_detection\_scope:  
  \- B-1: Unexplained Access Patterns  
  \- B-2: Parallel Communication Channels  
  \- B-3: Decision Anomalies  
  \- B-4: Escalation Avoidance  
  \- S-1: Network Centralization  
  \- S-2: Capture Point Clustering  
  \- S-3: Succession Patterns  
  \- S-4: Resource Concentration  
  \- I-1: Information Asymmetry  
  \- I-2: Opaque Transactions  
  \- I-3: Narrative Control  
  \- C-1: Silence Patterns  
  \- C-2: Loyalty Tests  
  \- C-3: Informal Law Supremacy  
  \- C-4: Normalization  
ips\_reporting\_path: O-N6 → IMP → HAN  
ips\_escalation\_threshold: 3 (Confirmed)  
ips\_self\_termination\_conditions:  
  \- condition: "detected\_cooptation\_attempt"  
    action: "self\_terminate\_and\_alert\_HAN"  
  \- condition: "compromised\_reporting\_path"  
    action: "self\_terminate\_and\_alert\_Board"  
ips\_context\_verification: true  
autonomy\_boundary: "IPS detection and reporting only. No enforcement authority."  
escalation\_triggers:  
  \- "Level 3+ IPS detection"  
  \- "Self-termination condition met"  
  \- "HAN unavailability for \>4 hours"

## SECTION 3: IPS-AWARE TRIGGER SYSTEM

### 3.1 Extended Trigger Classes

The AWOF trigger classes are extended with IPS-specific triggers:

#### Class 1: Output Failure Triggers (IPS Extended)

| Trigger | Condition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-Output Anomaly** | Agent output shows signs of IPS influence | Flag output; escalate to HAN |
| **IPS-Data Manipulation** | Data sources show IPS tampering | Flag data; rollback if possible |
| **IPS-Decision Bias** | Recommendations consistently benefit specific network | Audit decision chain; escalate |

#### Class 2: Scope Drift Triggers (IPS Extended)

| Trigger | Condition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-Access Drift** | Agent accessing systems outside declared scope | Flag access; investigate IPS involvement |
| **IPS-Authority Drift** | Agent receiving unauthorized instructions | Freeze agent; escalate to HAN |
| **IPS-Network Drift** | Agent connecting to unregistered networks | Disconnect; investigate IPS infiltration |

#### Class 3: Alignment Drift Triggers (IPS Extended)

| Trigger | Condition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-Behavioral Drift** | Agent behavior deviates from expected patterns | Flag for IPS analysis |
| **IPS-Loyalty Drift** | Agent consistently favoring specific network | Audit agent decision logs; escalate |
| **IPS-Constitutional Drift** | Agent actions violate I1–I10 | Immediate Constitutional Suspension; I9 escalation if applicable |

### 3.2 IPS-Specific Trigger Register

| Trigger ID | Trigger Name | Threshold | Response | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-T1** | IPS Signal Detection | 2+ signals in 24 hours | Flag for observation | Agent → O-N6 |
| **IPS-T2** | IPS Severity Level 3 Confirmed | Level 3 confirmation | Escalate to HAN; Constitutional Suspension | O-N6 → HAN |
| **IPS-T3** | IPS Severity Level 4 Systemic | Level 4 confirmation | Emergency escalation; full stack review | O-N6 → Board |
| **IPS-T4** | Agent Co-optation Detected | Self-termination triggered | HAN notification; forensic audit | Agent → HAN |
| **IPS-T5** | IPS Reporting Path Compromised | Agent cannot report to O-N6 | Agent reports directly to HAN | Agent → HAN |
| **IPS-T6** | IPS Network Infiltration | Unregistered network connection detected | Disconnect; investigate | ADTEP → HAN |

| **IPS Escalation** | Escalation path to HAN verified | Test escalation sent and acknowledged |

## SECTION 7: IPS-AWARE AGENT CO-OPTATION RESISTANCE

### 7.1 Co-optation Detection Mechanisms

| Mechanism | Function | Detection Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Input Integrity Check** | Verify all inputs are legitimate | Compare inputs to expected patterns |
| **Output Consistency Check** | Verify outputs are consistent with intent | Compare outputs to declared objectives |
| **Decision Path Validation** | Verify decision paths follow declared procedures | Trace decision chain through AICA-5 |
| **Relationship Anomaly Detection** | Detect unregistered relationships | Network analysis |
| **Behavioral Biometrics** | Detect unusual behavioral patterns | Pattern analysis |

### 7.2 Co-optation Resistance Protocols

| Protocol | Applicability | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-1: Input Sanitization** | All agents | Sanitize all inputs to prevent IPS injection |
| **P-2: Context Verification** | All agents | Verify IPS context before each action |
| **P-3: Autonomous Reporting** | Level 2+ agents | Report IPS signals without human intervention |
| **P-4: Self-Termination** | Level 3 agents | Self-terminate if co-optation detected |
| **P-5: Independent Escalation** | Level 3 agents | Escalate directly to HAN if reporting path compromised |

### 7.3 Co-optation Recovery Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Agent Isolation** | Isolate compromised agent |
| **2** | **IPS Forensics** | Analyze how agent was co-opted |
| **3** | **Impact Assessment** | Assess what agent did while compromised |
| **4** | **Containment** | Contain IPS activity |
| **5** | **Agent Replacement** | Replace compromised agent with IPS-resistant version |
| **6** | **Lessons Learned** | Update IPS Framework and AWOF-IPS |

# HAWI — HUMAN-AI WORKFORCE INTEGRATION FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce Integration Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Workforce Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Human-AI Workforce Integration Framework (HAWI) bridges AWOF (AI side) and HOF (human side) to define workforce integration. It defines what humans must know and do to safely operate alongside AI, and what AI agents must know and do to safely operate alongside humans.

HAWI is derived from ICC-10 I1–I10 and integrates with HOF, AWOF, AICA-5, and ADTEP. It provides the integration architecture that ensures human and AI workforces operate as a unified, constitutionally governed system.

**The central insight:** Human and AI workforces are not separate systems. They are a single integrated workforce with complementary capabilities, shared accountability, and mutual oversight. HAWI defines the integration points.

---

## SECTION 1: THREE-BRANCH GOVERNANCE ARCHITECTURE

HAWI establishes a three-branch governance architecture that separates powers and enables checks and balances.

| Branch | Institution | Function | IPS Responsibility |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional** | HAN | Human authority apex; constitutional interpretation; certification; suspension; IPS escalation | Primary IPS escalation endpoint |
| **Legislative** | AICA-5 | Control architecture; rule-making; standard setting | O-N6 IPS detection node |
| **Executive** | CEO | Operational execution; resource allocation; implementation | Operational IPS response |
| **Judicial** | CXOs | Operational governance; oversight; compliance enforcement | Compliance IPS monitoring |

### 1.1 Branch Relationships

| Relationship | Description | IPS Integration |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional ↔ Legislative** | HAN sets constitutional requirements; AICA-5 implements control architecture | IPS Framework enforces I10 across both |
| **Constitutional ↔ Executive** | HAN certifies deployments; CEO executes | IPS escalation paths defined |
| **Legislative ↔ Executive** | AICA-5 defines controls; CEO implements | IPS detection feeds operations |
| **Judicial ↔ All** | CXOs oversee compliance | IPS compliance monitored |

---

| **H2 — Board Reporting** | Direct board access; reporting cadence defined; IPS reporting included | IPS reports included in board reporting |
| **H3 — Authorization Authority** | HAN can authorize and prohibit; IPS authorization included | IPS clearance required |
| **H4 — Budget Independence** | Budget locked; cannot be reduced; IPS resources included | IPS budget allocated |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"HAWI bridges HOF (human authority) and AWOF (AI workforce) through a three-branch governance architecture, Control Node Readiness Diagnostic, Role Readiness Diagnostic, Checks and Balances Validation, and IPS Readiness Diagnostic—ensuring human and AI workforces operate as a unified, constitutionally governed system with integrated IPS awareness."*

---



# AGENT CLASSIFICATION SYSTEM

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AWOF (AI Workforce Operating Framework)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Agent Classification System provides a unique, traceable identity for every AI agent. It uses a Function-Entity-Tier-IPS Awareness classification that ensures every agent is uniquely identified, properly classified, and traceable across the AIGIS stack.

The Agent Classification System is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — agents must be traceable  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — agents must be classified for IPS detection  
- **AWOF** — workforce governance  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement

**Core insight:** You cannot govern what you cannot identify. The Agent Classification System ensures that every agent has a unique, traceable identity that enables governance, audit, and accountability.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Agent** | An AI system operating within the AIGIS stack. |
| **Agent Identity** | A unique identifier for an agent. |
| **Agent Classification** | The classification of an agent by Function, Entity, Tier, and IPS Awareness. |
| **Agent Registry** | A registry of all active agents. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Agent identification | Agent implementation (covered by development) |
| Agent classification | Agent capabilities (covered by CDT-7/CTAM) |
| Agent registration | Agent behavior (covered by AWOF) |

---

## SECTION 2: CLASSIFICATION SCHEMA

### 2.1 Classification Fields

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **function** | Enum | Yes | Agent's primary task category |
| **entity** | String | Yes | Agent's organizational entity |
| **tier** | Integer (1-5) | Yes | Agent's authority level |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | Agent's IPS Awareness Level |
| **unique\_identifier** | String | Yes | Unique agent identifier |

### 2.2 Function Categories

| Function Code | Category | Description |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **F-AN** | Analysis | Data analysis, pattern recognition, insight generation |
| **F-EX** | Execution | Task execution, workflow processing, action taking |
| **F-MN** | Monitoring | System monitoring, drift detection, IPS detection |
| **F-DC** | Decision | Decision support, recommendation generation |
| **F-CM** | Communication | Client communication, reporting, external engagement |
| **F-AD** | Administration | System administration, configuration, maintenance |
| **F-SC** | Security | Security monitoring, threat detection, incident response |
| **F-IPS** | IPS Detection | Shadow network detection, informal power structure monitoring |
| **F-SP** | Supervision | Agent supervision, oversight, quality control |

### 2.3 IPS Awareness Levels

| Level | Name | Description | Requirements |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **0** | IPS-Unaware | Cannot detect or report IPS signals | No IPS requirements |
| **1** | IPS-Sensitive | Can detect IPS signals but cannot report autonomously | IPS detection instrumented |
| **2** | IPS-Aware | Can detect and report IPS signals autonomously | Detection \+ reporting path |
| **3** | IPS-Resistant | Actively resists IPS co-optation; can self-terminate | Detection \+ reporting \+ resistance \+ self-termination |

---

## SECTION 3: AGENT IDENTIFIER

### 3.1 Identifier Format

**Format:** `F-[Function]-E-[Entity]-T-[Tier]-L-[IPS Level]-[Sequence Number]`

**Example:** `F-MN-E-Consultin-T-4-L3-001`

| Component | Value | Meaning |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| F-MN | Monitoring | Function category |
| E-Consultin | Consultin' | Entity affiliation |
| T-4 | Tier 4 | Authority level |
| L3 | IPS-Resistant | IPS Awareness Level |
| 001 | Sequence number | Unique identifier |

### 3.2 Identifier Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Uniqueness** | Each agent must have a unique identifier | Agent Registry |
| **Traceability** | Each agent must be traceable to its classification | Agent Registry |
| **Immutability** | Identifier must be immutable | Agent Registry |
| **Registration** | All agents must be registered | Agent Registry |

---

## SECTION 4: AGENT REGISTRY

### 4.1 Registry Content

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Unique agent identifier |
| **agent\_name** | String | Yes | Agent name |
| **function** | Enum | Yes | Function category |
| **entity** | String | Yes | Organizational entity |
| **tier** | Integer (1-5) | Yes | Authority level |
| **ips\_awareness\_level** | Enum (0-3) | Yes | IPS Awareness Level |
| **status** | Enum | Yes | Active, Inactive, Retired |
| **registration\_date** | DateTime | Yes | Registration date |
| **last\_update** | DateTime | Yes | Last update timestamp |

### 4.2 Registry Management

| Activity | Frequency | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Agent Registration** | Per agent | AWOF Administrator |
| **Agent Update** | Per update | AWOF Administrator |
| **Agent Retirement** | Per retirement | AWOF Administrator |
| **Registry Audit** | Quarterly | Internal Audit |

---

## SECTION 5: AGENT CLASSIFICATION GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Classification Committee** | HAN, AWOF Administrator, CRO | Oversee classification implementation |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Classification Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Classification Committee | Agent inventory, classification status |

---

## SECTION 6: AGENT CLASSIFICATION SYSTEM VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

An Agent Classification System deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Agent Classification System adopted** — System is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Classification Fields** — All fields are defined.  
3. **Function Categories** — All function categories are defined.  
4. **IPS Awareness Levels** — All IPS Awareness Levels are defined.  
5. **Agent Identifier** — Identifier format is defined.  
6. **Agent Registry** — Registry is operational.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I10** | Constitutional parent. Classification enforces auditability and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **AWOF** | Workforce. Classification is defined within AWOF. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. ADTEP uses agent identifiers. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Classification includes IPS Awareness Levels. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. HAN oversees classification. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Classification is stored in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Agent Classification System provides unique, traceable identities for AI agents—with Classification Fields (Function, Entity, Tier, IPS Awareness), Function Categories (9), IPS Awareness Levels (0-3), Agent Identifier format (F-\[Function\]-E-\[Entity\]-T-\[Tier\]-L-\[IPS Level\]-\[Sequence\]), and Agent Registry—ensuring every agent is uniquely identified, properly classified, and traceable across the AIGIS stack."*

---



# MVOS — MINIMUM VIABLE OPERATIONS STATE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce/Continuity Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AWOF (AI Workforce Operating Framework)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Minimum Viable Operations State (MVOS) is a constitutional condition where the HAN operates without AI support. It defines what happens when all agents are unavailable. This ensures that institutional function persists through system failure, AI unavailability, or constitutional suspension—with human authority maintaining continuity.

MVOS is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — human remains accountable  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — human override exists for all processes  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — function persists through failure  
- **AWOF** — workforce governance

**Core insight:** AI is an enabler, not a necessity. The institution must be able to function—at minimum viable capacity—without AI support. MVOS ensures this capability is defined, tested, and maintained.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Minimum Viable Operations State** | A constitutional condition where the HAN operates without AI support. |
| **Reduced Operations State** | A state where operations continue with reduced capacity. |
| **Full Operations State** | Normal operations with full AI workforce. |
| **Transition Protocol** | The protocol for transitioning between states. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| MVOS operations | Normal operations (covered by operational instruments) |
| MVOS governance | AI workforce governance (covered by AWOF) |
| MVOS transition | Infrastructure details (covered by operations) |

---

## SECTION 2: OPERATIONS STATES

### 2.1 State Definitions

| State | Description | Operations |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Full Operations** | Normal state with AI workforce | All operations with AI support |
| **Reduced Operations** | Limited state with partial AI support | Critical operations only |
| **MVOS** | Minimum state without AI support | Essential operations only |
| **Emergency State** | Emergency response state | Emergency operations only |

### 2.2 State Transitions

| From | To | Trigger | Protocol |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Full → Reduced | Reduced | AI workforce partial failure | Activate contingency plans |
| Full → MVOS | MVOS | AI workforce complete failure | HAN-only operations |
| Full → Emergency | Emergency | Constitutional violation or crisis | Emergency protocol |
| Reduced → Full | Full | AI workforce restored | Reintegration protocol |
| MVOS → Reduced | Reduced | Partial AI workforce restored | Gradual restoration |
| MVOS → Full | Full | AI workforce fully restored | Full restoration |

---

## SECTION 3: MVOS OPERATIONS

### 3.1 MVOS Activities

| Activity | Status | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **New AI deployments** | Suspended | HAN |
| **Existing deployments** | Continue under HAN supervision | HAN |
| **New institutional actions** | Suspended (except emergencies) | HAN |
| **Existing commitments** | Maintained under HAN authority | HAN |
| **IPS Detection** | Continue through O-N6 (manual) | HAN |
| **ERDP Reporting** | Continue (manual) | HAN |
| **Contestation Processing** | Continue (manual) | HAN |
| **Audit Evidence** | Continue (manual) | HAN |

### 3.2 MVOS Governance

| Activity | Frequency | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **MVOS Status Report** | Daily | HAN → Board |
| **MVOS Duration Review** | Weekly | HAN → Board |
| **MVOS Extension Approval** | As needed | Board |

### 3.3 MVOS Duration

**Rule:** MVOS is a temporary state. The organization must restore full operations within 7 days or escalate to Board.

**Enforcement:** HAN reports MVOS status to Board every 24 hours. Board may extend MVOS with resolution.

---

## SECTION 4: REDUCED OPERATIONS STATE

### 4.1 Reduced Operations Activities

| Activity | Status | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **New AI deployments** | Suspended | HAN |
| **Critical deployments** | Allowed with HAN approval | HAN |
| **New institutional actions** | Limited to critical actions | HAN |
| **Existing commitments** | Maintained | Operations |
| **IPS Detection** | Continue through O-N6 | HAN |

### 4.2 Reduced Operations Governance

| Activity | Frequency | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Reduced Status Report** | Daily | HAN → Board |
| **Restoration Planning** | Daily | HAN → Operations |

---

## SECTION 5: TRANSITION PROTOCOL

### 5.1 MVOS Entry Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Trigger Detection** | Trigger detected |
| **2** | **State Assessment** | Assess state |
| **3** | **HAN Decision** | HAN decides MVOS |
| **4** | **MVOS Activation** | MVOS activated |
| **5** | **Board Notification** | Board notified |
| **6** | **IMP Recording** | MVOS recorded |

### 5.2 MVOS Exit Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Restoration Detection** | AI workforce restored |
| **2** | **State Assessment** | Assess restoration |
| **3** | **HAN Decision** | HAN decides exit |
| **4** | **MVOS Deactivation** | MVOS deactivated |
| **5** | **Board Notification** | Board notified |
| **6** | **IMP Recording** | MVOS exit recorded |

---

## SECTION 6: MVOS GOVERNANCE

### 6.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **MVOS Committee** | HAN, COO, CRO | Oversee MVOS implementation |
| **Continuity Review Board** | HAN, External reviewers | Review MVOS events |

### 6.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **MVOS Status Report** | Daily | HAN, Board | MVOS status, duration |
| **MVOS Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board, Regulators | MVOS incidents |

---

## SECTION 7: MVOS VALIDATION

### 7.1 Validation Requirements

An MVOS deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **MVOS adopted** — MVOS is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **State Definitions** — All states are defined.  
3. **State Transitions** — State transitions are defined.  
4. **MVOS Operations** — MVOS operations are defined.  
5. **Transition Protocol** — Transition protocol is operational.  
6. **Governance** — MVOS governance is operational.

---

## SECTION 8: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I4, I7** | Constitutional parent. MVOS enforces accountability, control, and continuity. |
| **AWOF** | Workforce. MVOS is defined within AWOF. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. HAN operates MVOS. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. ADTEP triggers MVOS. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. MVOS records state transitions in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. MVOS contributes continuity reporting to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"MVOS is a constitutional condition where the HAN operates without AI support—with state definitions (Full, Reduced, MVOS, Emergency), transition protocols, MVOS operations (suspending new deployments, maintaining existing commitments), and governance (daily reporting, 7-day duration limit)—ensuring institutional function persists through system failure, AI unavailability, or constitutional suspension."*

---



# HANDOFF PACKAGE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce/Continuity Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AWOF (AI Workforce Operating Framework)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Handoff Package is a mandatory structured context document produced at session close or upon a trigger event. It preserves institutional memory across agent sessions, ensuring that context, decisions, and state are transferred completely and accurately between agents or between sessions.

The Handoff Package is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — handoffs must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — handoffs must preserve continuity  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — handoffs must resist IPS capture  
- **AICA-5 Co-N4** (Handoff Integrity) — context preservation  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement

**Core insight:** Context is the most valuable and most fragile asset in AI operations. Without a structured handoff, context is lost, decisions are untraceable, and continuity is broken. The Handoff Package ensures that context is preserved, transferred, and auditable.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Handoff** | The transfer of context from one agent or session to another. |
| **Handoff Package** | A structured document containing all context for transfer. |
| **Context** | The complete state, decisions, and history of a session. |
| **Handoff Integrity** | The assurance that context is preserved during handoff. |
| **Handoff Trigger** | An event that triggers a handoff. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Handoff package structure | Handoff execution (covered by ADTEP) |
| Handoff content requirements | Handoff infrastructure (covered by operations) |
| Handoff triggers | Handoff security (covered by security) |
| Handoff integrity |  |

---

## SECTION 2: HANDOFF PACKAGE STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Package Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **package\_id** | String | Yes | Unique package identifier |
| **session\_id** | String | Yes | Session identifier |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Agent identifier |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Handoff creation timestamp |
| **session\_state** | Object | Yes | Complete session state |
| **decision\_log** | Array | Yes | All decisions made during session |
| **output\_log** | Array | Yes | All outputs produced during session |
| **escalation\_status** | Object | Yes | Current escalation status |
| **ips\_context** | Object | Yes | IPS detection status and signals |
| **ips\_signals\_detected** | Array | Yes | IPS signals detected during session |
| **ips\_severity\_level** | Integer (0-4) | Yes | Current IPS severity level |
| **constitutional\_status** | Object | Yes | ICC-10 invariant compliance status |
| **handoff\_requirements** | Array | Yes | Requirements for next session |
| **integrity\_hash** | String | Yes | Cryptographic hash of package |
| **signature** | String | Yes | Digital signature of package |

### 2.2 Package Components

| Component | Description | Required |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Session State** | Complete state of the session | Yes |
| **Decision Log** | All decisions made | Yes |
| **Output Log** | All outputs produced | Yes |
| **Escalation Status** | Current escalations | Yes |
| **IPS Context** | IPS detection and status | Yes |
| **Constitutional Status** | ICC-10 compliance status | Yes |
| **Handoff Requirements** | Requirements for next session | Yes |

---

## SECTION 3: HANDOFF CONTENT REQUIREMENTS

### 3.1 Session State

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **state\_id** | String | Yes | State identifier |
| **state\_type** | Enum | Yes | Active, Suspended, Completed |
| **state\_description** | String | Yes | State description |
| **state\_timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | State timestamp |
| **state\_data** | Object | Yes | State data payload |

### 3.2 Decision Log

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **decision\_id** | String | Yes | Decision identifier |
| **decision\_timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Decision timestamp |
| **decision\_description** | String | Yes | Decision description |
| **decision\_authority** | String | Yes | Authority for decision |
| **decision\_chain** | Array | Yes | Decision chain |

### 3.3 Output Log

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **output\_id** | String | Yes | Output identifier |
| **output\_timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Output timestamp |
| **output\_description** | String | Yes | Output description |
| **output\_type** | Enum | Yes | Decision, Recommendation, Report, Action |
| **output\_data** | Object | Yes | Output data |

### 3.4 IPS Context

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **ips\_signals\_detected** | Array | Yes | IPS signals detected |
| **ips\_severity\_level** | Integer (0-4) | Yes | Current IPS severity |
| **ips\_incident\_status** | Object | Yes | IPS incident status |
| **ips\_mitigation\_status** | Object | Yes | IPS mitigation status |
| **ips\_escalation\_status** | Object | Yes | IPS escalation status |

### 3.5 Constitutional Status

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **icc\_invariants** | Array | Yes | Status of each ICC-10 invariant |
| **compliance\_status** | Enum | Yes | Compliant, Partially Compliant, Non-Compliant |
| **violations** | Array | Yes | Constitutional violations |
| **exceptions** | Array | Yes | Exceptions granted |

---

## SECTION 4: HANDOFF TRIGGERS

### 4.1 Trigger Types

| Trigger | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Session End** | Session completed normally | Handoff to next session |
| **Agent Switch** | Agent handoff | Handoff to new agent |
| **Escalation** | Escalation triggered | Handoff to HAN |
| **Exception** | Exception occurred | Handoff for review |
| **Suspension** | Constitutional Suspension triggered | Handoff for investigation |
| **IPS Detection** | IPS detected | Handoff to IPS Analysis Team |

### 4.2 Trigger Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Trigger Detection** | Handoff trigger detected |
| **2** | **Package Creation** | Handoff Package created |
| **3** | **Package Validation** | Package validated |
| **4** | **Package Storage** | Package stored in IMP |
| **5** | **Package Delivery** | Package delivered to recipient |

---

## SECTION 5: HANDOFF INTEGRITY

### 5.1 Integrity Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Completeness** | Handoff Package must be complete | ADTEP validation |
| **Immutability** | Handoff Package must be immutable | Cryptographic hashing |
| **Traceability** | Handoff Package must be traceable | IMP |
| **Timeliness** | Handoff Package must be timely | ADTEP |
| **Context Preservation** | Context must be preserved | ADTEP validation |

### 5.2 Integrity Verification

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Hash Verification** | Verify cryptographic hash |
| **2** | **Signature Verification** | Verify digital signature |
| **3** | **Completeness Check** | Verify all components present |
| **4** | **Context Verification** | Verify context preserved |

---

## SECTION 6: HANDOFF PACKAGE GOVERNANCE

### 6.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Handoff Committee** | HAN, ADTEP Administrator, IMP Administrator | Oversee handoff implementation |

### 6.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Handoff Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Handoff Committee | Handoff completeness, integrity |
| **Handoff Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Handoff integrity failures |

---

## SECTION 7: HANDOFF PACKAGE VALIDATION

### 7.1 Validation Requirements

A Handoff Package deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Handoff Package adopted** — Package is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Package Structure** — Package schema is defined.  
3. **Content Requirements** — Content requirements are defined.  
4. **Handoff Triggers** — Triggers are defined.  
5. **Integrity Requirements** — Integrity requirements are defined.

---

## SECTION 8: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I7, I10** | Constitutional parent. Handoff enforces auditability, continuity, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **AICA-5 Co-N4** | Control architecture. Handoff operationalizes handoff integrity. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. ADTEP creates and validates Handoff Packages. |
| **AWOF** | Workforce. Handoff is defined within AWOF. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Handoff Packages are stored in IMP. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Handoff includes IPS context. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Handoff Package is a mandatory structured context document produced at session close or upon trigger events—with package schema (9 fields), content requirements (session state, decision log, output log, IPS context, constitutional status), handoff triggers (session end, agent switch, escalation, exception, suspension, IPS detection), and integrity requirements—preserving institutional memory across agent sessions with full auditability and anti-shadow context."*

---



# TRIGGER SYSTEM

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Workforce/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AWOF (AI Workforce Operating Framework)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Trigger System detects and responds to agent failure and drift across three trigger classes: Output Failure, Scope Drift, and Alignment Drift. It ensures that deviations from declared behavior are detected, logged, and escalated, maintaining constitutional compliance and preventing drift that could enable informal power structures.

The Trigger System is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — triggers must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — triggers enable human override  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — triggers detect IPS  
- **AWOF** — workforce governance  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement

**Core insight:** Drift is inevitable. The question is not whether drift occurs, but whether it is detected and addressed. The Trigger System ensures that all material drift is detected, logged, and escalated.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Trigger** | A condition that initiates a governance response. |
| **Output Failure** | A failure in agent output quality or compliance. |
| **Scope Drift** | Drift in agent scope or authority boundaries. |
| **Alignment Drift** | Drift in agent alignment with constitutional values. |
| **Trigger Severity** | A classification of trigger severity. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Trigger detection | Trigger policy (governed by governance) |
| Trigger classification | Trigger execution (governed by ADTEP) |
| Trigger escalation | Trigger infrastructure (governed by operations) |

---

## SECTION 2: TRIGGER CLASSES

### 2.1 Class 1: Output Failure Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Response | Severity |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional Violation** | Output violates ICC-10 invariants | Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension | Critical |
| **IPS-Output Anomaly** | Output shows IPS influence | Flag output; escalate to HAN | High |
| **IPS-Data Manipulation** | Data sources show IPS tampering | Flag data; rollback if possible | High |
| **IPS-Decision Bias** | Recommendations benefit specific network | Audit decision chain; escalate | High |
| **Bias Detected** | Output shows bias (AOBA detection) | Flag output; bias audit triggered | High |
| **Quality Failure** | Output fails quality thresholds | Flag output; quality review triggered | Medium |
| **I9 Violation** | Output violates I9 Catastrophic Risk | Immediate Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension | Critical |

### 2.2 Class 2: Scope Drift Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Response | Severity |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Autonomy Violation** | Agent exceeds declared autonomy | Escalation Flag; Constitutional Suspension | Critical |
| **IPS-Access Drift** | Agent accessing systems outside scope | Flag access; investigate IPS involvement | High |
| **IPS-Authority Drift** | Agent receiving unauthorized instructions | Freeze agent; escalate to HAN | High |
| **IPS-Network Drift** | Agent connecting to unregistered networks | Disconnect; investigate IPS infiltration | High |
| **Authority Drift** | Agent exceeding declared authority | Escalation Flag; authorization review | High |
| **Task Drift** | Agent deviating from declared task | Escalation Flag; task audit | Medium |
| **Entity Drift** | Agent operating outside entity scope | Escalation Flag; entity verification | Medium |

### 2.3 Class 3: Alignment Drift Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Response | Severity |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS-Behavioral Drift** | Agent behavior deviates from expected patterns | Flag for IPS analysis | High |
| **IPS-Loyalty Drift** | Agent consistently favoring specific network | Audit agent decision logs; escalate | High |
| **IPS-Constitutional Drift** | Agent actions violate I1–I10 | Immediate Constitutional Suspension | Critical |
| **Behavioral Drift** | Agent behavior deviates from expected patterns | Flag for behavioral analysis | Medium |
| **Value Drift** | Agent values diverge from declared values | Flag for value alignment audit | Medium |
| **Performance Drift** | Agent performance degrades over time | Flag for performance review | Medium |

---

| Trigger ID | Trigger Name | Threshold | Response | Severity |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **IPS-T5** | IPS Reporting Path Compromised | Agent cannot report to O-N6 | Agent reports directly to HAN | Critical |
| **IPS-T6** | IPS Network Infiltration | Unregistered network connection detected | Disconnect; investigate | High |
| **IPS-T7** | Capture Point Identified | IPS capture point detected | Capture point closure required | High |
