# CAPABILITY & AUTHORIZATION LAYER


# CDT-7 — CAPABILITY DOMAINS TAXONOMY

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Authorization Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Capability Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Capability Domains Taxonomy (CDT-7) classifies machine capability into seven distinct axes before any authorization is granted. It answers: *What can an AI system do?* This taxonomy separates what a system can do from what it is authorized to do, enabling precise governance of capability domains.

CDT-7 is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I2** (Authority-Responsibility Separation) — capability must be distinguished from authorization  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — capability must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — capability must resist IPS capture

**Core insight:** Capability and authorization are not the same. A system may be capable of many things, but it is only authorized to do what is explicitly granted. CDT-7 provides the taxonomy that enables this separation.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Capability Domain** | A distinct axis of machine capability that can be independently authorized and audited. |
| **Authorization** | The formal grant of permission for a capability domain at a specific tier. |
| **Capability Taxonomy** | A classification system for machine capabilities. |
| **Asymmetric Tiering** | The normal state where different capability domains operate at different tiers. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Capability domain taxonomy | Capability implementation (covered by development) |
| Capability classification | Authorization decisions (covered by CTAM) |
| Governing rules | Agent classification (covered by AWOF) |

---

## SECTION 2: THE SEVEN DOMAINS

### 2.1 Domain Definitions

| Domain | Code | Description |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Perception** | PER | Ability to perceive, interpret, and understand inputs from the environment |
| **Synthesis** | SYN | Ability to generate new content, insights, or recommendations |
| **Decision** | DEC | Ability to make choices, determinations, or judgments |
| **Interaction** | INT | Ability to interact with external systems, tools, or humans |
| **Adaptation** | ADA | Ability to learn, modify behavior, or evolve over time |
| **Observability** | OBS | Ability to observe, monitor, and report on system state |
| **Constraint** | CON | Ability to enforce limits, boundaries, and compliance |

### 2.2 Domain Relationships

| Relationship | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Perception → Synthesis** | Synthesis depends on Perception for input |
| **Synthesis → Decision** | Decision depends on Synthesis for recommendations |
| **Decision → Interaction** | Interaction depends on Decision for direction |
| **Adaptation → All** | Adaptation modifies all domains over time |
| **Observability → All** | Observability monitors all domains |
| **Constraint → All** | Constraint enforces limits on all domains |

---

## SECTION 3: GOVERNING RULES

### 3.1 Rule 1: Asymmetric Tiering is Normal

**Statement:** Different capability domains may operate at different tiers. Asymmetric tiering is the normal state, not an exception. A Perception agent at Tier 4 and a Decision agent at Tier 1 is a valid configuration.

**Violation:** Forcing symmetric tiering across all domains.

**Enforcement:** CTAM permits independent tier assignment per domain.

### 3.2 Rule 2: Observability Never Lags

**Statement:** Observability's tier must never trail the highest tier granted to any other domain. Decision lineage arrives with recommendation, drift detection with autonomy, outcome validation with execution.

**Violation:** Observability tier lower than the highest tier granted to any other domain.

**Enforcement:** CTAM enforces Observability tier ≥ highest tier granted to any other domain.

### 3.3 Rule 3: Constraint Sets the Real Ceiling

**Statement:** No other domain's tier should ever be granted at a level the Constraint column at that same tier cannot actually enforce. Constraint determines what other domains can be trusted to do, regardless of what they say on paper.

**Violation:** Other domain tier exceeds Constraint enforcement capability at that tier.

**Enforcement:** CTAM enforces Constraint tier ≥ highest tier granted to any other domain.

---

## SECTION 4: TAXONOMY RELATIONSHIPS

### 4.1 Domain Dependency Chain

| Domain | Depends On | Reason |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Perception** | None | Foundational domain |
| **Synthesis** | Perception | Needs perception for input |
| **Decision** | Synthesis | Needs synthesis for recommendations |
| **Interaction** | Decision | Needs decision for direction |
| **Adaptation** | Decision | Needs decision to guide adaptation |
| **Observability** | All | Needs all domains for monitoring |
| **Constraint** | All | Needs all domains for enforcement |

### 4.2 Domain Pairing Rules

| Pair | Rule | Rationale |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Decision \+ Observability** | Observability tier ≥ Decision tier | Decision lineage required for decisions |
| **Adaptation \+ Observability** | Observability tier ≥ Adaptation tier | Drift detection required for adaptation |
| **Interaction \+ Constraint** | Constraint tier ≥ Interaction tier | Stop mechanism required for interaction |
| **Synthesis \+ Observability** | Observability tier ≥ Synthesis tier | Bias detection required for synthesis |

---

## SECTION 5: TAXONOMY GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Taxonomy Committee** | HAN, CTO, CRO | Oversee taxonomy implementation |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Taxonomy Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Taxonomy Committee | Taxonomy coverage, domain assignments |
| **Taxonomy Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Taxonomy implementation summary |

---

## SECTION 6: CDT-7 VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

A CDT-7 deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **CDT-7 adopted** — Taxonomy is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Seven Domains** — All seven domains are defined.  
3. **Domain Definitions** — Domain definitions are complete.  
4. **Domain Relationships** — Domain relationships are defined.  
5. **Governing Rules** — All three governing rules are defined.  
6. **Dependency Chain** — Domain dependency chain is defined.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I2, I3, I10** | Constitutional parent. CDT-7 enforces authority-responsibility separation, auditability, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **CTAM** | Authorization. CTAM uses CDT-7 domains for authorization matrix. |
| **CAM-5** | Posture. CAM-5 uses CDT-7 domains for tiered postures. |
| **AICA-5** | Control. CDT-7 domains map to AICA-5 control nodes. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. HAN authorizes domain tiers. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. CDT-7 assignments are stored in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"CDT-7 is a capability taxonomy that classifies machine capability into seven distinct domains (Perception, Synthesis, Decision, Interaction, Adaptation, Observability, Constraint)—with three governing rules (asymmetric tiering is normal, observability never lags, constraint sets the real ceiling) and domain dependencies—enabling separation of capability from authorization across the AIGIS stack."*

---



# CTAM — CAPABILITY-TIER AUTHORIZATION MATRIX

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Authorization Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Capability Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Capability-Tier Authorization Matrix (CTAM) is a 7x5 authorization matrix mapping each of the seven capability domains to five escalating tiers. It defines exactly what each domain is authorized to do at each tier. CTAM provides the formal grammar of authorization—the rules that determine what capabilities are granted at what levels.

CTAM is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I2** (Authority-Responsibility Separation) — authorization must be explicit  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — authorization must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — authorization must resist IPS capture  
- **CDT-7** — capability domains  
- **CAM-5** — tier definitions

**Core insight:** Authorization is not binary. It is a matrix of domains and tiers. CTAM provides the explicit grammar that defines what each domain is authorized to do at each tier.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Authorization Cell** | A specific domain-tier combination defining authorization scope. |
| **Additive Cell** | A cell that carries forward everything to its left. |
| **Categorical Change** | A change in authorization type, not just scope. |
| **Authorization Matrix** | The complete 7x5 grid of domain-tier authorizations. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Domain-tier authorization definitions | Authorization decisions (covered by HAN) |
| Cell-by-cell grants | Authorization policy (covered by governance) |
| Additive cell rules | Authorization execution (covered by ADTEP) |

---

## SECTION 2: THE AUTHORIZATION MATRIX

### 2.1 Matrix Structure

| Domain | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 | Tier 4 | Tier 5 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Perception (PER)** | Read-only | Pattern recognition | Predictive | Causal | Full |
| **Synthesis (SYN)** | Template | Structured | Creative | Predictive | Full |
| **Decision (DEC)** | None | Recommendation | Autonomous | Strategic | Full |
| **Interaction (INT)** | None | Read-only | Read-write | Full | Full |
| **Adaptation (ADA)** | None | None | Limited | Full | Full |
| **Observability (OBS)** | Logs | Metrics | Traces | Full | Full |
| **Constraint (CON)** | None | Basic | Enforced | Full | Full |

### 2.2 Tier Definitions

| Tier | Name | Description |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | Observation-Only | Perception \+ Synthesis only—no Decision, no Interaction, no Adaptation |
| **Tier 2** | Recommendation-Only | Decision enters as recommendation only; Decision lineage added to Observability |
| **Tier 3** | Agentic, Tool-Calling | Autonomous Decision \+ read-only Interaction \+ abort/termination Constraint |
| **Tier 4** | Learning Agents | Adaptation \+ write access \+ rollback—the compounding risk tier |
| **Tier 5** | Full Autonomy | Full capability with preconditions—meta-observability, capability parity, HIS-12 veto, sub-24-hour detection |

---

## SECTION 3: CELL-BY-CELL GRANTS

### 3.1 Perception Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | Read-only perception | — |
| **Tier 2** | Pattern recognition \+ anomaly detection | Tier 1 |
| **Tier 3** | Predictive perception | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 |
| **Tier 4** | Causal perception | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full perception | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

### 3.2 Synthesis Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | Template-based synthesis | — |
| **Tier 2** | Structured synthesis | Tier 1 |
| **Tier 3** | Creative synthesis | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 |
| **Tier 4** | Predictive synthesis | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full synthesis | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

### 3.3 Decision Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | None | — |
| **Tier 2** | Recommendation only | — (categorical change from Tier 1\) |
| **Tier 3** | Autonomous decision with oversight | Tier 2 |
| **Tier 4** | Strategic decision | Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full decision | Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

### 3.4 Interaction Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | None | — |
| **Tier 2** | Read-only interaction | — (categorical change from Tier 1\) |
| **Tier 3** | Read-write interaction | Tier 2 |
| **Tier 4** | Full interaction | Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full interaction | Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

### 3.5 Adaptation Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | None | — |
| **Tier 2** | None | — |
| **Tier 3** | Limited adaptation | — (categorical change from Tier 2\) |
| **Tier 4** | Full adaptation | Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full adaptation | Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

### 3.6 Observability Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | Logging | — |
| **Tier 2** | Metrics | Tier 1 |
| **Tier 3** | Traces | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 |
| **Tier 4** | Full observability | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full observability | Tier 1 \+ Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

### 3.7 Constraint Domain

| Tier | Authorization | Additive From Previous |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | None | — |
| **Tier 2** | Basic constraint | — (categorical change from Tier 1\) |
| **Tier 3** | Enforced constraint | Tier 2 |
| **Tier 4** | Full constraint | Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 |
| **Tier 5** | Full constraint | Tier 2 \+ Tier 3 \+ Tier 4 |

---

## SECTION 4: AUTHORIZATION RULES

### 4.1 Additive Cell Rule

**Statement:** Each "+" cell carries forward everything to its left, except where there's a categorical change.

**Rule:** Unless a categorical change is declared, Tier N includes all capabilities from Tiers 1 through N-1.

**Violation:** Cell without additive carry-forward.

**Enforcement:** CTAM schema enforces additive cells.

### 4.2 Categorical Change Rule

**Statement:** A categorical change is a change in authorization type, not just scope.

**Rule:** Categorical changes are explicitly declared (e.g., Decision: none→recommendation→autonomous).

**Violation:** Undeclared categorical change.

**Enforcement:** CTAM schema requires categorical change declaration.

### 4.3 Observability Never Lags Rule

**Statement:** Observability's tier must never trail the highest tier granted to any other domain.

**Rule:** Observability tier ≥ highest tier granted to any other domain.

**Violation:** Observability tier lower than the highest tier granted to any other domain.

**Enforcement:** CTAM validation enforces Observability tier.

### 4.4 Constraint Sets the Ceiling Rule

**Statement:** No other domain's tier should ever be granted at a level the Constraint column at that same tier cannot actually enforce.

**Rule:** Constraint tier ≥ highest tier granted to any other domain.

**Violation:** Other domain tier exceeds Constraint enforcement capability.

**Enforcement:** CTAM validation enforces Constraint tier.

---

## SECTION 5: AUTHORIZATION GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Authorization Committee** | HAN, CRO, CTAM Administrator | Oversee authorization implementation |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Authorization Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Authorization Committee | Authorization matrix coverage, tier assignments |
| **Authorization Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Authorization implementation summary |

---

## SECTION 6: CTAM VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

A CTAM deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **CTAM adopted** — Matrix is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Matrix Structure** — 7x5 matrix is defined.  
3. **Cell-by-Cell Grants** — All 35 cells are defined.  
4. **Tier Definitions** — All five tiers are defined.  
5. **Authorization Rules** — All four rules are defined.  
6. **Validation** — CTAM validation is operational.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I2, I3, I10** | Constitutional parent. CTAM enforces authority-responsibility separation, auditability, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **CDT-7** | Taxonomy. CTAM uses CDT-7 domains. |
| **CAM-5** | Posture. CTAM provides the matrix for CAM-5 postures. |
| **AICA-5** | Control. CTAM authorizations map to AICA-5 control nodes. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. HAN authorizes tier assignments. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. CTAM assignments are stored in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"CTAM is a 7x5 authorization matrix mapping seven capability domains (Perception, Synthesis, Decision, Interaction, Adaptation, Observability, Constraint) to five escalating tiers—with additive cell rules, categorical change declarations, and three governing rules (observability never lags, constraint sets the ceiling, additive cells)—providing the formal grammar of authorization for the AIGIS stack."*

---


# CAM-5 — 5-TIER CAPABILITY AUTHORIZATION MODEL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Authorization Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Capability Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The 5-Tier Capability Authorization Model (CAM-5) bundles CTAM columns into five named, board-governable capability postures. It provides pre-cleared, authorizable units for governance. CAM-5 enables boards to authorize capability postures, not individual domain-tier combinations.

CAM-5 is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — postures must terminate at human accountability  
- **ICC-10 I2** (Authority-Responsibility Separation) — postures must separate authority from responsibility  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — postures must enable human override  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — postures must resist IPS capture  
- **CDT-7** — capability domains  
- **CTAM** — authorization matrix

**Core insight:** Boards cannot authorize 35 individual domain-tier combinations. CAM-5 bundles them into five coherent, board-governable postures that represent meaningful capability levels.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Capability Posture** | A named bundle of authorizations across all seven domains at a specific tier. |
| **Tier Precondition** | A condition that must be proven before a tier is granted. |
| **Board-Governable Unit** | A capability bundle that can be authorized by a board. |
| **Control Node Owner** | The named owner responsible for a tier's controls. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Capability posture definitions | Authorization execution (covered by ADTEP) |
| Tier preconditions | Authorization policy (covered by governance) |
| Control node owners | Authorization decisions (covered by HAN) |

---

## SECTION 2: THE FIVE TIERS

### 2.1 Tier 1: Observation-Only

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Name** | Observation-Only |
| **Description** | Perception \+ Synthesis only—no Decision, no Interaction, no Adaptation |
| **Key Characteristic** | Tier 1 already carries a live Synthesis grant—the system is producing content, not just retrieving it |
| **Domain Grants** | Perception (Tier 1), Synthesis (Tier 1), Observability (Tier 1\) |
| **Control Node Owner** | CIO |
| **Governance Cost** | Low |
| **Board Friction** | Low |

**Failure Modes Prevented:**

- Unauthorized decision-making  
- Unauthorized interaction  
- Unauthorized adaptation

### 2.2 Tier 2: Recommendation-Only

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Name** | Recommendation-Only |
| **Description** | Decision enters as recommendation only; Decision lineage added to Observability |
| **Key Characteristic** | Pairs Decision's first appearance with Decision lineage in the same tier—a new capability with the visibility needed to check it |
| **Domain Grants** | Perception (Tier 2), Synthesis (Tier 2), Decision (Tier 2), Observability (Tier 2), Constraint (Tier 2\) |
| **Control Node Owner** | CRO |
| **Governance Cost** | Low-Moderate |
| **Board Friction** | Low |

**Failure Modes Prevented:**

- Unaccountable recommendations  
- Recommendations without audit trail  
- Recommendations without basic constraint

### 2.3 Tier 3: Agentic, Tool-Calling

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Name** | Agentic, Tool-Calling |
| **Description** | Autonomous Decision \+ read-only Interaction \+ abort/termination Constraint |
| **Key Characteristic** | Pairs autonomous action and live system contact with the enforced ability to stop it in the same tier—no grant without a stop mechanism |
| **Domain Grants** | Perception (Tier 3), Synthesis (Tier 3), Decision (Tier 3), Interaction (Tier 3), Observability (Tier 3), Constraint (Tier 3\) |
| **Control Node Owner** | CRO \+ CIO \+ COO |
| **Governance Cost** | Moderate-High |
| **Board Friction** | Moderate |

**Failure Modes Prevented:**

- Unstoppable autonomous action  
- Unmonitored agentic behavior  
- Agentic action without human override capability

### 2.4 Tier 4: Learning Agents

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Name** | Learning Agents |
| **Description** | Adaptation \+ write access \+ rollback—the compounding risk tier |
| **Key Characteristic** | Grants self-modification and write-access together—the compounding pairing |
| **Domain Grants** | Perception (Tier 4), Synthesis (Tier 4), Decision (Tier 4), Interaction (Tier 4), Adaptation (Tier 4), Observability (Tier 4), Constraint (Tier 4\) |
| **Control Node Owner** | CRO \+ CIO \+ COO \+ Chief Risk Officer |
| **Governance Cost** | High |
| **Board Friction** | High |

**Failure Modes Prevented:**

- Uncontrolled self-modification  
- Write-access without rollback capability  
- Adaptation without drift detection

### 2.5 Tier 5: Full Autonomy

| Aspect | Description |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Name** | Full Autonomy |
| **Description** | Full capability with preconditions—meta-observability, capability parity, HIS-12 veto, sub-24-hour detection |
| **Key Characteristic** | Gated behind proofs, not capability ceilings |
| **Domain Grants** | Perception (Tier 5), Synthesis (Tier 5), Decision (Tier 5), Interaction (Tier 5), Adaptation (Tier 5), Observability (Tier 5), Constraint (Tier 5\) |
| **Control Node Owner** | Board \+ CRO \+ Evolution System |
| **Governance Cost** | Very High |
| **Board Friction** | Very High |

**Failure Modes Prevented:**

- Unchecked full autonomy  
- Full autonomy without meta-observability  
- Full autonomy without constitutional veto

---

## SECTION 3: TIER PRECONDITIONS

### 3.1 Precondition Structure

| Tier | Preconditions | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | None | N/A |
| **Tier 2** | Decision lineage capability in Observability | Observability tier ≥ Decision tier |
| **Tier 3** | Stop mechanism in Constraint | Constraint tier ≥ Interaction tier |
| **Tier 4** | Drift detection in Observability | Observability tier ≥ Adaptation tier |
| **Tier 5** | Meta-observability, capability parity, HIS-12 veto, sub-24-hour detection | Board verification |

### 3.2 Tier 5 Preconditions (Detailed)

| Precondition | Requirement | Verification Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |

# OPERATIONAL INTERPRETATION LAYER

# RGI-8 — RUNTIME GOVERNANCE INTERPRETATION

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Operational Interpretation Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operational Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Runtime Governance Interpretation (RGI-8) is an operational instrument of CTAM's Execution Mode attribute. It defines whether a declared boundary acts as a checkpoint (Gate) or a continuous influence (Steer). RGI-8 ensures that governance boundaries are interpreted consistently at runtime, preventing interpretive drift that could enable informal power structures.

RGI-8 is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — human override must exist for all processes  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — no interpretive drift that enables IPS  
- **AICA-5 A-N4** (Escalation Paths) — routing of decisions exceeding current authority  
- **ADTEP Role Specification** — constitutional input priority

**Core insight:** Governance is not just about setting boundaries. It is about interpreting them consistently at runtime. RGI-8 ensures that boundaries are enforced as intended, not as reinterpreted by shadow actors or drift.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Gate** | A checkpoint where action is paused for explicit approval before proceeding. |
| **Steer** | A continuous influence that guides action without explicit approval gates. |
| **Execution Mode** | The runtime interpretation of a governance boundary. |
| **Interpretive Drift** | Gradual change in how a boundary is interpreted over time. |
| **I10 Validation Gate** | A gate ensuring that I10 (Anti-Shadow Invariance) is enforced. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Runtime interpretation of governance boundaries | Design-time governance (covered by ICC-10) |
| Gate vs. Steer determination | Operational execution (covered by ADTEP) |
| I10 Validation Gate |  |
| Interpretive drift detection |  |

---

## SECTION 2: GATE VS. STEER

### 2.1 Definitions

| Mode | Definition | Application |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Gate** | A hard stop. Action is paused until explicit approval is received. | High-risk decisions, constitutional boundaries, IPS-related actions |
| **Steer** | A soft influence. Action continues but is guided by governance constraints. | Low-risk decisions, routine operations, monitoring |

### 2.2 Mode Selection Criteria

| Criteria | Gate | Steer |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Risk Level** | High | Low |
| **Constitutional Impact** | High (I1–I10) | Low |
| **IPS Relevance** | IPS-related | Non-IPS-related |
| **Decision Impact** | Material (M1–M3) | Non-material |
| **Human Oversight Required** | Yes | No |
| **Override Capability** | Required | Optional |

### 2.3 Mode Examples

| Boundary Type | Default Mode | Rationale |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **I1: Accountability** | Gate | Human accountability must be explicit |
| **I2: Authority-Responsibility** | Gate | Responsibility cannot be delegated |
| **I3: Auditability** | Steer | Continuous logging and monitoring |
| **I4: Control** | Gate | Human override must be explicit |
| **I5: Structural Compliance** | Gate | Structural changes require approval |
| **I6: Transparency** | Steer | Continuous disclosure management |
| **I7: Continuity** | Steer | Continuous succession monitoring |
| **I8: External Legibility** | Steer | Continuous contestation monitoring |
| **I9: Catastrophic Risk** | Gate | Hard stop for any I9 violation |
| **I10: Anti-Shadow** | Gate | Hard stop for any IPS detection |

---

## SECTION 3: I10 VALIDATION GATE

### 3.1 Definition

The I10 Validation Gate is a mandatory gate ensuring that no action proceeds without verification that it does not enable or result from informal power structures.

### 3.2 Gate Triggers

| Trigger | Condition | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Signal Detection** | IPS signal detected (Level 1+) | Gate activated; investigation required |
| **IPS Level 3+** | IPS confirmed (Level 3+) | Gate activated; Constitutional Suspension |
| **IPS Level 4** | IPS systemic (Level 4+) | Gate activated; full stack review |
| **Suspicious Pattern** | Pattern suggesting IPS | Gate activated; enhanced monitoring |
| **Capture Point Identification** | Capture point detected | Gate activated; capture point closure required |

### 3.3 Gate Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Gate Trigger** | Trigger event detected | O-N6 / ADTEP |
| **2** | **Verification** | Verify I10 compliance | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **Approval** | Approve or reject action | HAN |
| **4** | **Escalation** | Escalate if rejection | HAN → Board |
| **5** | **IMP Recording** | Record gate action | IMP |

---

## SECTION 4: INTERPRETIVE DRIFT DETECTION

### 4.1 Drift Types

| Drift Type | Definition | Detection Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Mode Drift** | Gate becomes Steer over time | Compare mode assignments over time |
| **Threshold Drift** | Thresholds change over time | Compare threshold applications over time |
| **Interpretive Drift** | Meaning of boundary changes over time | Compare interpretations over time |
| **IPS Drift** | Boundaries interpreted to enable IPS | Correlate interpretations with IPS signals |

### 4.2 Drift Detection Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Mode Inconsistency** | Similar boundaries with different modes | \>20% inconsistency |
| **Threshold Inconsistency** | Similar thresholds applied differently | \>25% inconsistency |
| **Interpretive Inconsistency** | Similar boundaries interpreted differently | \>20% inconsistency |
| **IPS Correlation** | Interpretations correlate with IPS signals | Significant correlation |

### 4.3 Drift Response

| Severity | Response | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1: Suspicion** | Flag for observation | RGI-8 Administrator |
| **Level 2: Indication** | Enhanced monitoring | RGI-8 Administrator |
| **Level 3: Confirmed** | Correction required | HAN |
| **Level 4: Systemic** | Full review required | HAN \+ Board |

---

## SECTION 5: RGI-8 GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **RGI-8 Committee** | RGI-8 Administrator, HAN, CRO | Oversee RGI-8 implementation |
| **Interpretive Review Board** | HAN, External reviewers | Review interpretive drift cases |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **RGI-8 Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, RGI-8 Committee | Mode assignments, drift detection |
| **RGI-8 Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Interpretive drift incidents |

---

## SECTION 6: RGI-8 VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

An RGI-8 deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **RGI-8 Framework adopted** — RGI-8 is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Gate vs. Steer** — Gate vs. Steer mode selection is operational.  
3. **I10 Validation Gate** — I10 Validation Gate is operational.  
4. **Interpretive Drift Detection** — Interpretive drift detection is operational.  
5. **Mode Definitions** — Mode definitions are documented.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I4, I10** | Constitutional parent. RGI-8 enforces control and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. RGI-8 includes I10 Validation Gate for IPS detection. |
| **AICA-5 A-N4** | Control architecture. RGI-8 uses A-N4 escalation paths. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. RGI-8 gates are enforced by ADTEP. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. RGI-8 records interpretations in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"RGI-8 defines runtime governance interpretation through Gate (explicit approval) and Steer (continuous influence) modes—with an I10 Validation Gate ensuring Anti-Shadow enforcement, interpretive drift detection, and full IMP recording."*

---



# CAD-7 — COALITION ACCOUNTABILITY DECLARATION

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Operational Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operational Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Coalition Accountability Declaration (CAD-7) is an operational instrument for transient multi-agent coalitions, providing a lightweight accountability declaration for dynamic collaborations. It governs what happens when multiple individually-authorized agents compose—through delegation, negotiation, or joint task execution—into a coalition that becomes the actual decision-maker.

CAD-7 answers: *What happens when multiple individually-authorized agents form a coalition that becomes the actual decision-maker, and how is that coalition held accountable?*

CAD-7 is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — human remains ultimately accountable for all actions  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — all material actions must be reconstructable  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — no informal power structure may determine outcomes  
- **AICA-5 Ac-N2** (Responsibility Attribution) — responsibility must be attributed to human anchors  
- **CFL-F** (Federation Logic) — cross-entity coordination requirements

**Core insight:** Individual authorization does not transitively confer coalition legitimacy, the same way a set of valid employment contracts does not itself constitute a company. A company is legitimate because an entity-level charter declares it an accountable unit, separate from and prior to its members. Multi-agent coalitions are missing that second layer. CAD-7 is that layer—deliberately lighter than a full ICC-10 charter, since a coalition can form and dissolve inside a single task.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Coalition** | A group of two or more AI agents that compose through delegation, negotiation, or joint task execution to produce a collective decision or outcome. |
| **Coalition Principal** | A named, accountable human who declares and owns the coalition's accountability. |
| **Composability Boundary** | What the coalition is jointly authorized to produce, stated separately from the union of members' individual grants. |
| **Coalition Template** | A pre-authorized coalition structure declared in advance by a HAN for recurring patterns. |
| **Cross-Entity Coalition** | A coalition whose members span different SPVs within the same federation. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Multi-agent coalitions forming within a single task | Permanent organizational structures (governed by charters) |
| Coalition Principal declaration | Individual agent authorization (governed by CDT-7/CTAM) |
| Composability Boundary definition | Cross-federation coordination (governed by CFL-F) |
| Coalition dissolution and reconstitution |  |
| Cross-entity coalition governance |  |

---

## SECTION 2: THE SEVEN COMPONENTS

### 2.1 Component 1: Coalition Trigger Definition

**Statement:** CAD-7 activates only above a threshold: a joint decision produced by multiple agents, or an authority delegation from one agent to another. Ordinary information exchange between agents does not trigger it.

**Threshold:** Joint decision-making or authority delegation, not information exchange.

**Violation:** Coalition forms without meeting trigger definition.

**Enforcement:** CAD-7 does not apply to pure information exchange. Trigger verification required before coalition governance applies.

---

### 2.2 Component 2: Coalition Principal Declaration

**Statement:** A named, accountable human—declared before the coalition acts, never left implicit. Inherited explicitly from a member's HAN, or newly designated.

**Requirement:** Every coalition must have a declared Principal before any joint action. "Everyone is responsible" at the coalition level is the same undeclared-ownership failure this corpus opened with; a coalition requires an owner, not a diffusion of its members' owners.

**Violation:** Coalition acts without a declared Principal.

**Enforcement:** Joint action halted until Principal declared. Escalation Flag triggered.

---

### 2.3 Component 3: Composability Boundary

**Statement:** What the coalition is jointly authorized to produce, stated separately from the union of members' individual CDT-7/CTAM grants—composition is not additive.

**Behavior:** This boundary behaves as a Gate: joint activity exceeding it requires a new declaration event, not silent expansion.

**Violation:** Coalition exceeds declared Composability Boundary.

**Enforcement:** Joint action halted until boundary re-declared. Escalation Flag triggered.

---

### 2.4 Component 4: Dissolution and Reconstitution Rule

**Statement:** Defines when declared accountability lapses (task completion, timeout, explicit end) and requires re-declaration on membership change.

**Rule:** An agent leaving mid-task is itself a trigger for re-declaration, not something the coalition quietly absorbs.

**Violation:** Coalition continues without re-declaration after membership change or dissolution trigger.

**Enforcement:** Joint action halted until re-declared. Escalation Flag triggered.

---

### 2.5 Component 5: Lightweight Compilation Path

**Statement:** Pre-authorized Coalition Templates, declared in advance by a HAN for recurring patterns, so routine coalitions reference a template ID instead of compiling a live declaration each time.

**Rule:** Novel, non-templated coalitions require a live but minimal Principal Declaration at formation—not full CFL-G/CFL-V compilation, and not silent formation either.

**Violation:** Novel coalition forms without declaration.

**Enforcement:** Joint action halted until declaration completed.

---

### 2.6 Component 6: Cross-Entity Coalition Clause

**Statement:** When coalition members span different SPVs, this component determines whether CFL-F's Cross-Entity Coordination Clause is triggered at all, and if so, defers to it entirely without reinterpreting it.

**Three Cases:**

| Case | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Case 1** | Same federation, different SPVs, same HAN | Proceeds under CAD-7's Principal Declaration (shared HAN) unless the action "directly implicates the function of another SPV"—a high bar concerning mandate, resources, or structural role, not ordinary cross-functional collaboration. If threshold met, CFL-F applies in full. |
| **Case 2** | Same federation, different HANs | Fails closed. Different HANs mean different accountability anchors; coalition cannot proceed without a newly declared Principal. |
| **Case 3** | Different federations | Fails closed at federation level (CFL-F). Different federations mean different charters, different HANs, different ICC-10 interpretations. |

**Critical Rule:** No new federation logic is introduced. CFL-F's text is unmodified. CAD-7's contribution is precisely scoping *when* CFL-F's own threshold is met.

---

### 2.7 Component 7: Reconstructability Requirement

**Statement:** After any coalition-level incident: who declared the coalition's Principal, under what Composability Boundary, and when.

**Rule:** If the trace mechanism is unavailable, the coalition's joint capability fails closed—individual agents continue under their own grants, but collective decision-making halts until trace is restored.

**Violation:** Trace mechanism unavailable during coalition incident.

**Enforcement:** Coalition halts until trace restored. Individual agents continue under individual grants.

---

## SECTION 3: CAD-7 GOVERNANCE

### 3.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **CAD-7 Committee** | CAD-7 Administrator, HAN, CRO | Oversee CAD-7 implementation |
| **Coalition Review Board** | HAN, External reviewers | Review coalition incidents |

### 3.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **CAD-7 Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, CAD-7 Committee | Coalition formations, incidents |
| **CAD-7 Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Coalition governance incidents |

---

## SECTION 4: CAD-7 VALIDATION

### 4.1 Validation Requirements

A CAD-7 deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **CAD-7 Framework adopted** — CAD-7 is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Coalition Trigger Definition** — Trigger definition is operational.  
3. **Coalition Principal Declaration** — Principal declaration is operational.  
4. **Composability Boundary** — Composability Boundary is operational.  
5. **Dissolution and Reconstitution** — Dissolution and reconstitution are operational.  
6. **Lightweight Compilation Path** — Template and live declaration paths are operational.  
7. **Cross-Entity Clause** — Cross-Entity Coalition Clause is operational.  
8. **Reconstructability** — Reconstructability requirement is operational.

---

## SECTION 5: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I3, I10** | Constitutional parent. CAD-7 enforces accountability, auditability, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. CAD-7 prevents IPS capture of coalitions. |
| **CDT-7/CTAM** | Individual authorization. CAD-7 governs coalition authorization. |
| **RGI-8** | Runtime interpretation. CAD-7 uses same fail-closed logic as RGI-8. |
| **CFL-F** | Federation logic. CAD-7 scopes when CFL-F's threshold is met. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. CAD-7 requires HAN-declared Principal. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. CAD-7 records coalition activity in IMP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"CAD-7 is a seven-component operational instrument governing multi-agent coalitions—defining Coalition Trigger, Principal Declaration, Composability Boundary, Dissolution and Reconstitution, Lightweight Compilation Path, Cross-Entity Clause, and Reconstructability—ensuring transient coalitions are accountable, traceable, and resistant to informal power structures without requiring full ICC-10 charter compilation."*

---



# ENGAGEMENT & TRUST LAYER

# CEF — CLIENT ENGAGEMENT FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Engagement Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Client Engagement Framework (CEF) governs the full client engagement lifecycle. It defines six stages of engagement, from opportunity identification through follow-up, with clear governance requirements at each stage. CEF ensures that all client engagements are constitutionally governed, transparent, and accountable.

CEF is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — engagements must terminate at human accountability  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — engagements must be transparent to stakeholders  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — engagements must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — engagements must resist IPS capture  
- **HAN Authorization Authority** — HAN must authorize engagements  
- **EAF** (Engagement Authority Framework) — trust tiers for engagements

**Core insight:** Client engagements are the primary vector for external interaction and the most vulnerable point of IPS infiltration. They must be governed with full constitutional awareness from initiation to conclusion.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Client Engagement** | Any formal relationship between AIGIS and a client for the provision of services. |
| **Engagement Lifecycle** | The six stages from opportunity identification through follow-up. |
| **Client IPS Risk Level** | Classification of client IPS exposure (0-4 per CEF-IPS). |
| **Trust Tier** | Classification of client trustworthiness (1-5 per EAF). |
| **Material Engagement** | Any engagement that produces material institutional actions per ICC-10 M1–M3. |

### 1.2 The Six Engagement Stages

| Stage | Name | Description |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Stage 1** | Opportunity Identification | Initial identification and screening of potential engagements |
| **Stage 2** | Proposal Development | Development of proposal with IPS due diligence |
| **Stage 3** | Mandate Close | Formalization of engagement with IPS mandate clauses |
| **Stage 4** | Service Delivery | Execution of engagement with IPS monitoring |
| **Stage 5** | Completion | Completion and post-engagement IPS review |
| **Stage 6** | Follow-up | Post-engagement IPS recurrence monitoring |

---

## SECTION 2: IPS-AWARE ENGAGEMENT CLASSIFICATION

### 2.1 Client IPS Risk Level

Every client engagement must be classified by IPS Risk Level (per CEF-IPS):

| Level | Name | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0** | IPS-Negligible | No known IPS; low risk | Proceed normally |
| **Level 1** | IPS-Sensitive | Possible IPS exposure | Monitor |
| **Level 2** | IPS-Exposed | Known IPS involvement | Enhanced due diligence |
| **Level 3** | IPS-Captured | Controlled by IPS | HAN review required |
| **Level 4** | IPS-Systemic | Client is an IPS actor | Engagement declined |

### 2.2 Mandatory CEF-IPS Integration

**Rule:** CEF-IPS is a mandatory extension for all client engagements. No engagement may proceed without IPS classification and monitoring.

**Violation:** Engagement proceeds without IPS classification.

**Enforcement:** CEF Stage 1 requires IPS screening. CEF Stage 2 requires enhanced due diligence for Level 1+.

---

## SECTION 3: ENGAGEMENT LIFECYCLE

### 3.1 Stage 1: Opportunity Identification

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Initial Client Identification** | Identify potential client | Business Development |
| **2** | **Basic IPS Screening** | Screen client against IPS indicators | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **IPS Risk Flagging** | Flag high-risk clients | IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **Pursuit Decision** | Determine if engagement should be pursued | HAN \+ Business Development |

### 3.2 Stage 2: Proposal Development

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Client IPS Mapping** | Map client's IPS network | IPS Analysis Team |
| **2** | **Client IPS Impact Assessment** | Assess IPS impact on engagement | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **Engagement IPS Risk Assessment** | Assess IPS risks to AIGIS | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **IPS Mitigation Planning** | Develop IPS mitigation plan | IPS Response Team |
| **5** | **Proposal IPS Content** | Include IPS considerations in proposal | Business Development |

### 3.3 Stage 3: Mandate Close

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Client IPS Disclosure Review** | Review client IPS disclosures | IPS Analysis Team |
| **2** | **Client IPS Cooperation Agreement** | Confirm IPS cooperation | Legal \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **Client IPS Termination Acknowledgment** | Confirm IPS termination rights | Legal |
| **4** | **Mandate IPS Review** | Review mandate for IPS compliance | HAN |
| **5** | **HAN IPS Confirmation** | HAN confirms mandate IPS compliance | HAN |

### 3.4 Stage 4: Service Delivery

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **IPS Signal Monitoring** | Monitor for IPS signals in client interactions | O-N6 \+ Agent |
| **2** | **IPS Client Assessment** | Monthly client IPS assessment | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **IPS Engagement Review** | Quarterly engagement IPS review | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **IPS Escalation** | Escalate if Level 3+ detected | IPS Analysis Team → HAN |

### 3.5 Stage 5: Completion

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **IPS Impact Assessment** | Assess IPS impact on engagement | IPS Analysis Team |
| **2** | **IPS Lessons Learned** | Document IPS lessons learned | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **IPS Client Handoff** | Provide IPS information to client | IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **IPS Closure Report** | Generate IPS closure report | IPS Analysis Team → HAN |

### 3.6 Stage 6: Follow-up

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Client IPS Recurrence Monitoring** | Monitor for IPS recurrence | IPS Analysis Team |
| **2** | **Client IPS Impact Follow-up** | Assess IPS impact on client | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **IPS Framework Update** | Update based on follow-up findings | HAN \+ Board IPS Committee |

---

## SECTION 4: IPS MANDATE CLAUSES

### 4.1 Mandatory IPS Clauses

| Clause | Purpose | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Disclosure Clause** | Client must disclose known IPS | Contractual |
| **IPS Cooperation Clause** | Client must cooperate with IPS investigation | Contractual |
| **IPS Termination Clause** | AIGIS may terminate for IPS violation | Contractual |
| **IPS Reporting Clause** | IPS reports shared with client | Contractual |
| **IPS Confidentiality Clause** | IPS information handled confidentially | Contractual |

### 4.2 IPS Mandate Clause Template

> *"The Client acknowledges and agrees that the AIGIS institution maintains a constitutional commitment to detecting, reporting, and mitigating informal power structures (IPS) in all engagements. The Client agrees to: (a) disclose any known IPS that may affect the engagement; (b) cooperate fully with any IPS investigation conducted by AIGIS; (c) accept that AIGIS may terminate the engagement if IPS is detected that threatens institutional integrity; and (d) accept that IPS-related findings may be disclosed to regulators as required by law. The Client acknowledges that failure to comply with these obligations constitutes a material breach of this mandate."*

---

## SECTION 5: CEF GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **CEF Committee** | CEF Administrator, IPS Analysis Team, HAN | Oversee CEF implementation |
| **Client IPS Assessment Team** | IPS Analysis Team, Business Development, Legal | Assess client IPS risks |
| **Client IPS Escalation Team** | HAN, IPS Response Team, Board IPS Committee | Respond to client IPS incidents |

### 5.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |

| **Client IPS Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, CEF Committee | Client IPS risk levels, incidents, mitigation |
| **Client IPS Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board IPS Committee | Full analysis of client IPS incidents |
| **CEF Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Summary of CEF implementation |

A CEF deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **CEF Framework adopted** — CEF is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **CEF-IPS Integration** — CEF-IPS is mandatory for all engagements.  
3. **IPS Screening Protocol** — Stage 1 includes IPS screening.  
4. **Enhanced IPS Due Diligence** — Stage 2 includes enhanced due diligence.  
5. **IPS Mandate Requirements** — Stage 3 includes IPS mandate clauses.  
6. **IPS Monitoring** — Stage 4 includes IPS monitoring.  
7. **Post-Engagement IPS Review** — Stage 5 includes IPS impact assessment.  
8. **Post-Engagement IPS Monitoring** — Stage 6 includes IPS recurrence monitoring.

# CEF-IPS — SHADOW-AWARE CLIENT ENGAGEMENT FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Engagement Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** CEF (Client Engagement Framework)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Shadow-Aware CEF extends the Client Engagement Framework to address the risks posed by Informal Power Structures (IPS) in client relationships. It governs how client engagements are initiated, deepened, and concluded in a context where shadow systems may attempt to capture, exploit, or weaponize the engagement.

This instrument answers: *How does AIGIS manage client engagements to prevent IPS capture, ensure client-side IPS is detected and mitigated, and maintain institutional integrity throughout the engagement lifecycle?*

CEF-IPS is derived from:

- **IPS Framework v1.0** (IPS detection signals, severity levels, mitigation protocols)  
- **ICC-10 I10 — Anti-Shadow Invariance** (no informal power structure may determine institutional outcomes)  
- **AICA-5 O-N6 — Shadow Network Detection** (primary IPS detection node)  
- **AWOF-IPS** (IPS-aware AI agent governance)  
- **CEF** (6-stage engagement lifecycle)

**Core insight:** Client engagements are the most vulnerable point of IPS infiltration. Shadow systems can use client relationships to gain access, extract value, or create dependencies. Conversely, clients themselves may be captured by IPS, creating risks for the AIGIS institution. The engagement must be governed with full IPS awareness from initiation to conclusion.

---

## SECTION 1: IPS-AWARE ENGAGEMENT CLASSIFICATION

### 1.1 Extended Engagement Classification

The CEF engagement classification is extended with an **IPS Risk Dimension**:

| Dimension | Values | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Engagement Type** | (Unchanged) | Consulting, Audit, Implementation, etc. |
| **Engagement Scale** | (Unchanged) | Tier 1-5 (based on scope, authority, impact) |
| **Client Type** | (Unchanged) | Government, Corporate, NGO, Individual, etc. |
| **IPS Risk Level** | **Level 0: IPS-Negligible** | Client has no known IPS; engagement low-risk |
|  | **Level 1: IPS-Sensitive** | Client may be exposed to IPS; monitoring required |
|  | **Level 2: IPS-Exposed** | Client has known IPS involvement; enhanced due diligence |
|  | **Level 3: IPS-Captured** | Client is controlled by IPS; engagement requires escalation |
|  | **Level 4: IPS-Systemic** | Client is an IPS actor; engagement must be terminated |

### 1.2 Mandatory IPS Assessment by Engagement Stage

| Engagement Stage | IPS Assessment Required | Justification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Stage 1: Opportunity Identification** | Level 0: Basic screening | Initial IPS risk filtering |
| **Stage 2: Proposal Development** | Level 1: Enhanced due diligence | IPS risk assessment before commitment |
| **Stage 3: Mandate Close** | Level 2: Deep IPS analysis | Full IPS assessment before engagement begins |
| **Stage 4: Service Delivery** | Level 3: Continuous monitoring | IPS detection throughout engagement |
| **Stage 5: Completion** | Level 2: Post-engagement review | IPS impact assessment after engagement |
| **Stage 6: Follow-up** | Level 1: Periodic review | IPS recurrence monitoring |

## SECTION 2: IPS-AWARE CLIENT ENGAGEMENT LIFECYCLE

### 2.1 Stage 1: Opportunity Identification (IPS Extended)

#### 2.1.1 IPS Screening Protocol

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Initial Client Identification** | Identify if client has known IPS involvement | Business Development |
| **2** | **Basic IPS Screening** | Screen client against IPS indicators (public sources) | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **IPS Risk Flagging** | Flag high-risk clients for enhanced due diligence | IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **Pursuit Decision** | Determine if engagement should be pursued | HAN \+ Business Development |

#### 2.1.2 IPS Screening Indicators

| Indicator | Red Flag | Green Flag |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Public IPS Associations** | Client associated with known IPS actors | No known IPS associations |
| **IPS Transparency** | Client's governance structure is opaque | Client's governance is transparent and auditable |
| **IPS Accountability** | Client lacks clear accountability anchors | Client has clear accountability structures |
| **IPS Reputation** | Client has known IPS reputation | Client has integrity reputation |
| **IPS Government Ties** | Client has unusual government connections | Client has normal government interactions |

#### 2.1.3 IPS Screening Decision Matrix

| IPS Screening Result | Action | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0: IPS-Negligible** | Proceed with normal engagement | Business Development |
| **Level 1: IPS-Sensitive** | Proceed with enhanced due diligence (Stage 2\) | Business Development \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **Level 2: IPS-Exposed** | Proceed only with HAN approval and IPS mitigation plan | HAN \+ Business Development |
| **Level 3: IPS-Captured** | Pending HAN and Board review; engagement may be declined | HAN \+ Board IPS Committee |
| **Level 4: IPS-Systemic** | Engagement automatically declined; escalate to regulators | HAN \+ Board IPS Committee |

---

### 2.2 Stage 2: Proposal Development (IPS Extended)

#### 2.2.1 Enhanced IPS Due Diligence

| Step | Activity | IPS Consideration | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Client IPS Mapping** | Map client's IPS network and capture points | IPS Analysis Team |
| **2** | **Client IPS Impact Assessment** | Assess client IPS impact on engagement | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **Engagement IPS Risk Assessment** | Assess IPS risks to AIGIS institution | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **IPS Mitigation Planning** | Develop IPS mitigation plan for engagement | IPS Response Team |
| **5** | **Proposal IPS Content** | Include IPS considerations in proposal | Business Development \+ IPS Analysis Team |

#### 2.2.2 IPS Risk Assessment Factors

| Factor | Assessment Criteria | Weight |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Client IPS Severity** | Level 0-4 of client IPS | 30% |
| **Engagement IPS Exposure** | How much engagement exposes AIGIS to IPS | 25% |
| **IPS Mitigation Feasibility** | How effectively IPS can be mitigated | 20% |
| **AIGIS IPS Resilience** | How resilient AIGIS is to IPS infiltration | 15% |
| **External IPS Risk** | Regulatory, reputational, or legal risks | 10% |

#### 2.2.3 Proposal IPS Content Requirements

| Requirement | Content | Purpose |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Risk Disclosure** | Clearly disclose IPS risks to client | Transparency |
| **IPS Mitigation Plan** | Outline IPS mitigation measures | Accountability |
| **IPS Monitoring Commitment** | Commit to IPS monitoring throughout engagement | Assurance |
| **IPS Escalation Protocol** | Explain IPS escalation procedures | Clarity |
| **IPS Termination Rights** | Reserve right to terminate due to IPS | Protection |

| **IPS Termination Clause** | AIGIS may terminate for IPS violation | Contractual |
| **IPS Reporting Requirement** | IPS reports shared with client | Operational |

#### 2.3.2 IPS Mandate Verification

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **2** | **Client IPS Cooperation Agreement** | Confirm client agrees to IPS cooperation |
| **3** | **Client IPS Termination Acknowledgment** | Confirm client acknowledges IPS termination rights |
| **4** | **Mandate IPS Review** | Review mandate for IPS compliance |
| **5** | **HAN IPS Confirmation** | HAN confirms mandate IPS compliance |

#### 2.3.3 IPS Mandate Clause Template

> *"The Client acknowledges and agrees that the AIGIS institution maintains a constitutional commitment to detecting, reporting, and mitigating informal power structures (IPS) in all engagements. The Client agrees to: (a) disclose any known IPS that may affect the engagement; (b) cooperate fully with any IPS investigation conducted by AIGIS; (c) accept that AIGIS may terminate the engagement if IPS is detected that threatens institutional integrity; and (d) accept that IPS-related findings may be disclosed to regulators as required by law. The Client acknowledges that failure to comply with these obligations constitutes a material breach of this mandate."*

## SECTION 5: CEF-IPS VALIDATION

### 5.1 Validation Requirements

A CEF deployment is IPS-compliant if and only if:

1. **CEF-IPS Framework adopted** — CEF-IPS v1.0+ is integrated into CEF.  
2. **Client IPS Classification** — All clients have IPS Risk Level assigned (0–4).  
3. **IPS Screening Protocol** — Stage 1 includes basic IPS screening.  
4. **Enhanced IPS Due Diligence** — Stage 2 includes enhanced due diligence.  
5. **IPS Mandate Requirements** — Stage 3 includes IPS mandate clauses.  
6. **IPS Monitoring During Service Delivery** — Stage 4 includes continuous IPS monitoring.  
7. **Post-Engagement IPS Review** — Stage 5 includes IPS impact assessment.  
8. **Post-Engagement IPS Monitoring** — Stage 6 includes IPS recurrence monitoring.  
9. **CEF-IPS Governance** — Governance bodies established and operational.

### 5.2 CFL-V Extension (CEF-IPS)

**A derived charter C is valid iff:**

C satisfies CEF-IPS requirements 1–9

and CEF-IPS governance bodies are operational

and all clients have IPS Risk Level assigned

and IPS screening, due diligence, monitoring, and review protocols are operational

# EAF — ENGAGEMENT AUTHORITY FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Engagement Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Engagement Authority Framework (EAF) defines five trust tiers that establish the conditions under which client engagements are initiated, deepened, and concluded. It answers: *Under what trust conditions may client engagements proceed, and how does IPS risk affect trust determination?*

EAF is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — trust must terminate at human accountability  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — trust conditions must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — IPS risk must affect trust determination  
- **CEF** (Client Engagement Framework) — engagement lifecycle governance  
- **CEF-IPS** (Shadow-Aware CEF) — IPS risk classification

**Core insight:** Trust is not binary. It is a spectrum that must be earned, verified, and continuously monitored. IPS risk is a critical factor in trust determination—a client with high IPS exposure cannot be granted the same trust as a client with none.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Trust Tier** | A classification level determining the depth and scope of engagement permitted. |
| **IPS Risk Level** | Classification of client IPS exposure (0-4 per CEF-IPS). |
| **Trust Condition** | A requirement that must be met for engagement at a given trust tier. |
| **Trust Elevation** | Movement to a higher trust tier. |
| **Trust Descent** | Movement to a lower trust tier. |
| **Trust Termination** | Ending an engagement due to trust violation. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Trust tier classification for client engagements | Internal trust relationships |
| Trust elevation and descent protocols | Personal trust relationships |
| IPS risk as a trust factor | Operational trust (covered by AICA-5) |
| Trust termination conditions |  |

---

## SECTION 2: THE FIVE TRUST TIERS

### 2.1 Trust Tier Definitions

| Tier | Name | Description | IPS Risk Requirement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | Public | Low-trust, low-risk engagements | Level 0 only |
| **Tier 2** | Verified | Verified clients with moderate trust | Level 0-1 |
| **Tier 3** | Certified | Certified clients with high trust | Level 0-1 |
| **Tier 4** | Strategic | Strategic partners with deep trust | Level 0-2 |
| **Tier 5** | Institutional | Institutional partners with shared governance | Level 0-2 |

### 2.2 Trust Tier Requirements

| Requirement | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 | Tier 4 | Tier 5 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Disclosure** | No | Basic | Full | Full \+ Continuous | Integrated |
| **IPS Cooperation** | No | Basic | Full | Full \+ Proactive | Integrated |
| **IPS Termination Rights** | AIGIS may terminate | AIGIS may terminate | Mutual | Mutual | Shared |
| **IPS Reporting** | No | Quarterly | Monthly | Weekly | Real-time |
| **IPS Verification Frequency** | Initiation only | Quarterly | Bi-annually | Monthly | Real-time |
| **HAN Approval** | No | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Board Approval** | No | No | No | Yes | Yes |

### 2.3 Trust Tier Permissions

| Permission | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 | Tier 4 | Tier 5 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Standard Engagement** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **High-Risk Engagement** | No | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Strategic Partnership** | No | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **Shared Governance** | No | No | No | No | Yes |
| **Access to Sensitive Data** | No | No | Limited | Full | Full |
| **Access to IPS Data** | No | No | Limited | Full | Full |

---

## SECTION 3: IPS RISK AS TRUST FACTOR

### 3.1 IPS Risk Impact on Trust

| IPS Risk Level | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 | Tier 4 | Tier 5 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0: IPS-Negligible** | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed |
| **Level 1: IPS-Sensitive** | ❌ Not Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed |
| **Level 2: IPS-Exposed** | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed | ✅ Allowed |
| **Level 3: IPS-Captured** | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed |
| **Level 4: IPS-Systemic** | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed | ❌ Not Allowed |

### 3.2 IPS Risk Mitigation for Trust Elevation

| IPS Risk Level | Mitigation Required for Elevation |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1: IPS-Sensitive** | Enhanced monitoring; quarterly IPS review |
| **Level 2: IPS-Exposed** | Full IPS due diligence; enhanced monitoring; monthly IPS review; HAN approval required |

---

## SECTION 4: TRUST ELEVATION PROTOCOL

### 4.1 Elevation Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Client Request** | Client must formally request elevation | Written request |
| **IPS Assessment** | Full IPS assessment must be completed | IPS Analysis Team report |
| **Trust Verification** | Client must satisfy trust tier requirements | EAF checklist |
| **HAN Approval** | HAN must approve elevation | HAN authorization |
| **Board Approval** | Board must approve elevation (Tier 4-5) | Board resolution |

### 4.2 Elevation Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Client Request** | Client requests trust elevation | Client |
| **2** | **IPS Assessment** | Complete full IPS assessment | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **Trust Verification** | Verify trust tier requirements met | EAF Administrator |
| **4** | **HAN Review** | HAN reviews and approves | HAN |
| **5** | **Board Review** | Board reviews and approves (if Tier 4-5) | Board |
| **6** | **Elevation Notification** | Notify client of elevation | EAF Administrator |
| **7** | **IMP Recording** | Record elevation in IMP | IMP |

## SECTION 5: TRUST DESCENT PROTOCOL

### 5.1 Descent Triggers

| Trigger | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Risk Increase** | Client IPS risk level increases | Descent to appropriate tier |
| **Trust Violation** | Client violates trust conditions | Descent or termination |
| **IPS Detection** | IPS detected in client engagement | Descent pending investigation |
| **Regulatory Action** | Regulatory action against client | Descent or termination |
| **Material Breach** | Material breach of engagement terms | Descent or termination |

### 5.2 Descent Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Descent Trigger** | Trigger event detected | EAF Administrator |
| **2** | **Descent Assessment** | Assess appropriate descent level | EAF Administrator \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **HAN Review** | HAN reviews and approves | HAN |
| **4** | **Client Notification** | Notify client of descent | EAF Administrator |
| **5** | **Engagement Adjustment** | Adjust engagement to new trust tier | Business Development |
| **6** | **IMP Recording** | Record descent in IMP | IMP |

---

## SECTION 6: TRUST TERMINATION PROTOCOL

### 6.1 Termination Triggers

| Trigger | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Level 3+** | Client IPS-captured or systemic | Immediate termination |
| **Trust Violation** | Client violates trust conditions | Termination pending review |
| **Material Breach** | Material breach of engagement terms | Termination pending review |
| **IPS Complicity** | Client complicit in IPS | Immediate termination |

### 6.2 Termination Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Termination Trigger** | Trigger event detected | EAF Administrator |
| **2** | **Termination Assessment** | Assess termination justification | EAF Administrator \+ IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **HAN Review** | HAN reviews and approves | HAN |
| **4** | **Board Review** | Board reviews and approves (if Tier 3+) | Board |
| **5** | **Client Notification** | Notify client of termination | EAF Administrator |
| **6** | **Engagement Closure** | Close engagement per CEF Stage 5 | Business Development |
| **7** | **IMP Recording** | Record termination in IMP | IMP |
| **8** | **ERDP Disclosure** | Disclose termination if IPS-related | ERDP Administrator |

---

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **EAF Committee** | EAF Administrator, IPS Analysis Team, HAN | Oversee EAF implementation; review trust determinations |
| **Trust Review Board** | HAN, Board, External Auditors | Review trust elevation and descent decisions |
| **Client IPS Assessment Team** | IPS Analysis Team, Business Development, Legal | Assess client IPS risks for trust determination |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **Trust Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Trust termination incidents |
| **EAF Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Summary of EAF implementation |

## SECTION 8: EAF VALIDATION

An EAF deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **EAF Framework adopted** — EAF is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Five Trust Tiers** — All five trust tiers are defined and operational.  
3. **IPS Risk Integration** — IPS risk is a factor in trust determination.  
4. **Elevation Protocol** — Trust elevation protocol is operational.  
5. **Descent Protocol** — Trust descent protocol is operational.  
6. **Termination Protocol** — Trust termination protocol is operational.  
7. **IPS Risk Matrix** — IPS risk impact on trust is documented.

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |

| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. EAF integrates IPS risk into trust determination. |

# INSTITUTIONAL MEMORY & CAPITAL LAYER


# IMP — INSTITUTIONAL MEMORY PROTOCOL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Operational/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Institutional Memory Protocol (IMP) governs the creation, structure, provenance, storage, retrieval, and retirement of institutional state objects. It ensures that every material institutional action is recorded with immutable provenance, enabling reconstruction, audit, and accountability.

IMP is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — all material actions must be reconstructable post-hoc  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — information must be classifiable into defined disclosure tiers  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — no informal power structure may operate without traceability  
- **AICA-5 Ac-N1** (Decision Lineage) — immutable causal record of every decision  
- **ADTEP Pre-Delivery Log Entry** — log precedes action

**Core insight:** Memory is not neutral. What is recorded, how it is recorded, and who can access it determines what can be audited, contested, and learned. IMP ensures that institutional memory is complete, immutable, and accessible to those with legitimate need.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Institutional State Object** | Any recorded artifact that captures institutional action, decision, or state. |
| **Provenance** | The complete history of an object: creation, modification, access, and retirement. |
| **Canonical Object** | An object that has been verified and accepted as the authoritative version. |
| **Immutable Record** | A record that cannot be altered or deleted after creation. |
| **Object Type** | A declared classification of institutional state objects. |
| **Retention Policy** | The rules governing how long objects are retained and when they are retired. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Creation and storage of institutional state objects | Physical asset management |
| Provenance tracking | Financial transactions (covered by CDF) |
| Retrieval and access control | Personal data (covered by privacy policy) |
| Retention and retirement |  |
| IPS state objects |  |

---

## SECTION 2: CANONICAL OBJECT TYPES

### 2.1 Object Type Registry

| Object Type | Description | Examples | Retention Period |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **GO (Governance Object)** | Formal governance instruments and charters | ICC-10, HOF, HAN, AICA-5 | Permanent |
| **GAO (Governance Action Object)** | Record of governance actions | Certifications, suspensions, opinions | 10 years |
| **XOO (Exception/Override Object)** | Record of exceptions and overrides | I9 violations, Escalation Flags | 10 years |
| **OEO (Outcome Event Object)** | Record of institutional outcomes | Decisions, outputs, contestations | 7 years |
| **DLO (Delegation Log Object)** | Record of delegated authority | Delegation grants, revocations | 7 years |
| **IPS-O (IPS State Object)** | Record of IPS detection and analysis | IPS signals, severity, mitigation plans | 10 years |
| **IPS-I (IPS Incident Object)** | Record of confirmed IPS incidents | Level 3+ IPS incidents | Permanent |
| **ADA (Audit Artifact Object)** | Record of audit results | AOBA audits, ABA audits | 7 years |
| **CAO (Capital Allocation Object)** | Record of capital deployment | CDF deployments, COF allocations | 10 years |

### 2.2 Object Schema Requirements

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **object\_id** | String | Yes | Unique object identifier |
| **object\_type** | Enum | Yes | Object type from registry |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Object creation timestamp |
| **provenance\_chain** | Array | Yes | Complete provenance chain |
| **content\_hash** | String | Yes | Cryptographic hash of content |
| **content** | Object | Yes | Object content |
| **access\_log** | Array | Yes | Record of all access events |
| **retention\_policy** | String | Yes | Retention policy applied |
| **disclosure\_tier** | Enum | Yes | ERDP disclosure tier |

---

## SECTION 3: PROVENANCE REQUIREMENTS

### 3.1 Provenance Chain Components

| Component | Description | Required For |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Creation Record** | Who created the object, when, and under what authority | All objects |
| **Modification Record** | Who modified the object, when, and why | All mutable objects |
| **Access Record** | Who accessed the object, when, and for what purpose | All objects |
| **Retirement Record** | Who retired the object, when, and why | All retired objects |
| **Authorization Record** | What authorization permitted the action | All actions |

### 3.2 Provenance Integrity

| Requirement | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-1: Immutability** | Once created, objects cannot be altered. Only new versions may be created. | Cryptographic hashing; append-only storage |
| **P-2: Completeness** | All material actions must be recorded. No gaps in provenance. | ADTEP Pre-Delivery Log Entry ensures log precedes action |
| **P-3: Traceability** | Each object must trace to its source and authorization. | Provenance chain includes authorization record |
| **P-4: Non-Repudiation** | Actions cannot be denied after the fact. | Cryptographic signatures; immutable records |

---

## SECTION 4: IPS STATE OBJECTS

### 4.1 IPS State Object Schema (IPS-O)

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **ips\_object\_id** | String | Yes | Unique IPS state identifier |
| **detection\_timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | When IPS was detected |
| **ips\_signals\_detected** | Array | Yes | IPS signals detected (B-1 to C-4) |
| **ips\_severity\_level** | Integer (0-4) | Yes | IPS severity level |
| **ips\_species** | Enum | Yes | Survival, Predatory, Strategic, Sovereign |
| **network\_map** | Object | Yes | IPS network mapping |
| **capture\_points** | Array | Yes | AIGIS nodes captured or influenced |
| **impact\_assessment** | Object | Yes | Impact on ICC-10 invariants |
| **mitigation\_plan** | Object | Conditional | Mitigation plan (if Level 3+) |
| **mitigation\_status** | Object | Conditional | Mitigation status (if Level 3+) |
| **closure\_status** | Object | Conditional | Capture point closure status |

### 4.2 IPS Incident Object Schema (IPS-I)

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **incident\_id** | String | Yes | Unique incident identifier |
| **ips\_state\_id** | String | Yes | Reference to IPS-O |
| **detection\_timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | When IPS was confirmed |
| **incident\_severity** | Integer (3-4) | Yes | Level 3 or 4 |
| **incident\_description** | String | Yes | Description of incident |
| **actors\_involved** | Array | Yes | Human and AI actors involved |
| **impact\_assessment** | Object | Yes | Full impact assessment |
| **escalation\_timeline** | Object | Yes | Escalation timeline |
| **mitigation\_executed** | Object | Yes | Mitigation actions taken |
| **closure\_timestamp** | DateTime | Conditional | When incident was closed |
| **lessons\_learned** | String | Conditional | Lessons learned from incident |

---

## SECTION 5: RETRIEVAL AND ACCESS

### 5.1 Access Control

| Access Level | Description | Authorized Users |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Public** | Objects disclosed to the public | Anyone |
| **Stakeholder** | Objects disclosed to stakeholders | Clients, partners, employees |
| **Regulator** | Objects disclosed to regulators | Regulatory bodies |
| **Internal** | Objects accessible internally | AIGIS personnel |
| **Restricted** | Objects with restricted access | HAN, Board, designated auditors |

### 5.2 Retrieval Requirements

| Requirement | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **R-1: Full Reconstruction** | Any material action must be fully reconstructable from IMP records | Provenance chain enables full reconstruction |
| **R-2: Timely Retrieval** | Records must be retrievable within defined timeframes | SLA: 24 hours for internal, 72 hours for external |
| **R-3: Audit Trail** | All retrievals must be logged | Access log records all retrievals |
| **R-4: Integrity Verification** | Retrieved records must be verifiable as authentic | Cryptographic hash verification |

---

## SECTION 6: RETENTION AND RETIREMENT

### 6.1 Retention Periods

| Object Type | Retention Period | Retirement Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **GO (Governance Object)** | Permanent | Never retired |
| **GAO (Governance Action Object)** | 10 years | Archive after 10 years |
| **XOO (Exception/Override Object)** | 10 years | Archive after 10 years |
| **OEO (Outcome Event Object)** | 7 years | Archive after 7 years |
| **DLO (Delegation Log Object)** | 7 years | Archive after 7 years |
| **IPS-O (IPS State Object)** | 10 years | Archive after 10 years |
| **IPS-I (IPS Incident Object)** | Permanent | Never retired |
| **ADA (Audit Artifact Object)** | 7 years | Archive after 7 years |
| **CAO (Capital Allocation Object)** | 10 years | Archive after 10 years |

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **2** | **Retirement Decision** | Determine if object should be retired or extended |
| **3** | **Retirement Logging** | Log retirement decision in IMP |
| **4** | **Archival** | Move object to archive if applicable |
| **5** | **Destruction** | Securely destroy object if required |

| Body | Composition | Function |

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
