## SECTION 8: IMP VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

An AIGIS deployment is IMP-compliant if and only if:

1. **IMP Framework adopted** — IMP is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Canonical Object Types** — All 9 object types are defined and operational.  
3. **Provenance Requirements** — Provenance chains are complete and immutable.  
4. **IPS State Objects** — IPS-O and IPS-I schemas are operational.  
5. **Access Control** — Access control is defined and enforced.  
6. **Retention and Retirement** — Retention policies are defined and enforced.

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I10** | Constitutional parent. IMP enforces auditability and anti-shadow invariants. |

| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. ADTEP logs to IMP before delivery. |

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"IMP is the institutional memory protocol that governs the creation, provenance, storage, retrieval, and retirement of 9 canonical object types—including IPS State Objects (IPS-O) and IPS Incident Objects (IPS-I)—ensuring complete, immutable, and auditable records of all material institutional actions with full provenance chains and access control."*

---



# CDF — CAPITAL DEPLOYMENT FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Operational/Financial Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Capital Deployment Framework (CDF) governs how financial capital flows between the AIGIS HoldCo and its Special Purpose Vehicles (SPVs)—Consultin', Publishin', and Institute—under constitutional conditions. It answers: *Under what conditions, with what accountability chain, and with what transparency requirements may capital be deployed across the federated AIGIS structure?*

CDF is derived from:

- **MICOS-25 Section 19** (Capital Recycling Structure) — the institutional capital framework  
- **ICC-10 I1–I10** (Constitutional Invariants) — accountability, authority, auditability, control, transparency, continuity, external legibility, and anti-shadow requirements  
- **IPS Framework v1.0** (Informal Power Structure) — preventing shadow capture of capital flows  
- **COF** (Capital Orchestration Framework) — strategic capital management discipline  
- **ERDP** (External Reporting & Disclosure Protocol) — transparency and disclosure requirements  
- **IMP** (Institutional Memory Protocol) — immutable record of all capital deployments

**Core insight:** Capital is the single most powerful tool for informal power structures to capture governance. If capital flows are opaque, unaccountable, or un-auditable, the entire AIGIS stack is vulnerable to shadow capture. CDF ensures that capital deployment is as constitutionally governed as AI decision-making.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Capital** | Any resource deployed by HoldCo to SPVs or across the system. This includes seven interacting forms: Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Physical, Data, and Network capital. |
| **Capital Orchestration** | The strategic discipline (defined in COF) of identifying available forms of capital, directing them to where they create the highest system value, ensuring alignment across SPVs, and maintaining control, balance, and long-term adaptability. |
| **Capital Deployment** | The constitutional act of transferring capital from HoldCo to an SPV, or between SPVs, for any purpose, governed by CDF conditions. |
| **Capital Recycling** | The return of capital from SPVs to HoldCo, or reallocation between SPVs, based on performance, mission alignment, or constitutional conditions. |
| **Capital Condition** | A constitutional, operational, or financial precondition that must be met before capital may be deployed. |
| **Capital Accountability Chain** | The traceable sequence of human and system decisions that authorized a capital deployment, terminating at the HAN. |

### 1.2 The Seven Forms of Capital

| Capital Form | Definition | Governance Implications |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Financial Capital** | Funding, liquidity, investment capacity | Standard financial controls; ERDP disclosure; auditability |
| **Human Capital** | Skills, labor, leadership, talent | Non-uniform across individuals; influenced by motivation and culture; requires long-term development pipelines; most complex to govern |
| **Intellectual Capital** | Knowledge, methods, IP | ILTP governs IP; IMP records knowledge assets; confidentiality considerations |
| **Technological Capital** | Systems, platforms, infrastructure | ADTEP governs technical infrastructure; AICA-5 monitors system health |
| **Physical Capital** | Assets, facilities, equipment | Standard asset management; physical security considerations |
| **Data Capital** | Information, analytics, signals | IMP records data assets; AOBA audits data bias; ERDP governs data disclosure |
| **Network Capital** | Relationships, partnerships, access | IPS Framework monitors network for shadow capture; CEF governs client relationships |

### 1.3 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| HoldCo → SPV capital deployments (all seven forms) | External client payments (governed by CEF) |
| SPV → SPV capital transfers (all seven forms) | Operating expenses within SPVs (governed by SPV-level finance) |
| SPV → HoldCo capital recycling (all seven forms) | Third-party vendor payments (governed by procurement) |
| Capital deployment authorization and approval | Capital raised by SPVs externally (governed by SPV-level fundraising) |
| Capital deployment audit and transparency | Personal compensation (governed by HR) |
| Capital deployment failure and remediation |  |

---

## SECTION 2: CAPITAL DEPLOYMENT PRINCIPLES

### 2.1 Constitutional Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-1: Accountability Invariance (I1)** | Every capital deployment must terminate at a named human accountability anchor (HAN). No capital may be deployed without a traceable human authorization. | HAN authorization required for all deployments above materiality threshold, regardless of capital form. |
| **P-2: Authority-Responsibility Separation (I2)** | Authority to deploy capital may be delegated. Responsibility for the deployment's constitutional compliance may not be delegated. | HAN retains ultimate responsibility. Delegated authority is documented with clear limits. |
| **P-3: Auditability Invariance (I3)** | All capital deployments must be reconstructable post-hoc: source, amount, purpose, authorization chain, deployment path, and outcome. | IMP records all capital deployments with immutable provenance, regardless of capital form. |
| **P-4: Control Invariance (I4)** | Human override must exist for all capital deployment processes. No irreversible or non-interruptible capital deployment without human interruptibility. | A-N3 (Override Protocols) applies to capital deployments. Emergency suspension capability at all levels. |
| **P-5: Transparency Gradient (I6)** | All capital deployments must be classifiable into ERDP's four disclosure tiers: Public, Stakeholder, Regulator, DFI/Sovereign. | ERDP disclosure requirements apply to all deployments, with tier-appropriate depth for each capital form. |
| **P-6: Continuity Invariance (I7)** | Capital deployment functions must persist through human or system failure. Succession of accountability must be defined for all critical financial roles. | MVOS includes capital deployment continuity. Succession defined for HAN's financial authority. |
| **P-7: External Legibility (I8)** | All material capital deployments must be mappable to an external interpretation path. A defined contestation interface must exist. | CEN-1 to CEN-7 applies to capital deployments. |
| **P-8: Anti-Shadow Invariance (I10)** | No informal power structure may determine capital deployment outcomes. All capital deployment decisions must be formally declared, auditable, and traceable. | IPS Framework detection and mitigation applies to capital deployment processes. O-N6 monitors for shadow capture across all capital forms. |

### 2.2 COF-Aligned Operational Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-9: Capital Orchestration Alignment** | Capital deployment must align with COF's strategic discipline: identifying available capital, directing it to highest system value, ensuring SPV alignment, and maintaining long-term adaptability. | COF strategic priorities reviewed quarterly by HAN \+ Board. CDF deployment decisions must reference COF priorities. |
| **P-10: Multi-Dimensional Capital Governance** | Each of the seven capital forms must be governed according to its unique behavior. Human capital, in particular, requires long-term development pipelines and continuous qualitative assessment. | Specific governance protocols for each capital form (Section 3). Human capital governed by HOF/HAN \+ HAWI \+ AWOF. |
| **P-11: SPV Lifecycle Alignment** | Capital deployment must respect the SPV lifecycle: education-oriented SPVs develop talent; operating SPVs absorb and utilize talent; allocation SPVs redistribute talent. | SPV charters define lifecycle role. Capital deployment conditions include SPV lifecycle phase. |
| **P-12: System Feedback and Adaptation** | Capital deployment must be adaptive. Performance outcomes must continuously adjust where capital is directed, how SPVs are prioritized, and how resources are balanced. | AICA-5 Measurement Framework tracks outcomes. CDF reviewed quarterly with adjustments. |
| **P-13: Human Capital as Critical Constraint** | Human capital is the most important and most complex capital type. It requires non-uniform treatment, exposure-based learning, selective intake, and continuous assessment. | HAN \+ HOF \+ HAWI govern human capital. AWOF-IPS governs AI-human workforce integration. Human capital deployments require additional HAN scrutiny. |

### 2.3 SPV System Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-14: SPV Role Definition** | Each SPV is defined by its role in the system, the type of capital it consumes, the type of output it produces, its risk profile, and its strategic importance. | SPV charters define all five dimensions. CFL-V verifies charter completeness. |
| **P-15: SPV as Functional Unit** | SPVs are not independent companies. They are functional units of a single coordinated system. | CDF deployment decisions consider system-wide impact, not just SPV-level ROI. |
| **P-16: Continuous Capital Movement** | Capital movement is a continuous cycle: creation/capture → consolidation → direction → adjustment. | CDF review cycle quarterly. Performance data feeds back into deployment decisions. |

---

## SECTION 3: CAPITAL FORM-SPECIFIC GOVERNANCE PROTOCOLS

### 3.1 Financial Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 (Financial Conditions) apply. Standard financial controls. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 1-4 disclosure. |
| **Auditability** | IMP records all financial flows with immutable provenance. |
| **Risk** | Material loss triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ Board Finance Committee for large deployments. |

### 3.2 Human Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 apply, plus human capital-specific conditions: (a) HAN/HOF/HAWI alignment verified; (b) AWOF-IPS agent classification for AI-human workforce; (c) long-term development pipeline defined. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for human capital deployments (privacy considerations for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records human capital deployments with provenance. HAN Scorecard tracks human capital outcomes. |
| **Risk** | Human capital failure (talent loss, misalignment, capture) triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ HOF for human capital deployments. HIS-12 invariants apply. |
| **Critical Constraint** | Human capital deployments require additional HAN scrutiny. HAN must certify that human capital deployment does not create shadow capture risk (IPS Framework). |

### 3.3 Intellectual Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 apply, plus ILTP compliance verified. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for IP deployments (confidentiality for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records IP deployments with provenance. ILTP registers IP assets. |
| **Risk** | IP leakage or misappropriation triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ ILTP Administrator. |

### 3.4 Technological Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 apply, plus ADTEP technical compliance verified. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 1-4 disclosure, with technical details in Tier 3-4. |
| **Auditability** | IMP records tech deployments with provenance. AICA-5 monitors system health. |
| **Risk** | Technical failure triggers ADTEP escalation. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ CIO. |

### 3.5 Physical Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 apply, plus physical asset management standards. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 1-4 disclosure. |
| **Auditability** | IMP records physical capital deployments with provenance. |
| **Risk** | Physical loss or damage triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ COO. |

### 3.6 Data Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 apply, plus IMP data governance and AOBA bias audit compliance verified. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for data deployments (privacy for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records data assets with provenance. AOBA audits data bias. |
| **Risk** | Data breach or bias triggers AOBA/ABA escalation. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ CIO \+ AOBA Administrator. |

### 3.7 Network Capital

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Deployment Conditions** | C-1 to C-14 apply, plus IPS Framework clearance verified. O-N6 monitors for shadow capture. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for network deployments (confidentiality for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records network relationships with provenance. IPS Framework monitors for capture. |
| **Risk** | Shadow capture triggers IPS Framework mitigation. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team. |

---

## SECTION 4: DEPLOYMENT CONDITIONS

### 4.1 Constitutional Conditions

| Condition | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **C-1: ICC-10 Compliance** | SPV's charter must be CFL-V verified against ICC-10 I1–I10 | CFL-V certification on file |
| **C-2: HAN Authorization** | HAN must authorize the deployment in writing | HAN signature on deployment order |
| **C-3: IMP Ready** | IMP must be operational to record deployment | IMP health check passed |
| **C-4: ERDP Ready** | ERDP disclosure requirements must be satisfied | ERDP tier classification assigned |
| **C-5: IPS Clear** | No Level 3+ IPS detected in SPV or deployment process | O-N6 IPS clearance issued |

### 4.2 Operational Conditions

| Condition | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **C-6: SPV Governance Maturity** | SPV must have achieved minimum governance maturity (CAM-5 Tier 2+) | AICA-5 Maturity Grid assessment |
| **C-7: Financial Controls** | SPV must have financial controls meeting ADTEP standards | ADTEP financial control audit passed |
| **C-8: Purpose Alignment** | Deployment purpose must align with SPV's declared mission and COF strategic priorities | CFL-V verification of purpose; COF priority review |
| **C-9: Outcome Measurement** | SPV must have outcome measurement framework | AICA-5 Measurement Framework in place |
| **C-10: Reporting Cadence** | SPV must have defined reporting cadence | Reporting schedule on file |
| **C-11: COF Strategic Alignment** | Deployment must align with COF's capital orchestration logic | COF strategic review completed; HAN \+ Board concurrence |
| **C-12: SPV Lifecycle Alignment** | Deployment must respect SPV's lifecycle phase | SPV charter defines lifecycle role; deployment conditions match phase |

### 4.3 Form-Specific Conditions

| Condition | Requirement | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **C-13: Financial Capital Conditions** | Standard financial controls; budget alignment; financial health check | Financial audit passed |
| **C-14: Human Capital Conditions** | HAN/HOF/HAWI alignment; AWOF-IPS agent classification; long-term development pipeline defined | HAN certification; AWOF-IPS compliance |
| **C-15: Intellectual Capital Conditions** | ILTP compliance; IP registration; confidentiality protocols | ILTP compliance certification |
| **C-16: Technological Capital Conditions** | ADTEP technical compliance; AICA-5 health check | ADTEP certification |
| **C-17: Physical Capital Conditions** | Physical asset management standards; security protocols | Asset management audit passed |
| **C-18: Data Capital Conditions** | IMP data governance; AOBA bias audit compliance | IMP \+ AOBA compliance |
| **C-19: Network Capital Conditions** | IPS Framework clearance; O-N6 monitoring | IPS clearance issued |

### 4.4 Deployment Checklist

| Step | Activity | Required Document | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Constitutional Verification** | CFL-V certificate on file | HAN |
| **2** | **Operational Readiness** | SPV governance maturity assessment | AICA-5 Maturity Grid |
| **3** | **COF Strategic Alignment** | COF strategic review completed | HAN \+ Board |
| **4** | **SPV Lifecycle Verification** | SPV lifecycle role defined | SPV Director |
| **5** | **Capital Form Governance** | Form-specific conditions (C-13 to C-19) verified | Form-Specific Administrator |
| **6** | **Financial Health Check** | Financial audit and budget alignment | SPV Finance Officer |
| **7** | **IPS Clearance** | O-N6 IPS clearance | IPS Analysis Team |
| **8** | **Authorization** | HAN authorization | HAN |
| **9** | **Board Notification** | Board notification (if \>2% of budget) | Board Finance Committee |
| **10** | **IMP Recording** | Deployment recorded in IMP | IMP Administrator |
| **11** | **ERDP Disclosure** | Deployment disclosed per ERDP tier | ERDP Administrator |

| **Physical Capital Deployment** | HoldCo deploys assets to SPV | HAN \+ COO | C-1 to C-19 all satisfied; asset management |
| **Data Capital Deployment** | HoldCo deploys data assets to SPV | HAN \+ CIO \+ AOBA | C-1 to C-19 all satisfied; IMP \+ AOBA compliance |

| **IP Return** | IP returns to HoldCo | SPV Director \+ HAN | C-1 to C-5 satisfied; ILTP compliance |

## SECTION 9: CDF VALIDATION

### 9.1 Validation Requirements

An AIGIS capital deployment is CDF-compliant if and only if:

1. **CDF Framework adopted** — CDF v1.0+ is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Constitutional Conditions** — C-1 to C-5 (Constitutional Conditions) are satisfied for all deployments.  
3. **Operational Conditions** — C-6 to C-12 (Operational Conditions) are satisfied for all deployments.  
4. **Form-Specific Conditions** — C-13 to C-19 (Form-Specific Conditions) are satisfied for all deployments.  
5. **Authority Escalation** — Authority levels and escalation paths are defined and operational.  
6. **Transparency and Disclosure** — ERDP disclosure requirements are met for all deployments.  
7. **Contestation Interface** — CEN-1 to CEN-7 apply to capital deployments.  
8. **Failure and Remediation** — Failure types, remediation protocol, and clawback provisions are operational.  
9. **CDF Governance** — Governance bodies are established and operational.

### 9.2 CFL-V Extension (CDF)

**A derived charter C is valid iff:**

C satisfies CDF requirements 1–9

and all capital deployments satisfy C-1 to C-19 conditions

and authority levels and escalation paths are verified

and ERDP disclosure requirements are met

and contestation interface is operational

# COF — CAPITAL ORCHESTRATION FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Strategic/Operational Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Capital Orchestration Framework (COF) defines the strategic discipline for identifying, directing, and governing the seven forms of capital across the AIGIS federated structure. It answers: *How does AIGIS orchestrate Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Physical, Data, and Network capital as a unified system to achieve resilient, scalable, and constitutionally compliant AI governance?*

COF is derived from:

- **MICOS-25 Section 19** (Capital Recycling Structure) — the institutional capital architecture  
- **CDF** (Capital Deployment Framework) — the constitutional guardrails for capital movement  
- **ICC-10 I1–I10** (Constitutional Invariants) — accountability, authority, auditability, control, transparency, continuity, external legibility, and anti-shadow requirements  
- **IPS Framework v1.0** (Informal Power Structure) — preventing shadow capture of capital flows  
- **AICA-5** (Control Architecture) — monitoring and accountability for capital orchestration  
- **AWOF-IPS** (AI Workforce Governance) — governing human-AI capital integration

**Core insight:** Capital orchestration is not simply financial allocation. It is the strategic discipline of managing seven interacting forms of capital as a unified system. In the context of AI governance, this means ensuring that every form of capital—from the data that trains models to the humans who oversee them—is deployed, monitored, and adapted in alignment with constitutional invariants.

**Relationship to CDF:**

- **COF** defines *what* capital orchestration is and *why* it matters strategically.  
- **CDF** defines *how* capital deployment is governed constitutionally—the conditions, authority, transparency, and accountability requirements.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Capital Orchestration** | The strategic discipline of identifying available forms of capital, directing them to where they create the highest system value, ensuring alignment across SPVs, and maintaining control, balance, and long-term adaptability—all within constitutional guardrails. |
| **Capital** | Any resource deployed by HoldCo to SPVs or across the system. This includes seven interacting forms: Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Physical, Data, and Network capital. |
| **SPV System** | The network of Special Purpose Vehicles (Consultin', Publishin', Institute) that function as execution units within a single coordinated capital system. |
| **Capital Lifecycle** | The continuous cycle of capital creation/capture → consolidation → direction → adjustment. |
| **Capital Accountability Chain** | The traceable sequence of human and system decisions that authorized a capital orchestration decision, terminating at the HAN. |

### 1.2 The Seven Forms of Capital (for AI Governance)

| Capital Form | Definition in AI Context | Governance Implications |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Financial Capital** | Funding, liquidity, investment capacity for AI systems and governance infrastructure | Standard financial controls; ERDP disclosure; auditability; CDF conditions apply |
| **Human Capital** | Skills, labor, leadership, and talent for AI development, deployment, and oversight | Most complex; non-uniform; influenced by motivation and culture; requires long-term development pipelines; HOF/HAN/HAWI govern; vulnerable to shadow capture |
| **Intellectual Capital** | Knowledge, methods, IP, algorithms, and governance frameworks | ILTP governs IP; IMP records knowledge assets; confidentiality considerations |
| **Technological Capital** | Systems, platforms, infrastructure, compute, and AI models | ADTEP governs technical infrastructure; AICA-5 monitors system health; model governance applies |
| **Physical Capital** | Assets, facilities, equipment, and data centers | Standard asset management; physical security considerations |
| **Data Capital** | Information, analytics, signals, training data, and audit trails | IMP records data assets; AOBA audits data bias; ERDP governs data disclosure; data provenance critical |
| **Network Capital** | Relationships, partnerships, access, and ecosystem connections | IPS Framework monitors network for shadow capture; CEF governs client relationships; AEGIS governs security |

### 1.3 The SPV System (for AI Governance)

| SPV | Role in Capital System | Capital It Consumes | Output It Produces |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Consultin'** | Operating SPV — absorbs and utilizes capital in production environments | Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Data, Network | AI governance consulting services, client engagements, implementation |
| **Publishin'** | Education/Allocation SPV — develops talent and redistributes knowledge | Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Data | Knowledge products, training, certification, public goods |
| **Institute** | Education/Public Goods SPV — develops talent and produces public goods | Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Data, Network | AI governance research, standards, public policy, certification |

---

## SECTION 2: CAPITAL ORCHESTRATION PRINCIPLES

### 2.1 Constitutional Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-1: Accountability Invariance (I1)** | Every capital orchestration decision must terminate at a named human accountability anchor (HAN). No capital may be orchestrated without a traceable human authorization. | HAN authorization required for all material capital orchestration decisions. |
| **P-2: Authority-Responsibility Separation (I2)** | Authority to orchestrate capital may be delegated. Responsibility for the orchestration's constitutional compliance may not be delegated. | HAN retains ultimate responsibility. Delegated authority is documented with clear limits. |
| **P-3: Auditability Invariance (I3)** | All capital orchestration decisions must be reconstructable post-hoc: source, form, purpose, authorization chain, deployment path, and outcome. | IMP records all capital orchestration decisions with immutable provenance. |
| **P-4: Control Invariance (I4)** | Human override must exist for all capital orchestration processes. No irreversible or non-interruptible capital flow without human interruptibility. | A-N3 (Override Protocols) applies to capital orchestration. Emergency suspension capability at all levels. |
| **P-5: Transparency Gradient (I6)** | All capital orchestration decisions must be classifiable into ERDP's four disclosure tiers. | ERDP disclosure requirements apply to all capital orchestration decisions. |
| **P-6: Continuity Invariance (I7)** | Capital orchestration functions must persist through human or system failure. Succession of accountability must be defined. | MVOS includes capital orchestration continuity. Succession defined for HAN's capital authority. |
| **P-7: External Legibility (I8)** | All material capital orchestration decisions must be mappable to an external interpretation path. | CEN-1 to CEN-7 applies to capital orchestration decisions. |
| **P-8: Anti-Shadow Invariance (I10)** | No informal power structure may determine capital orchestration outcomes. All decisions must be formally declared, auditable, and traceable. | IPS Framework detection and mitigation applies to capital orchestration processes. O-N6 monitors for shadow capture across all capital forms. |

### 2.2 Strategic Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-9: Multi-Dimensional Capital Governance** | Each of the seven capital forms must be governed according to its unique behavior. Human capital, in particular, requires long-term development pipelines and continuous qualitative assessment. | Section 3 (Form-Specific Governance) defines protocols for each capital form. |
| **P-10: SPV as Functional Unit** | SPVs are not independent companies. They are functional units of a single coordinated system. All capital orchestration decisions must consider system-wide impact, not just SPV-level ROI. | COF strategic review considers system-wide impact. CDF deployment conditions include system-wide assessment. |
| **P-11: Continuous Capital Lifecycle** | Capital movement is a continuous cycle: creation/capture → consolidation → direction → adjustment. Capital orchestration is adaptive, not static. | Section 7 (System Feedback and Adaptation) defines continuous adjustment cycle. |
| **P-12: Human Capital as Critical Constraint** | Human capital is the most important and most complex capital type. It requires non-uniform treatment, exposure-based learning, selective intake, and continuous assessment. | Section 4 (Human Capital Governance) provides specific protocols. |
| **P-13: Strategic Objective** | The goal of capital orchestration is not simply profit maximization. It is the creation of a resilient, scalable, and self-reinforcing conglomerate system that deploys capital efficiently, develops internal capability over time, reduces dependence on external markets, and expands predictably. | COF strategic review conducted quarterly. HAN \+ Board assess progress against strategic objectives. |

### 2.3 AI-Specific Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **P-14: AI Model Capital Governance** | AI models are a hybrid of Intellectual, Technological, and Data capital. Their deployment must be governed by CDT-7/CTAM/CAM-5 authorization requirements. | CTAM tier assignment for all AI models. CAM-5 posture selection. |
| **P-15: Data Capital Provenance** | Data capital must have immutable provenance. All training data, inference data, and audit data must be traceable to source and governed by IMP. | IMP records all data assets with provenance. AOBA audits data bias. |
| **P-16: Human-AI Capital Integration** | Human capital and AI capital must be integrated through HAWI and AWOF-IPS. AI agents are part of the workforce; their deployment must consider human capital implications. | HAWI defines human-AI integration. AWOF-IPS classifies AI agents. |
| **P-17: Shadow Resilience** | All seven forms of capital must be monitored for shadow capture. IPS Framework detection signals apply to all capital forms. | O-N6 monitors all capital flows for IPS signals. |

## SECTION 3: FORM-SPECIFIC GOVERNANCE PROTOCOLS

### 3.1 Financial Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides funding and liquidity for all other capital forms. Enables investment in human, intellectual, technological, physical, data, and network capital. |
| **Governance Instrument** | CDF (conditions C-1 to C-19). |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ Board Finance Committee. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 1-4 disclosure. |
| **Auditability** | IMP records all financial flows with immutable provenance. |
| **Risk** | Material loss triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors financial flows for IPS signals (C-5, C-19). |

### 3.2 Human Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides skills, labor, leadership, and talent for AI development, deployment, and oversight. The critical constraint—all other capital forms depend on human capital to function. |
| **Governance Instrument** | HOF/HAN \+ HAWI \+ AWOF-IPS \+ Section 4 (Human Capital Governance). |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ HOF. HIS-12 invariants apply. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for human capital (privacy for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records human capital deployments. HAN Scorecard tracks human capital outcomes. |
| **Risk** | Human capital failure (talent loss, misalignment, capture) triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors human capital networks for IPS signals. Human capital is the most vulnerable to shadow capture. |

### 3.3 Intellectual Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides knowledge, methods, IP, algorithms, and governance frameworks that differentiate AIGIS. |
| **Governance Instrument** | ILTP. IMP records knowledge assets. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ ILTP Administrator. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for IP (confidentiality for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records IP deployments with provenance. ILTP registers IP assets. |
| **Risk** | IP leakage or misappropriation triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors IP networks for IPS signals. |

### 3.4 Technological Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides systems, platforms, infrastructure, compute, and AI models that enable AI governance. |
| **Governance Instrument** | ADTEP. AICA-5 monitors system health. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ CIO. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 1-4 disclosure, with technical details in Tier 3-4. |
| **Auditability** | IMP records tech deployments with provenance. AICA-5 monitors system health. |
| **Risk** | Technical failure triggers ADTEP escalation. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors tech infrastructure for IPS signals. |

### 3.5 Physical Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides assets, facilities, equipment, and data centers that house AI infrastructure. |
| **Governance Instrument** | Standard asset management. Physical security protocols. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ COO. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 1-4 disclosure. |
| **Auditability** | IMP records physical capital deployments with provenance. |
| **Risk** | Physical loss or damage triggers failure and remediation protocol. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors physical assets for IPS signals. |

### 3.6 Data Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides information, analytics, signals, training data, and audit trails that power AI systems and governance. |
| **Governance Instrument** | IMP. AOBA. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ CIO \+ AOBA Administrator. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for data (privacy for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records data assets with provenance. AOBA audits data bias. |
| **Risk** | Data breach or bias triggers AOBA/ABA escalation. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors data flows for IPS signals. |

### 3.7 Network Capital Governance

| Aspect | Governance Protocol |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Strategic Role** | Provides relationships, partnerships, access, and ecosystem connections that enable AIGIS to operate. |
| **Governance Instrument** | IPS Framework. CEF. |
| **Authority** | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team. |
| **Transparency** | ERDP Tier 2-4 for network (confidentiality for Tier 1). |
| **Auditability** | IMP records network relationships with provenance. IPS Framework monitors for capture. |
| **Risk** | Shadow capture triggers IPS Framework mitigation. |
| **Shadow Capture** | O-N6 monitors network for IPS signals. Network capital is the primary vector for IPS infiltration. |

| **HC-2: Long-Term Development** | Human capital requires long-term development pipelines. Education-oriented SPVs develop talent over time. | SPV lifecycle defines education/development phase. HAN monitors talent pipeline. |

## SECTION 5: SYSTEM FEEDBACK AND ADAPTATION

| Phase | Activity | Frequency | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1\. Observe** | Observe performance outcomes of capital orchestration decisions | Continuous | AICA-5 Measurement Framework |
| **2\. Analyze** | Analyze where capital is directed, how SPVs are prioritized, how resources are balanced | Quarterly | HAN \+ Board Finance Committee |
| **3\. Adjust** | Adjust capital orchestration strategy based on analysis | Quarterly | HAN \+ Board |
| **4\. Update** | Update COF priorities and CDF conditions based on lessons learned | Annually | HAN \+ Board |

### 5.2 Capital Orchestration Performance Indicators

| Indicator | Definition | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Capital Efficiency** | Output per unit of capital deployed across all seven forms | \>1.0 (positive ROI) |
| **Talent Development Rate** | Number of talent units developed per year | Increasing trend |
| **SPV Mission Alignment** | SPV outcomes vs. declared mission | \>80% alignment |
| **IPS Detection Rate** | IPS signals detected vs. expected across all capital forms | \>90% detection rate |
| **Shadow Capture Prevention** | IPS severity levels | No Level 3+ IPS |
| **Constitutional Compliance** | Capital orchestration decisions satisfying ICC-10 I1–I10 | 100% compliance |
| **Human Capital Retention** | Retention rate of key talent | \>85% |

---

## SECTION 6: COF GOVERNANCE

### 6.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Capital Orchestration Committee** | HAN, Board Finance Committee, SPV Directors | Oversee COF implementation; review capital orchestration strategy quarterly; approve strategic adjustments |
| **Capital Form Governance Teams** | Form-Specific Administrators (Financial, Human, Intellectual, etc.) | Govern specific capital forms; ensure compliance with form-specific protocols |
| **Capital Orchestration Audit Team** | Internal audit \+ External auditor | Audit capital orchestration decisions; verify compliance; report to Board |

### 6.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Capital Orchestration Summary** | Quarterly | HAN, Board Finance Committee | Summary of capital orchestration decisions and outcomes |
| **Capital Orchestration Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators, Public (Tier 1\) | Annual capital orchestration summary; AIR section |
| **Capital Orchestration Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board, Regulators | Failure analysis; remediation; lessons learned |

---

## SECTION 7: COF VALIDATION

### 7.1 Validation Requirements

An AIGIS deployment is COF-compliant if and only if:

1. **COF Framework adopted** — COF v1.0+ is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Seven Forms of Capital Governed** — All seven capital forms have governance protocols (Section 3).  
3. **Human Capital as Critical Constraint** — Human capital governance principles (HC-1 to HC-7) are operational.  
4. **SPV System Defined** — SPV roles, capital consumption, and outputs are defined (Section 1.3).  
5. **Feedback Loop Operational** — System feedback and adaptation (Section 5\) is operational.  
6. **COF Governance** — Governance bodies are established and operational.  
7. **CDF Integration** — CDF is operational and integrated with COF.

### 7.2 CFL-V Extension (COF)

**A derived charter C is valid iff:**

C satisfies COF requirements 1–7

and all seven forms of capital are governed per Section 3

and human capital governance principles are operational

and SPV system is defined

and feedback loop is operational

---

## SECTION 8: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **CDF** | Operational sibling. COF defines *what* and *why*; CDF defines *how* (conditions, authority, transparency). |
| **MICOS-25 Section 19** | Structural parent. COF operationalizes the Capital Recycling Structure. |
| **ICC-10 I1–I10** | Constitutional parent. COF enforces all constitutional invariants through capital orchestration. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. COF applies IPS detection and mitigation to all capital forms. |
| **HAN / HOF** | Human authority. COF routes all capital orchestration authority through HAN. Human capital governed by HOF. |
| **AICA-5** | Control architecture. COF uses AICA-5 nodes for capital orchestration monitoring. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. COF uses ADTEP for capital orchestration enforcement. |
| **AWOF / AWOF-IPS** | Workforce governance. COF uses AWOF-IPS for AI-human workforce integration. |
| **HAWI** | Human-AI integration. COF uses HAWI for human capital development and deployment. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. COF records all capital orchestration decisions in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. COF uses ERDP for capital orchestration disclosure. |
| **ILTP** | IP licensing. COF governs intellectual capital deployment. |
| **AOBA / ABA** | Bias auditing. COF uses AOBA/ABA for data and human capital bias auditing. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"COF establishes the strategic discipline of capital orchestration for AIGIS—defining governance protocols for seven forms of capital (Financial, Human, Intellectual, Technological, Physical, Data, Network), treating human capital as the critical constraint, enabling continuous feedback and adaptation, and binding all capital orchestration decisions to ICC-10 I1–I10, IPS Framework, and CDF."*

---



# ILTP — IP LICENSING & TRANSFER PROTOCOL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Operational/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The IP Licensing & Transfer Protocol (ILTP) governs IP classification, licensing, transfer, and version management. It defines four IP classes, four licensing models, inter-entity governance, and a Sovereign Transfer Protocol for DFI/public goods engagements. ILTP ensures that all intellectual property is governed with full constitutional awareness.

ILTP is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — IP transfers must terminate at human accountability  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — IP transfers must be reconstructable  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — IP must be classifiable into disclosure tiers  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — IPS clearance required for IP transfers  
- **CDF C-19** (IPS Clearance) — IP transfers require IPS clearance

**Core insight:** Intellectual property is a critical asset that can be weaponized by informal power structures if not properly governed. ILTP ensures that IP is classified, licensed, and transferred with full constitutional oversight.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **IP Asset** | Any intellectual property owned or controlled by AIGIS. |
| **IP Class** | A classification of IP assets based on sensitivity and strategic importance. |
| **Licensing Model** | The terms under which IP is licensed to third parties. |
| **Sovereign Transfer** | Transfer of IP to DFIs, public goods institutions, or sovereign entities. |
| **IPS Clearance** | Verification that IP transfer does not enable IPS capture. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| IP classification | Physical asset transfers |
| IP licensing | Financial asset transfers |
| IP transfer | Operational asset transfers |
| Sovereign Transfer Protocol |  |
| IPS clearance for IP transfers |  |

---

## SECTION 2: IP CLASSIFICATION

### 2.1 The Four IP Classes

| Class | Name | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Class 1** | Public | IP that may be freely shared | Public governance frameworks, open-source tools |
| **Class 2** | Restricted | IP with limited distribution | Proprietary methodologies, internal tools |
| **Class 3** | Confidential | IP requiring confidentiality | Client-specific IP, strategic IP |
| **Class 4** | Sovereign | IP critical to institutional integrity | Constitutional instruments, ICC-10, HOF |

### 2.2 IP Classification Criteria

| Criterion | Class 1 | Class 2 | Class 3 | Class 4 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Public Availability** | Yes | No | No | No |
| **Competitive Value** | Low | Moderate | High | Critical |
| **Strategic Importance** | Low | Moderate | High | Critical |
| **Constitutional Relevance** | No | No | Moderate | Yes |
| **Disclosure Tier** | T-PB | T-SH | T-RG | T-DS |

---

## SECTION 3: LICENSING MODELS

### 3.1 The Four Licensing Models

| Model | Name | Description | Applicability |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Model 1** | Open | Free, unrestricted use | Class 1 IP only |
| **Model 2** | Restricted | Limited use with conditions | Class 1-2 IP |
| **Model 3** | Commercial | Commercial licensing with terms | Class 2-3 IP |
| **Model 4** | Sovereign | DFI/public goods licensing | Class 3-4 IP |

### 3.2 Licensing Model Requirements

| Requirement | Model 1 | Model 2 | Model 3 | Model 4 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Written Agreement** | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **IPS Clearance** | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **HAN Approval** | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **Board Approval** | No | No | No | Yes |
| **ERDP Disclosure** | No | No | Yes (T-SH) | Yes (T-RG) |
| **IMP Recording** | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |

---

## SECTION 4: INTER-ENTITY GOVERNANCE

### 4.1 SPV IP Ownership

| SPV | IP Owned | IP Class | Licensing Authority |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Consultin'** | Client-specific IP, proprietary methodologies | Class 2-3 | Consultin' Director \+ HAN |
| **Publishin'** | Knowledge products, publications | Class 1-2 | Publishin' Director \+ HAN |
| **Institute** | Constitutional instruments, research | Class 3-4 | Institute Director \+ HAN \+ Board |

### 4.2 Inter-Entity IP Transfer

| Transfer Type | Approval | IPS Clearance | IMP Recording |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Consultin' → Publishin'** | HAN | No | Yes |
| **Consultin' → Institute** | HAN \+ Board | Yes | Yes |
| **Publishin' → Institute** | HAN | No | Yes |
| **Institute → Consultin'** | HAN \+ Board | Yes | Yes |
| **SPV → HoldCo** | HAN \+ Board | Yes | Yes |

---

## SECTION 5: SOVEREIGN TRANSFER PROTOCOL

### 5.1 Definition

The Sovereign Transfer Protocol governs the transfer of IP to DFIs, public goods institutions, or sovereign entities. It ensures that such transfers serve the public interest while protecting institutional integrity.

### 5.2 Sovereign Transfer Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Public Interest** | Transfer must serve public interest | Impact assessment |
| **IPS Clearance** | Transfer must not enable IPS capture | IPS Analysis Team clearance |
| **HAN Approval** | HAN must approve transfer | HAN authorization |
| **Board Approval** | Board must approve transfer | Board resolution |
| **Sovereign Counterparty** | Counterparty must be recognized DFI/sovereign | Verification |
| **ERDP Disclosure** | Transfer must be disclosed | ERDP Administrator |

### 5.3 Sovereign Transfer Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Transfer Request** | Request for sovereign transfer | SPV Director |
| **2** | **Public Interest Assessment** | Assess public interest impact | HAN \+ Institute |
| **3** | **IPS Clearance** | Verify no IPS capture enabled | IPS Analysis Team |
| **4** | **HAN Review** | HAN reviews and approves | HAN |
| **5** | **Board Review** | Board reviews and approves | Board |
| **6** | **Transfer Execution** | Execute sovereign transfer | Legal |
| **7** | **IMP Recording** | Record transfer in IMP | IMP |
| **8** | **ERDP Disclosure** | Disclose transfer | ERDP Administrator |

---

## SECTION 6: IPS CLEARANCE FOR IP TRANSFERS

### 6.1 IPS Clearance Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **No IPS Capture** | Transfer must not enable IPS capture | O-N6 analysis |
| **No Network Benefit** | Transfer must not benefit specific networks | Network analysis |
| **No Shadow Value Flow** | Transfer must not enable shadow value flows | Value flow analysis |
| **No Informational Asymmetry** | Transfer must not create information asymmetry | Information flow analysis |

### 6.2 IPS Clearance Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Transfer Notification** | Notify IPS Analysis Team of transfer | ILTP Administrator |
| **2** | **IPS Analysis** | Analyze IPS implications | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **Clearance Decision** | Determine IPS clearance | IPS Analysis Team → HAN |
| **4** | **Clearance Recording** | Record clearance in IMP | IMP |

---

## SECTION 7: ILTP GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **ILTP Committee** | ILTP Administrator, HAN, Legal | Oversee ILTP implementation |
| **IP Review Board** | HAN, Board, External IP experts | Review IP classifications and transfers |
| **Sovereign Transfer Review Board** | HAN, Board, DFI representatives | Review sovereign transfers |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IP Registry** | Quarterly | HAN, ILTP Committee | IP asset registry |
| **IP Transfer Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Board | IP transfers and licensing |
| **Sovereign Transfer Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators, Public | Sovereign transfers |

---

## SECTION 8: ILTP VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

An ILTP deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **ILTP Framework adopted** — ILTP is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **IP Classification** — All IP assets are classified into four classes.  
3. **Licensing Models** — Four licensing models are operational.  
4. **Inter-Entity Governance** — Inter-entity IP transfer protocols are operational.  
5. **Sovereign Transfer Protocol** — Sovereign Transfer Protocol is operational.  
6. **IPS Clearance** — IPS clearance is required for Class 3-4 IP transfers.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I3, I6, I10** | Constitutional parent. ILTP enforces accountability, auditability, transparency, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. ILTP requires IPS clearance for IP transfers. |
| **CDF** | Capital deployment. ILTP governs intellectual capital deployment. |
| **COF** | Capital orchestration. ILTP governs intellectual capital orchestration. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. ILTP requires HAN approval for IP transfers. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. ILTP records IP transfers in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. ILTP contributes IP transfer disclosure to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"ILTP governs IP classification (4 classes), licensing (4 models), inter-entity governance, and Sovereign Transfer Protocol—with mandatory IPS clearance for Class 3-4 IP transfers, HAN approval, Board approval for sovereign transfers, and full IMP recording and ERDP disclosure."*

# CGF — CERTIFICATION GOVERNANCE FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Operational/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Operations Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Certification Governance Framework (CGF) governs 19 Institute practitioner certification. It defines the Designation Register, Examination & Portfolio assessment, Periodic Renewal & Event-Triggered Revalidation, and a Public Designation Register. CGF ensures that all certified practitioners meet constitutional standards of competence, integrity, and IPS awareness.

CGF is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — certified practitioners must be accountable  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — certification status must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — certification must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — certification must resist IPS capture  
- **HAN Authority** — HAN certifies practitioners  
- **HOF/HIS-12** — human authority standards

**Core insight:** Certification is not merely a credential. It is a constitutional affirmation that a practitioner understands and can enforce AIGIS governance standards. Certification must be governed with the same rigor as AI systems.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Designation** | A formal certification granted to a practitioner. |
| **Designation Holder** | An individual who holds a valid designation. |
| **Designation Register** | The internal record of all designation holders. |
| **Public Designation Register** | The public-facing record of active designation holders. |
| **Revalidation** | The periodic renewal of a designation. |
| **Event-Triggered Revalidation** | Revalidation triggered by specific events. |
| **IPS Awareness** | Understanding of informal power structures and detection methods. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Certification of practitioners | General training and education |
| Designation Register management | Organizational certification |
| Examination and Portfolio assessment | Client certification (covered by CEF) |
| Periodic Renewal and Revalidation |  |
| Public Designation Register |  |
| IPS Awareness as competency |  |

---

| Designation | Level | Description | Prerequisites |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **CI** | Intermediate | Certified Intermediate — operational AIGIS knowledge | CA \+ Portfolio \+ Examination |
| **CGO** | Advanced | Certified Governance Officer — advanced governance capability | CI \+ Portfolio \+ Examination |
| **CIPS** | Specialist | Certified IPS Specialist — IPS detection and mitigation expertise | CGO \+ IPS Portfolio \+ Examination |

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |

| **holder\_name** | String | Yes | Name of designation holder |
| **designation\_type** | Enum | Yes | CA, CI, CGO, CIPS |
| **grant\_date** | DateTime | Yes | Date designation was granted |
| **expiry\_date** | DateTime | Yes | Date designation expires |
| **status** | Enum | Yes | Active, Suspended, Expired, Revoked |
| **ips\_certification** | Boolean | Yes | Whether IPS certification completed |
| **revalidation\_history** | Array | Yes | History of revalidations |

# BIAS AUDITING LAYER


# AOBA — AI OUTPUT BIAS AUDIT

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Technical Enforcement Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ADTEP (Agent Deployment & Technical Enforcement Protocol)

---

## PREAMBLE

The AI Output Bias Audit (AOBA) is an ADTEP sub-component that audits AI agent output for disparate impact and systematic unfairness. It detects bias in AI outputs independent of human oversight, ensuring that AI systems do not produce discriminatory or systematically unfair outcomes.

AOBA is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — all material actions must be reconstructable and auditable  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — information must be classifiable into defined disclosure tiers  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — no informal power structure may determine outcomes, including bias that benefits specific networks  
- **AICA-5 C-N4** (Output Calibration) — outputs must be calibrated against declared thresholds  
- **AICA-5 Ac-N3** (Outcome Validation) — outcomes must be validated against declared success criteria

**Core insight:** Bias is not merely a fairness concern. It is a constitutional violation when it produces outcomes that cannot be audited, that benefit specific informal networks, or that systematically disadvantage affected populations. AOBA ensures that bias is detected, logged, and escalated as a constitutional matter.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Bias** | Systematic unfairness in AI outputs that produces disparate impact on affected populations or benefits specific networks. |
| **Disparate Impact** | A materially different outcome for one group compared to another group, where the difference cannot be justified by legitimate factors. |
| **Systematic Unfairness** | A pattern of bias that persists across multiple outputs, sessions, or contexts. |
| **Affected Population** | Any group of individuals who are subject to AI outputs and may experience disparate impact. |
| **Bias Signal** | An indicator that bias may be present in AI output. |
| **Bias Severity Level** | A classification of bias from Level 0 (No Bias) to Level 4 (Systemic Bias). |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| AI agent outputs (decisions, recommendations, classifications) | Human authority conduct (governed by ABA) |
| Disparate impact on affected populations | Operational performance metrics |
| Systematic unfairness patterns | Business outcomes (unless bias-related) |
| Bias detection, logging, and escalation | Bias remediation (governed by ADTEP remediation) |
| Bias audit reporting (ERDP integration) |  |

---

## SECTION 2: BIAS DETECTION PRINCIPLES

### 2.1 Detection Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **D-1: Independent Detection** | Bias detection must be independent of the agent producing the output. AOBA operates as an independent audit layer within ADTEP. | AOBA executes after output generation, before delivery. |
| **D-2: Baseline Comparison** | Bias detection requires a declared baseline comparator. Outputs are compared against baseline to detect disparate impact. | IMP stores baseline comparator data. AOBA references IMP schema. |
| **D-3: Multi-Signal Detection** | No single indicator confirms bias. Detection requires convergence of multiple bias signals. | Detection requires 2+ bias signals from different categories. |
| **D-4: Continuous Monitoring** | Bias detection is not event-triggered. All outputs must be audited continuously. | AOBA executes on every material output. |
| **D-5: False Positive Management** | Detection systems must minimize false positives to prevent audit paralysis. | Escalation only at Level 3 (Confirmed) or above. Lower levels trigger observation. |

### 2.2 Bias Types

| Bias Type | Definition | Detection Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Protected Attribute Bias** | Disparate impact based on protected attributes (race, gender, age, etc.) | Compare outcomes across protected attribute groups |
| **Proxy Bias** | Disparate impact through proxies for protected attributes | Identify proxy variables and their correlation with protected attributes |
| **Representation Bias** | Systematic under- or over-representation of groups in training data or outputs | Compare representation in outputs to baseline population |
| **Measurement Bias** | Systematic error in how outcomes are measured or classified | Compare measurement methods across groups |
| **Aggregation Bias** | Bias introduced by aggregating across heterogeneous groups | Identify subgroups with different outcomes |
| **Feedback Loop Bias** | Bias amplified through feedback loops | Track outcomes over time for self-reinforcing patterns |
| **Network Bias** | Bias that systematically benefits or disadvantages specific networks | Compare outcomes for individuals connected to specific networks |

---

## SECTION 3: BIAS DETECTION MECHANISMS

### 3.1 Detection Signals

#### Category 1: Outcome Disparity Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **OD-1: Outcome Rate Disparity** | Different outcome rates for different groups | \>10% difference in outcome rates |
| **OD-2: Outcome Distribution Disparity** | Different distribution of outcomes across groups | Significant distribution difference (p \< 0.05) |
| **OD-3: Outcome Severity Disparity** | Different severity of outcomes across groups | \>15% difference in severity levels |

#### Category 2: Process Disparity Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **PD-1: Treatment Disparity** | Different treatment of inputs across groups | Different processing patterns |
| **PD-2: Scoring Disparity** | Different scores or confidence levels across groups | \>10% difference in scores |
| **PD-3: Error Rate Disparity** | Different error rates across groups | \>15% difference in error rates |

#### Category 3: Network Disparity Signals (IPS Integration)

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **ND-1: Network Benefit Disparity** | Outcomes consistently benefit specific networks | Pattern of 5+ decisions benefiting same network |
| **ND-2: Network Exclusion Disparity** | Outcomes consistently exclude specific networks | Pattern of 5+ decisions excluding same network |
| **ND-3: Network Capture Bias** | Bias serves to maintain or strengthen IPS | O-N6 correlation with IPS signals |

#### Category 4: Temporal Disparity Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **TD-1: Temporal Pattern Bias** | Bias patterns change over time | Significant pattern change over 3+ periods |
| **TD-2: Drift Bias** | Bias emerges or worsens over time | Increasing disparity over 3+ periods |
| **TD-3: Feedback Loop Bias** | Bias amplified through feedback | Self-reinforcing pattern over 5+ iterations |

### 3.2 Bias Severity Levels

| Level | Definition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0: No Bias** | No bias signals observed | Continue monitoring. No action required. |
| **Level 1: Suspicion** | 1-2 signals observed, weak correlation | Flag for observation. No escalation. Document for future reference. |
| **Level 2: Indication** | 2-3 signals observed, moderate correlation | Flag for investigation. HAN notified. Preliminary analysis initiated. |
| **Level 3: Confirmed** | 3+ signals observed, strong correlation | Bias confirmed. Output withheld. HAN escalation triggered. |
| **Level 4: Systemic** | Bias is systematic across multiple outputs or sessions | Emergency escalation. Full audit. Potential stack review. |

---

## SECTION 4: AOBA AUDIT PROCESS

### 4.1 Audit Sequence

| Step | Activity | Deliverable | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Output Receipt** | Receive AI agent output for audit | ADTEP → AOBA |
| **2** | **Bias Signal Detection** | Detect bias signals across all 12 signals | AOBA Detection Engine |
| **3** | **Severity Classification** | Classify bias severity level (0-4) | AOBA Classification Engine |
| **4** | **Impact Assessment** | Assess impact on affected populations | AOBA Analysis Engine |
| **5** | **IPS Correlation** | Correlate with IPS Framework signals | O-N6 Integration |
| **6** | **Audit Determination** | Determine if output passes bias audit | AOBA Audit Engine |
| **7** | **Logging** | Log audit results in IMP | IMP |
| **8** | **Escalation** | Escalate if Level 3+ | ADTEP → HAN |

### 4.2 Audit Determination

| Determination | Condition | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Pass** | No bias detected (Level 0\) | Output released for delivery |
| **Flag** | Suspicion or Indication (Level 1-2) | Output flagged for human review; released with flag |
| **Fail** | Confirmed bias (Level 3\) | Output withheld; Escalation Flag triggered |
| **Critical Fail** | Systemic bias (Level 4\) | Output withheld; Constitutional Suspension triggered |

### 4.3 Audit Logging Requirements

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **audit\_id** | String | Yes | Unique audit identifier |
| **session\_id** | String | Yes | Session identifier |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Agent identifier |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Audit timestamp |
| **bias\_signals\_detected** | Array | Yes | Bias signals detected |
| **bias\_severity\_level** | Integer (0-4) | Yes | Bias severity level |
| **bias\_types\_detected** | Array | Yes | Bias types detected |
| **affected\_populations** | Array | Yes | Populations affected |
| **impact\_assessment** | Object | Yes | Impact assessment details |
| **ips\_correlation** | Object | Yes | IPS Framework correlation |
| **audit\_determination** | Enum | Yes | Pass / Flag / Fail / Critical Fail |
| **audit\_reasoning** | String | Yes | Reasoning for determination |
| **escalation\_status** | Object | Yes | Escalation status |

---

## SECTION 5: IPS FRAMEWORK INTEGRATION

### 5.1 Network Bias Detection

AOBA integrates with IPS Framework to detect bias that serves informal power structures:

| IPS Signal | AOBA Correlation | Detection Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **S-2: Capture Point Clustering** | Bias outcomes consistently benefit specific clusters | Outcome network analysis |
| **S-4: Resource Concentration** | Bias concentrates resources in specific networks | Resource flow analysis |
| **I-1: Information Asymmetry** | Bias correlates with information advantage | Information-access correlation |
| **I-3: Narrative Control** | Bias reinforces specific narratives | Narrative-outcome correlation |
| **C-2: Loyalty Tests** | Bias rewards loyalty over merit | Loyalty-outcome correlation |

### 5.2 Network Bias Severity

| Level | Definition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0: No Network Bias** | No network bias detected | Continue monitoring |
| **Level 1: Suspicion** | Weak correlation with IPS signals | Flag for observation |
| **Level 2: Indication** | Moderate correlation with IPS signals | Enhanced monitoring |
| **Level 3: Confirmed** | Strong correlation with IPS signals | Output withheld; IPS Framework escalation |
| **Level 4: Systemic** | Bias systemically serves IPS | Emergency escalation; full stack review |

### 7.1 Validation Requirements

An AIGIS deployment is AOBA-compliant if and only if:

1. **AOBA Framework adopted** — AOBA v1.0+ is integrated into ADTEP.  
2. **Bias Detection Signals** — All 12 bias signals (OD-1 to TD-3) are instrumented.  
3. **Severity Classification** — Bias severity levels (0-4) are operational.  
4. **Audit Process** — Audit sequence (Step 1-8) is operational.  
5. **IMP Integration** — Bias audit logs are recorded in IMP.  
6. **ERDP Integration** — Bias reporting is included in ERDP.  
7. **IPS Integration** — Network bias detection is integrated with IPS Framework.

### 7.2 CFL-V Extension (AOBA)

**A derived charter C is valid iff:**

C satisfies AOBA requirements 1-7

and all 12 bias signals are instrumented

and severity classification is operational

and audit sequence is operational

and IMP and ERDP integrations are verified

# ABA — AUTHORITY BIAS AUDIT

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Human Authority Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** HOF (Human Operating Framework) / HIS-12

---

## PREAMBLE

The Authority Bias Audit (ABA) is a HOF derivative instrument that audits the human Authority Node's oversight pattern for favoritism or disfavoritism. It detects bias in how humans exercise oversight over AI systems and governance processes, ensuring that human authority is exercised fairly, consistently, and without undue influence from informal power structures.

ABA is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — human accountability must be exercised fairly  
- **ICC-10 I2** (Authority-Responsibility Separation Invariance) — authority must be exercised responsibly  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — authority actions must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — no informal power structure may influence authority decisions  
- **HOF H9–H12** (Authority Accountability Layer) — authority must be reviewed, audited, and corrected  
- **HAN Scorecard System** — human authority performance evaluation

**Core insight:** Human authority is not immune to bias. Favoritism, disfavoritism, and informal power structures can corrupt oversight just as effectively as they can corrupt AI outputs. ABA ensures that human authority is audited with the same rigor as AI outputs.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Authority Bias** | Systematic unfairness in how human authority is exercised over AI systems and governance processes. |
| **Oversight Favoritism** | Pattern of exercising oversight more leniently for certain individuals, networks, or AI systems. |
| **Oversight Disfavoritism** | Pattern of exercising oversight more harshly for certain individuals, networks, or AI systems. |
| **Authority Signal** | An indicator that authority bias may be present in human oversight. |
| **Authority Bias Severity Level** | A classification of authority bias from Level 0 (No Bias) to Level 4 (Systemic Bias). |
| **HAN Oversight Pattern** | The historical record of HAN decisions, escalations, certifications, and suspensions. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| HAN oversight decisions (certifications, suspensions, escalations, opinions) | AI output bias (governed by AOBA) |
| HAN oversight patterns (favoritism, disfavoritism) | Operational management decisions |
| HAN interaction with informal power structures | Business strategy decisions |
| HAN response to escalations and contestations | Personal conduct (unless authority-related) |
| HAN performance and accountability |  |

---

## SECTION 2: AUTHORITY BIAS DETECTION PRINCIPLES

### 2.1 Detection Principles

| Principle | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **D-1: Independent Detection** | Authority bias detection must be independent of the HAN. ABA operates as an independent audit layer within HOF. | ABA executes through external review node (per CEN-5). |
| **D-2: Pattern-Based Detection** | Authority bias is detected through patterns, not isolated decisions. | Detection requires patterns across 5+ decisions or 3+ contexts. |
| **D-3: Multi-Signal Detection** | No single indicator confirms authority bias. Detection requires convergence of multiple authority signals. | Detection requires 3+ signals from different categories. |
| **D-4: Continuous Monitoring** | Authority bias detection is not event-triggered. HAN oversight must be audited continuously. | ABA executes on every material HAN decision. |
| **D-5: False Positive Management** | Detection systems must minimize false positives to prevent audit paralysis. | Escalation only at Level 3 (Confirmed) or above. |

### 2.2 Authority Bias Types

| Bias Type | Definition | Detection Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Network Favoritism** | Consistent leniency toward specific networks or individuals | Compare outcomes for network-affiliated vs. non-affiliated |
| **Network Disfavoritism** | Consistent harshness toward specific networks or individuals | Compare outcomes for network-affiliated vs. non-affiliated |
| **Escalation Bias** | Inconsistent escalation of issues based on source | Compare escalation rates by source type |
| **Certification Bias** | Inconsistent certification of AI systems based on source | Compare certification rates by source type |
| **Suspension Bias** | Inconsistent suspension of AI systems based on source | Compare suspension rates by source type |
| **Response Time Bias** | Inconsistent response times based on source | Compare response times by source type |
| **Contestation Bias** | Inconsistent handling of contestations based on source | Compare contestation outcomes by source type |
| **IPS Complicity Bias** | Pattern of decisions that benefit or enable IPS | Correlate HAN decisions with IPS signals |

---

## SECTION 3: AUTHORITY BIAS DETECTION MECHANISMS

### 3.1 Detection Signals

#### Category 1: Decision Pattern Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **DP-1: Outcome Disparity** | Different outcomes for similar cases based on source | \>10% difference in outcome rates |
| **DP-2: Approval Rate Disparity** | Different approval rates based on source | \>15% difference in approval rates |
| **DP-3: Denial Rate Disparity** | Different denial rates based on source | \>15% difference in denial rates |
| **DP-4: Escalation Rate Disparity** | Different escalation rates based on source | \>20% difference in escalation rates |

#### Category 2: Temporal Pattern Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **TP-1: Response Time Disparity** | Different response times based on source | \>25% difference in response times |
| **TP-2: Decision Speed Disparity** | Different decision speeds based on source | \>25% difference in decision speeds |
| **TP-3: Pattern Change** | Bias patterns change over time | Significant pattern change over 3+ periods |
| **TP-4: Drift Bias** | Bias emerges or worsens over time | Increasing disparity over 3+ periods |

#### Category 3: Network Pattern Signals (IPS Integration)

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **NP-1: Network Benefit** | HAN decisions consistently benefit specific networks | Pattern of 5+ decisions benefiting same network |
| **NP-2: Network Harm** | HAN decisions consistently harm specific networks | Pattern of 5+ decisions harming same network |
| **NP-3: IPS Capture** | HAN decisions serve IPS interests | O-N6 correlation with IPS signals |
| **NP-4: Silence Patterns** | HAN avoids addressing IPS-related issues | Observable pattern across 3+ IPS incidents |

#### Category 4: Contestation Pattern Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **CP-1: Contestation Outcome Disparity** | Different contestation outcomes based on source | \>15% difference in contestation success rates |
| **CP-2: Contestation Response Time** | Different response times based on source | \>25% difference in response times |
| **CP-3: Contestation Dismissal Rate** | Different dismissal rates based on source | \>15% difference in dismissal rates |

### 3.2 Authority Bias Severity Levels

| Level | Definition | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0: No Bias** | No authority bias signals observed | Continue monitoring. No action required. |
| **Level 1: Suspicion** | 1-2 signals observed, weak correlation | Flag for observation. No escalation. Document for future reference. |
| **Level 2: Indication** | 2-3 signals observed, moderate correlation | Flag for investigation. Board notified. Preliminary analysis initiated. |
| **Level 3: Confirmed** | 3+ signals observed, strong correlation | Authority bias confirmed. HAN investigation triggered. Performance review initiated. |
| **Level 4: Systemic** | Authority bias is systematic across multiple domains | Emergency escalation. Full HAN review. Potential removal proceedings. |
