| **ICC-10 I1, I2, I8, I10** | Constitutional parent. ABA enforces accountability, authority-responsibility separation, external legibility, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. ABA integrates IPS complicity detection with IPS signals. |
| **HAN Scorecard** | Performance evaluation. ABA findings feed into HAN Scorecard. |
| **AOBA** | Sibling instrument. ABA audits human authority bias; AOBA audits AI output bias. |
| **Board IPS Committee** | Governance. ABA escalates confirmed authority bias to Board IPS Committee. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. ABA contributes authority bias disclosure to ERDP. |
| **HIS-12** | Activity logging. ABA records authority bias audit logs in HIS-12. |
| **CEN-5** | Independence. ABA executes through independent review node. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"ABA is a built HOF derivative instrument that audits human authority bias across 11 detection signals, 8 bias types, 4 severity levels, and IPS complicity correlation—ensuring that human oversight is exercised fairly, consistently, and free from informal power structure influence, with escalation to the Board IPS Committee, logging in HIS-12, and disclosure via ERDP."*

---



# EXTERNAL LEGIBILITY & DISCLOSURE LAYER


# ERDP — EXTERNAL REPORTING & DISCLOSURE PROTOCOL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** External Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS External Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The External Reporting & Disclosure Protocol (ERDP) governs external reporting and disclosure obligations. It defines four disclosure tiers, an Annual Institutional Report, event-triggered reporting, and jurisdiction-aware disclosure requirements. ERDP ensures that AIGIS governance is externally legible, transparent, and accountable to stakeholders, regulators, and the public.

ERDP is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — information must be classifiable into defined disclosure tiers  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — actions must be mappable to external interpretation  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — IPS incidents must be disclosed  
- **CEN-1 to CEN-7** (Contestation Interface) — external contestation pathway

**Core insight:** Governance that is not externally legible is not legitimate. ERDP ensures that AIGIS operations are transparent to those with legitimate need, while protecting confidential information through structured disclosure tiers.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Disclosure Tier** | A classification level determining what information is disclosed to which audience. |
| **Material Action** | Any action satisfying M1–M3 from ICC-10 Section 4\. |
| **Event-Triggered Reporting** | Reporting triggered by specific events (I9 violation, IPS Level 3+, etc.). |
| **Jurisdictional Scope** | The legal jurisdictions within which disclosure is required. |
| **Annual Institutional Report (AIR)** | The annual external disclosure of governance health. |
| **Residual Gap Disclosure** | Honest disclosure of what the framework cannot yet enforce. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| External disclosure of governance information | Internal reporting (covered by IMP) |
| Regulatory reporting | Operational performance reporting |
| Stakeholder communication | Client-specific reporting (covered by CEF) |
| Public transparency |  |
| IPS incident disclosure |  |
| Residual gap disclosure |  |

---

## SECTION 2: DISCLOSURE TIERS

### 2.1 The Four Disclosure Tiers

| Tier | Name | Audience | Content Depth |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **T-PB** | Public | General public, media, civil society | Summary-level information; no sensitive details |
| **T-SH** | Stakeholder | Clients, partners, employees, affected communities | Moderate detail; sufficient for stakeholder understanding |
| **T-RG** | Regulator | Regulatory bodies, government agencies | Full detail; sufficient for regulatory oversight |
| **T-DS** | DFI/Sovereign | DFIs, sovereign counterparties, international bodies | Complete detail; sufficient for institutional oversight |

### 2.2 Tier Content Standards

| Content Category | T-PB | T-SH | T-RG | T-DS |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Constitutional Status** | Summary | Moderate | Full | Full |
| **Governance Health Indicators** | Summary | Moderate | Full | Full |
| **IPS Detection** | Summary | Moderate | Full | Full |
| **IPS Incidents** | Summary (Level 4 only) | Full (Level 3+) | Full | Full |
| **Bias Audit Results** | Summary | Moderate | Full | Full |
| **Capital Deployment** | Aggregate | By SPV | Full | Full |
| **Contestations** | Summary | Full | Full | Full |
| **Residual Gaps** | Summary | Full | Full | Full |

### 2.3 Tier Elevation Protocol

| Trigger | Elevation Action | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **DFI/Sovereign Request** | Elevate to T-DS upon formal request | Within 14 days |
| **Regulatory Request** | Elevate to T-RG upon formal request | Within 7 days |
| **Material Incident** | Elevate to appropriate tier based on severity | Within 24 hours |
| **IPS Level 3+** | Elevate to T-RG and T-DS | Within 72 hours |
| **I9 Violation** | Elevate to T-RG and T-DS | Within 24 hours |

---

## SECTION 3: ANNUAL INSTITUTIONAL REPORT (AIR)

### 3.1 AIR Structure

| Section | Content | Purpose |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Section 1: Executive Summary** | Summary of governance health and key metrics | High-level overview for all audiences |
| **Section 2: Constitutional Status** | ICC-10 compliance status; invariant coverage | Demonstrate constitutional compliance |
| **Section 3: Governance Health Indicators** | AICA-5 Measurement Framework results | Demonstrate governance health |
| **Section 4: IPS Detection and Mitigation** | IPS detection metrics, incidents, mitigation | Demonstrate anti-shadow resilience |
| **Section 5: Bias Audit Results** | AOBA and ABA results | Demonstrate fairness |
| **Section 6: Capital Deployment** | CDF and COF summary | Demonstrate capital governance |
| **Section 7: Contestations** | Contestation summary and resolutions | Demonstrate external legibility |
| **Section 8: Residual Gaps** | Honest disclosure of enforcement limitations | Demonstrate constitutional honesty |

### 3.2 AIR Publication Requirements

| Requirement | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **R-1: Annual Publication** | AIR must be published annually within 120 days of fiscal year-end | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-2: Tiered Access** | AIR must be accessible at all four disclosure tiers | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-3: Public Availability** | T-PB version must be publicly available | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-4: Regulatory Filing** | T-RG version must be filed with regulators | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-5: Stakeholder Distribution** | T-SH version must be distributed to stakeholders | ERDP Administrator |

---

## SECTION 4: EVENT-TRIGGERED REPORTING

### 4.1 Trigger Events

| Trigger Event | Disclosure Tier | Timeline | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **I9 Catastrophic Risk Violation** | T-RG, T-DS | Within 24 hours | Full incident details; impact assessment; containment actions |
| **IPS Level 3+ Confirmed** | T-RG, T-DS | Within 72 hours | IPS analysis; impact assessment; mitigation plan |
| **IPS Level 4 Systemic** | T-RG, T-DS | Within 24 hours | Full IPS analysis; systemic impact; stack review plan |
| **Constitutional Suspension** | T-RG, T-DS | Within 72 hours | Suspension details; resolution plan |
| **Material Bias Confirmed** | T-SH, T-RG, T-DS | Within 7 days | Bias analysis; affected populations; remediation plan |
| **Authority Bias Confirmed** | T-SH, T-RG, T-DS | Within 7 days | Authority bias analysis; HAN review; remediation plan |
| **Material Capital Loss** | T-SH, T-RG, T-DS | Within 7 days | Loss analysis; remediation plan; clawback status |
| **Contestation Escalation** | T-SH, T-RG, T-DS | Within 7 days | Contestation details; resolution status |

### 4.2 Reporting Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Event Detection** | Detect trigger event | AICA-5, O-N6, ADTEP |
| **2** | **Event Classification** | Classify event and determine disclosure tier | ERDP Administrator |

| **4** | **HAN Review** | Review report for accuracy and completeness | HAN |
| **5** | **Disclosure** | Disclose report to appropriate audiences | ERDP Administrator |
| **6** | **IMP Recording** | Record report in IMP | IMP |

| **Board Notification** | Required | Required |

## SECTION 6: JURISDICTIONAL SCOPE

### 6.1 Jurisdictional Scope Rule

**Statement:** Mandatory disclosure is required only within operating jurisdictions. The ERDP shall not impose disclosure obligations that violate local law or create undue burden.

**Violation:** Disclosure required outside operating jurisdictions.

**Enforcement:** ERDP Administrator maintains jurisdictional scope registry. Disclosure is limited to jurisdictions where AIGIS operates.

### 6.2 Jurisdiction Registry

| Jurisdiction | Disclosure Requirements | Regulatory Contact |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Philippines** | Full compliance with local laws | SEC, DICT, NPC |
| **EU (if applicable)** | GDPR compliance; EU AI Act reporting | EDPS, national authorities |
| **US (if applicable)** | State and federal compliance | FTC, state authorities |

---

## SECTION 7: RESIDUAL GAP DISCLOSURE

### 7.1 Statement

> *"ADTEP narrows the gap between normative and technical enforcement to the irreducible minimum of current LLM infrastructure capability. The residual gap is: An agent that receives full constitutional context can still produce outputs outside its authority if the underlying model does not refuse. ADTEP makes this detectable through pre-delivery logging, accountable through the audit chain, subject to the AWOF trigger system, and enforceable through Escalation Flags and Constitutional Suspension. It does not make violation impossible."*

### 7.2 Residual Gap Disclosure Requirements

| Requirement | Statement | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **R-1: AIR Inclusion** | Residual gap must be disclosed in AIR | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-2: Stakeholder Communication** | Residual gap must be communicated to stakeholders | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-3: Regulatory Filing** | Residual gap must be filed with regulators | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-4: Public Transparency** | Residual gap summary must be publicly available | ERDP Administrator |
| **R-5: Annual Review** | Residual gap must be reviewed annually for changes | ERDP Administrator \+ HAN |

---

### 8.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **ERDP Committee** | HAN, ERDP Administrator, Legal | Oversee ERDP implementation; approve disclosures |
| **Disclosure Review Board** | HAN, External Auditors, Legal | Review sensitive disclosures; ensure compliance |
| **Regulatory Liaison** | HAN, Legal, ERDP Administrator | Manage regulatory relationships and filings |

### 8.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **Event-Triggered Report** | Event-triggered | Tier-appropriate | Trigger event details |
| **IPS Disclosure** | Event-triggered | T-RG, T-DS | IPS incident details |
| **Regulatory Filing** | As required | Regulators | Compliance reporting |

---

### 9.1 Validation Requirements

An AIGIS deployment is ERDP-compliant if and only if:

1. **ERDP Framework adopted** — ERDP is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Disclosure Tiers** — Four disclosure tiers are defined and operational.  
3. **AIR** — Annual Institutional Report is published annually.  
4. **Event-Triggered Reporting** — Event-triggered reporting protocols are operational.  
5. **IPS Disclosure** — IPS disclosure requirements are operational.  
6. **Jurisdictional Scope** — Jurisdictional scope registry is maintained.  
7. **Residual Gap Disclosure** — Residual gap is disclosed in AIR.

> *"ERDP governs external reporting and disclosure through four tiers (Public, Stakeholder, Regulator, DFI/Sovereign), an Annual Institutional Report (AIR) with 8 sections, event-triggered reporting for I9 violations, IPS Level 3+ incidents, and material bias—including IPS disclosure, jurisdictional scope, and honest residual gap disclosure."*

# CIS — CONTESTATION INTERFACE STANDARD

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** External Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** ICC-10 (Intelligence Constitutional Charter)

---

## PREAMBLE

The Contestation Interface Standard (CIS) establishes a set of seven standards for the Contestation Interface: Interpretability, Accessibility, Traceability, Confidentiality Protocol, Independence, Redress & Remediation, and Public Contestation. It provides external legibility and a contestation pathway for institutional actions.

CIS is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — actions must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — contestation must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — contestation must resist IPS capture  
- **ICC-10 CEN-1 to CEN-7** — contestation standards

**Core insight:** An institution that is internally coherent but externally illegible fails the legitimacy condition. CIS ensures that all material institutional actions are externally contestable, auditable, and redressable.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Contestation** | A formal challenge to a material institutional action. |
| **Contestation Node** | The structural node responsible for receiving and processing contestations. |
| **Contestation Finding** | The result of a contestation review. |
| **Contestation Redress** | The corrective action resulting from a contestation. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Contestation standards | Contestation policy (governed by governance) |
| Contestation interface | Contestation execution (governed by operations) |
| Contestation processing | Contestation infrastructure (governed by operations) |

---

## SECTION 2: THE SEVEN STANDARDS

### 2.1 CEN-1: Interpretability

**Statement:** Any material institutional action must be describable in terms intelligible to an external party without access to internal systems or proprietary doctrine.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Plain Language** | Actions described in plain language | ERDP |
| **No Proprietary Dependency** | Description does not require internal access | ERDP |
| **Action Context** | Context included in description | ERDP |
| **Action Rationale** | Rationale included in description | ERDP |

**Violation:** Action described only in internal or proprietary terms.

**Enforcement:** ERDP requires plain language description for all contested actions.

---

### 2.2 CEN-2: Accessibility

**Statement:** A defined pathway must exist through which external parties—regulators, affected stakeholders, DFI counterparties—may raise questions, objections, or audit requests against material institutional actions.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Defined Pathway** | Formal contestation pathway exists | ERDP |
| **Public Access** | Pathway publicly accessible | ERDP |
| **Stakeholder Access** | Stakeholders have defined access | ERDP |
| **Regulator Access** | Regulators have defined access | ERDP |

**Violation:** No defined contestation pathway.

**Enforcement:** ERDP must publish contestation pathway.

---

### 2.3 CEN-3: Traceability

**Statement:** The contestation node must connect to the audit chain established under I3. External contestation must be capable of triggering internal reconstructability review.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Audit Chain Connection** | Contestation connects to audit chain | IMP |
| **Reconstructability Trigger** | Contestation triggers review | IMP |
| **Traceability** | Contestation traceable to audit chain | IMP |

**Violation:** Contestation not connected to audit chain.

**Enforcement:** IMP must support contestation traceability.

---

### 2.4 CEN-4: Confidentiality Protocol

**Statement:** Where a valid contest involves I6-restricted information, that information is not disclosed to the contesting party. An independent review node—structurally separate from both the originating authority and the contesting party—is granted full access and renders a finding. The contesting party receives the finding.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Restricted Information Protection** | I6-restricted information not disclosed | ERDP |
| **Independent Review Node** | Structurally separate review node | CIS |
| **Full Access** | Review node has full access | CIS |
| **Finding Delivery** | Contesting party receives finding | ERDP |

**Violation:** Restricted information disclosed to contesting party.

**Enforcement:** CIS confidentiality protocol enforced.

---

### 2.5 CEN-5: Independence

**Statement:** The contestation node must not be administered by any party that holds voting or blocking capacity in the originating authority. The originating authority may hold observation rights in contestation proceedings. Decision rights in contestation are prohibited for the originating authority.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Structural Independence** | Contestation node independent | CIS |
| **No Voting Rights** | Originating authority has no vote | CIS |
| **No Blocking Rights** | Originating authority has no block | CIS |
| **Observation Rights** | Originating authority may observe | CIS |

**Violation:** Originating authority holds decision rights.

**Enforcement:** CIS independence requirement enforced.

---

### 2.6 CEN-6: Redress and Remediation

**Statement:** A valid contestation finding must produce a defined corrective pathway. Corrective actions, including rollbacks via AICA-5 Ac-N4, are treated as proposed actions subject to CFL-V incremental verification and I7 continuity impact assessment before confirmation. Redress is not automatic.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Corrective Pathway** | Defined corrective actions | CIS |
| **CFL-V Verification** | Corrective actions CFL-V verified | CFL-V |
| **I7 Impact Assessment** | Continuity impact assessed | AICA-5 Ac-N4 |

**Violation:** No defined corrective pathway.

**Enforcement:** CIS redress protocol enforced.

---

### 2.7 CEN-7: Public Contestation

**Statement:** Any individual may submit a governance concern regarding AI actions affecting them. Submission is logged as OEO (`outcome_type: public_contestation`). 19 Integrated has 30 days to review and respond. Response is logged as GAO (`artifact_type: contestation_response`). If unresolved, escalates to HAN.

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Public Access** | Any individual may submit | ERDP |
| **OEO Logging** | Logged as OEO | IMP |
| **30-Day Review** | Response within 30 days | ERDP |
| **GAO Logging** | Response logged as GAO | IMP |
| **HAN Escalation** | Escalates to HAN if unresolved | ERDP |

**Violation:** Public contestation not processed.

**Enforcement:** ERDP must process public contestations.

---

## SECTION 3: CONTESTATION PROCESS

### 3.1 Contestation Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Contestation Submission** | External party submits contestation | External Party |
| **2** | **Contestation Receipt** | Contestation received and logged | ERDP |
| **3** | **Contestation Review** | Independent review node reviews | CIS |
| **4** | **Finding** | Finding rendered | CIS |
| **5** | **Redress** | Corrective pathway defined | CIS |
| **6** | **Resolution** | Contestation resolved | CIS |

### 3.2 Contestation Resolution Types

| Resolution | Description | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Upheld** | Contestation valid | Corrective action taken |
| **Partially Upheld** | Contestation partially valid | Partial corrective action |
| **Denied** | Contestation invalid | No action |
| **Escalated** | Escalated to HAN | HAN review |

---

## SECTION 4: CONTESTATION GOVERNANCE

### 4.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Contestation Committee** | HAN, CIS Administrator, External Review Node | Oversee contestation implementation |
| **Contestation Review Board** | HAN, Independent review node | Review contestation findings |

### 4.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Contestation Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Contestation Committee | Contestation metrics, trends |
| **Contestation Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators, Public | Contestation summary |

---

## SECTION 5: CIS VALIDATION

### 5.1 Validation Requirements

A CIS deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **CIS adopted** — Contestation Interface Standard is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Seven Standards** — All seven standards are defined.  
3. **Interpretability** — CEN-1 is operational.  
4. **Accessibility** — CEN-2 is operational.  
5. **Traceability** — CEN-3 is operational.  
6. **Confidentiality** — CEN-4 is operational.  
7. **Independence** — CEN-5 is operational.  
8. **Redress** — CEN-6 is operational.  
9. **Public Contestation** — CEN-7 is operational.  
10. **Contestation Process** — Contestation process is operational.

---

## SECTION 6: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I6, I8, I10** | Constitutional parent. CIS enforces transparency, external legibility, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. CIS uses ERDP for contestation disclosure. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. CIS logs contestations in IMP. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. CIS escalates unresolved contestations to HAN. |
| **AICA-5 Ac-N4** | Control architecture. CIS uses Ac-N4 for rollback. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"CIS establishes seven contestation standards (Interpretability, Accessibility, Traceability, Confidentiality Protocol, Independence, Redress & Remediation, Public Contestation)—ensuring all material institutional actions are externally contestable, auditable, and redressable, with defined pathways for regulators, stakeholders, and the public, independent review, confidentiality protection, and HAN escalation."*

---



# SECURITY & SUPPLY CHAIN LAYER


# AEGIS — AGENTIC SECURITY & IDENTITY

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Security/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Security Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

AEGIS applies Forrester's AEGIS principles to AI agents, treating them as a distinct identity class. It implements Least Agency, Mutual Authentication, and expanded IAM governance for agentic non-human identities. AEGIS ensures that AI agents are governed with the same security rigor as human identities, with additional safeguards for the unique risks of autonomous agents.

AEGIS is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — human override must exist for all processes  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — agents must resist IPS capture  
- **AWOF-IPS** — IPS-aware agent governance  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **Zero Trust** — zero trust principles applied to agents

**Core insight:** AI agents are not human identities, but they are not mere tools either. They are a distinct identity class that requires its own security governance—with Least Agency, Mutual Authentication, and continuous verification. AEGIS provides this governance.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Agentic Identity** | A digital identity representing an AI agent with distinct properties and governance requirements. |
| **Least Agency** | The principle that agents have only the minimum capabilities necessary to perform their declared functions. |
| **Mutual Authentication** | Authentication of both the agent and the system it is accessing. |
| **Agent IAM** | Identity and Access Management specifically designed for AI agents. |
| **Agent Credential** | A credential used by an agent to authenticate and authorize actions. |
| **Agent Session** | A bounded period of agent activity with defined start and end. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Agent identity governance | Human identity governance (covered by IAM) |
| Least Agency implementation | General cybersecurity (covered by security) |
| Mutual Authentication | Infrastructure security (covered by operations) |
| Agent IAM |  |
| Agent credential management |  |

---

## SECTION 2: LEAST AGENCY

### 2.1 Definition

**Least Agency Principle:** Agents must have only the minimum capabilities necessary to perform their declared functions. Capabilities must be explicitly granted, not implicitly inherited.

### 2.2 Implementation Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Capability Scoping** | Agent capabilities must be scoped to declared function | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **No Implicit Inheritance** | Agents do not inherit capabilities from other agents | ADTEP |
| **Explicit Grants** | All capabilities must be explicitly granted | ADTEP |
| **Time-Bound Grants** | Capabilities expire and must be renewed | ADTEP |
| **Revocation** | Capabilities can be revoked immediately | ADTEP |

### 2.3 Least Agency Tiers

| Tier | Capability Level | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Tier 1** | Observation Only | Read-only access, monitoring |
| **Tier 2** | Limited Action | Specific actions with approval |
| **Tier 3** | Agentic Action | Autonomous actions within boundaries |
| **Tier 4** | Full Autonomy | Autonomous actions with oversight |
| **Tier 5** | Institutional Action | Actions with constitutional authority |

---

## SECTION 3: MUTUAL AUTHENTICATION

### 3.1 Definition

**Mutual Authentication Principle:** Both the agent and the system it is accessing must authenticate each other. No one-way authentication is permitted for agentic actions.

### 3.2 Implementation Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Agent Authentication** | Agent must authenticate to system | Agent Credential |
| **System Authentication** | System must authenticate to agent | System Credential |
| **Session Binding** | Authentication is bound to session | ADTEP Session Initialization |
| **Re-authentication** | Re-authentication required periodically | ADTEP Constitutional Refresh |

### 3.3 Authentication Methods

| Method | Description | Applicability |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Token-Based** | Agent holds cryptographically signed token | All agents |
| **Certificate-Based** | Agent holds X.509 certificate | High-security agents |
| **Biometric Equivalent** | Agent behavior pattern authentication | Continuous verification |

---

## SECTION 4: AGENT IAM

### 4.1 Identity Lifecycle

| Phase | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Provisioning** | Agent identity created | AWOF Administrator |
| **Onboarding** | Agent identity activated | ADTEP |
| **Operation** | Agent identity used | Agent |
| **Offboarding** | Agent identity deactivated | ADTEP |
| **Retirement** | Agent identity destroyed | AWOF Administrator |

### 4.2 Identity Attributes

| Attribute | Description | Required |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Agent ID** | Unique agent identifier | Yes |
| **Function** | Agent's declared function | Yes |
| **Entity** | Agent's organizational entity | Yes |
| **Tier** | Agent's authority level | Yes |
| **IPS Awareness Level** | Agent's IPS awareness classification | Yes |
| **Capabilities** | Agent's declared capabilities | Yes |
| **Credentials** | Agent's authentication credentials | Yes |

---

## SECTION 5: AGENT CREDENTIAL MANAGEMENT

### 5.1 Credential Types

| Type | Description | Use Case |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Session Token** | Short-lived token for a session | Standard agent operations |
| **Long-Lived Credential** | Longer-lived credential for recurring operations | Routine agent operations |
| **One-Time Credential** | Single-use credential for specific actions | High-security actions |
| **Escalated Credential** | Higher-privilege credential for escalation | Escalation events |

### 5.2 Credential Lifecycle

| Phase | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Generation** | Credential generated | ADTEP |
| **Distribution** | Credential distributed to agent | ADTEP |
| **Use** | Credential used for authentication | Agent |
| **Rotation** | Credential rotated periodically | ADTEP |
| **Revocation** | Credential revoked immediately | ADTEP |

---

## SECTION 6: ZERO TRUST PRINCIPLES

### 6.1 Zero Trust Application

| Principle | Application to Agents | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Never Trust, Always Verify** | Agents are never trusted implicitly | Continuous verification |
| **Least Privilege** | Agents have minimum capabilities | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **Assume Breach** | Design assumes agents may be compromised | AWOF-IPS co-optation resistance |
| **Verify Explicitly** | All actions require explicit verification | ADTEP Pre-Delivery Log Entry |

### 6.2 Continuous Verification

| Verification Type | Frequency | Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Identity Verification** | Per-session | Mutual Authentication |
| **Capability Verification** | Per-action | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **Behavioral Verification** | Continuous | AWOF-IPS behavioral monitoring |
| **Integrity Verification** | Continuous | ADTEP integrity checks |

---

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **AEGIS Committee** | HAN, CIO, CISO, AWOF Administrator | Oversee AEGIS implementation |
| **Agent Identity Review Board** | HAN, External security experts | Review agent identity governance |
| **Credential Management Team** | ADTEP, CISO, AWOF Administrator | Manage agent credentials |

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |

## SECTION 8: AEGIS VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

An AEGIS deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **AEGIS Framework adopted** — AEGIS is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Least Agency** — Least Agency is implemented for all agents.  
3. **Mutual Authentication** — Mutual Authentication is implemented for all agent-system interactions.  
4. **Agent IAM** — Agent IAM lifecycle is operational.  
5. **Credential Management** — Credential management is operational.  
6. **Zero Trust** — Zero Trust principles are applied to all agents.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I4, I10** | Constitutional parent. AEGIS enforces control and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. AEGIS integrates IPS awareness into agent identity. |
| **AWOF-IPS** | Workforce governance. AEGIS aligns with AWOF-IPS agent classification. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. AEGIS uses ADTEP for enforcement. |
| **Zero Trust** | Security. AEGIS applies Zero Trust principles to agents. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. AEGIS is overseen by HAN. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. AEGIS records agent identity events in IMP. |

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"AEGIS applies Forrester's AEGIS principles to AI agents—implementing Least Agency, Mutual Authentication, Agent IAM lifecycle, credential management, and Zero Trust principles—ensuring agentic non-human identities are governed with security rigor equivalent to human identities, with additional safeguards for autonomous agent risks."*

# ZERO TRUST & AI RUNTIME SECURITY

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Security/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Security Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

Zero Trust & AI Runtime Security applies Zero Trust principles to AI agents at runtime. It integrates with AARM for pre-execution interception, tamper-evident receipts, and continuous verification. This ensures that AI agents operate within strict security boundaries at all times, with no implicit trust and continuous verification of every action.

Zero Trust & AI Runtime Security is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — human override must exist for all processes  
- **ICC-10 I9** (Catastrophic Risk Invariance) — catastrophic risks must be prevented  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — agents must resist IPS capture  
- **AEGIS** — agentic security principles  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement  
- **Zero Trust Architecture** — never trust, always verify

**Core insight:** Trust is a vulnerability. In AI runtime environments, implicit trust is catastrophic. Zero Trust ensures that every action is verified, every access is authenticated, and every execution is monitored—with tamper-evident receipts and continuous verification.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Zero Trust** | A security model that assumes no implicit trust, requiring verification for every action. |
| **AI Runtime** | The execution environment in which AI agents operate. |
| **Pre-Execution Interception** | Interception of actions before execution for verification. |
| **Tamper-Evident Receipt** | A cryptographically verifiable receipt for every action. |
| **Continuous Verification** | Ongoing verification of agent identity, capability, and behavior. |
| **AARM** | AI Action Runtime Monitoring — the monitoring system for AI agent actions. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Zero Trust application to AI runtime | General Zero Trust architecture (covered by security) |
| Pre-execution interception | Network security (covered by operations) |
| Tamper-evident receipts | Infrastructure security (covered by operations) |
| Continuous verification |  |
| AARM integration |  |

---

## SECTION 2: ZERO TRUST PRINCIPLES

### 2.1 Core Principles

| Principle | Application to AI Runtime | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Never Trust, Always Verify** | No implicit trust for any agent action | Continuous verification |
| **Least Privilege** | Agents have minimum necessary capabilities | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **Assume Breach** | Design assumes agents may be compromised | AWOF-IPS co-optation resistance |
| **Verify Explicitly** | All actions require explicit verification | ADTEP Pre-Delivery Log Entry |
| **Monitor Continuously** | All actions are monitored in real-time | AARM |

### 2.2 Zero Trust Implementation

| Implementation Layer | Function | Integration |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Identity Layer** | Agent identity verification | AEGIS |
| **Capability Layer** | Agent capability verification | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **Execution Layer** | Action verification before execution | Pre-Execution Interception |
| **Monitoring Layer** | Continuous action monitoring | AARM |
| **Verification Layer** | Post-execution verification | Tamper-Evident Receipts |

---

## SECTION 3: PRE-EXECUTION INTERCEPTION

### 3.1 Definition

**Pre-Execution Interception:** The interception of AI agent actions before execution for verification, ensuring that no action proceeds without passing verification gates.

### 3.2 Interception Gates

| Gate | Function | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Identity Gate** | Verify agent identity | AEGIS Mutual Authentication |
| **Capability Gate** | Verify agent has required capabilities | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **Constitutional Gate** | Verify action satisfies ICC-10 invariants | CFL-V |
| **IPS Gate** | Verify action does not enable IPS | O-N6 |
| **Risk Gate** | Verify action is within risk tolerance | Systemic Risk Framework |

### 3.3 Gate Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Action Initiation** | Agent initiates action |
| **2** | **Interception** | Action intercepted |
| **3** | **Identity Verification** | Verify agent identity |
| **4** | **Capability Verification** | Verify agent capabilities |
| **5** | **Constitutional Verification** | Verify ICC-10 compliance |
| **6** | **IPS Verification** | Verify no IPS enablement |
| **7** | **Risk Verification** | Verify within risk tolerance |
| **8** | **Execution** | Action proceeds or is blocked |

---

## SECTION 4: TAMPER-EVIDENT RECEIPTS

### 4.1 Definition

**Tamper-Evident Receipt:** A cryptographically verifiable receipt for every AI action, providing evidence of what was done, when, by whom, and with what authorization.

### 4.2 Receipt Structure

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **receipt\_id** | String | Yes | Unique receipt identifier |
| **action\_id** | String | Yes | Action identifier |
| **agent\_id** | String | Yes | Agent identifier |
| **timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | Action timestamp |
| **action\_description** | String | Yes | Description of action |
| **authorization\_chain** | Array | Yes | Authorization chain for action |
| **verification\_results** | Array | Yes | Results of pre-execution verification |
| **cryptographic\_hash** | String | Yes | Cryptographic hash of receipt |
| **signature** | String | Yes | Digital signature of receipt |

### 4.3 Receipt Lifecycle

| Phase | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Generation** | Receipt generated at action execution | Pre-Execution Interception |
| **Storage** | Receipt stored in IMP | IMP |
| **Verification** | Receipt verified by auditors | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Retirement** | Receipt retired per policy | IMP |

---

## SECTION 5: CONTINUOUS VERIFICATION

### 5.1 Verification Types

| Type | Frequency | Method |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Identity Verification** | Continuous | AEGIS Mutual Authentication |
| **Capability Verification** | Continuous | ADTEP Role Specification |
| **Behavioral Verification** | Continuous | AWOF-IPS behavioral monitoring |
| **Constitutional Verification** | Continuous | CFL-V |
| **IPS Verification** | Continuous | O-N6 |

### 5.2 Verification Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Identity Drift** | Agent identity changes | Any change |
| **Capability Drift** | Agent capabilities change | Any change |
| **Behavioral Drift** | Agent behavior deviates | \>2 standard deviations |
| **Constitutional Drift** | Actions violate ICC-10 | Any violation |
| **IPS Drift** | IPS signals detected | Any signal |

### 5.3 Verification Response

| Severity | Response | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Suspicion** | Flag for observation | AARM |
| **Indication** | Enhanced monitoring | AARM |
| **Confirmed** | Action blocked, HAN notified | Pre-Execution Interception |
| **Systemic** | Immediate shutdown, Board notified | Pre-Execution Interception |

---

## SECTION 6: AARM INTEGRATION

### 6.1 AARM Functions

| Function | Description | Integration |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Action Monitoring** | Monitor all agent actions | Pre-Execution Interception |
| **Drift Detection** | Detect drift from baseline | AWOF-IPS |
| **Alert Generation** | Generate alerts for anomalies | Real-Time Evidence Streaming |
| **Receipt Generation** | Generate tamper-evident receipts | Tamper-Evident Receipts |
| **Incident Response** | Respond to security incidents | ADTEP |

### 6.2 AARM Integration Points

| Integration | Function | Instrument |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Pre-Execution Interception** | Action verification | Zero Trust |
| **ADTEP** | Enforcement | ADTEP |
| **AWOF-IPS** | Behavioral monitoring | AWOF-IPS |
| **O-N6** | IPS detection | IPS Framework |
| **Real-Time Evidence Streaming** | Evidence generation | Real-Time Evidence Streaming |
| **IMP** | Evidence storage | IMP |

---

## SECTION 7: ZERO TRUST GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Zero Trust Committee** | HAN, CISO, CRO | Oversee Zero Trust implementation |
| **Security Review Board** | HAN, External security experts | Review security incidents and gaps |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Zero Trust Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Zero Trust Committee | Security metrics, incidents |
| **Zero Trust Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board, Regulators | Security incidents involving agents |

---

## SECTION 8: ZERO TRUST VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

A Zero Trust deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Zero Trust adopted** — Zero Trust is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Zero Trust Principles** — All Zero Trust principles are implemented.  
3. **Pre-Execution Interception** — Pre-execution interception is operational.  
4. **Tamper-Evident Receipts** — Tamper-evident receipts are generated for all actions.  
5. **Continuous Verification** — Continuous verification is operational.  
6. **AARM Integration** — AARM integration is operational.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I4, I9, I10** | Constitutional parent. Zero Trust enforces control, catastrophic risk, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **AEGIS** | Security. Zero Trust extends AEGIS with runtime controls. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Zero Trust uses ADTEP for enforcement. |
| **AWOF-IPS** | Workforce governance. Zero Trust uses AWOF-IPS for behavioral monitoring. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Zero Trust uses O-N6 for IPS detection. |
| **Real-Time Evidence Streaming** | Evidence. Zero Trust generates real-time evidence. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Zero Trust stores receipts in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. Zero Trust contributes security reporting to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Zero Trust & AI Runtime Security applies Zero Trust principles to AI agents at runtime—with pre-execution interception through Identity, Capability, Constitutional, IPS, and Risk gates; tamper-evident receipts for every action; continuous verification of identity, capability, behavior, and compliance; and full integration with AEGIS, ADTEP, AWOF-IPS, O-N6, Real-Time Evidence Streaming, and IMP."*

# SUPPLY CHAIN GOVERNANCE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Security/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Security Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

Supply Chain Governance establishes comprehensive governance for AI supply chains, including model provenance and third-party assessments. It ensures every component in the AI supply chain is governed, verified, and traceable—from training data through model development to deployment.

Supply Chain Governance is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — supply chain must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — supply chain must preserve structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I9** (Catastrophic Risk Invariance) — supply chain must not introduce catastrophic risks  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — supply chain must resist IPS capture  
- **Skills & Plugins Governance** — extension governance  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement

**Core insight:** AI systems are only as trustworthy as their supply chains. Every component—data, models, libraries, skills—must be governed, verified, and traceable. Supply Chain Governance ensures that the entire AI supply chain is constitutionally governed.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **AI Supply Chain** | The complete chain of components used in AI development and deployment. |
| **Model Provenance** | The complete history of a model from development to deployment. |
| **Third-Party Component** | Any component developed by an external party. |
| **Supplier IPS Clearance** | Verification that a supplier does not enable IPS capture. |
| **Supply Chain Risk** | A risk introduced through the supply chain. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Model provenance | Model development (covered by development) |
| Third-party assessments | Model deployment (covered by deployment) |
| Supplier IPS clearance | Infrastructure supply chain (covered by operations) |
| Supply chain risk management |  |
| Component lifecycle |  |

---

## SECTION 2: SUPPLY CHAIN COMPONENTS

### 2.1 Component Types

| Type | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Data Component** | Data used for training, validation, or inference | Training data, validation data, inference data |
| **Model Component** | AI model or algorithm | Foundation model, fine-tuned model, custom model |
| **Library Component** | Software library or framework | ML framework, data processing library |
| **Skill Component** | Skill or plugin | Claude Skill, GPT Store app |
| **Infrastructure Component** | Infrastructure component | Compute, storage, networking |
| **Service Component** | External service | API, cloud service |

### 2.2 Component Risk Levels

| Level | Description | Criteria |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1: Low Risk** | Minimal risk, well-known source | Open source, widely used |
| **Level 2: Moderate Risk** | Moderate risk, known source | Commercial, reputable vendor |
| **Level 3: High Risk** | High risk, unknown source | Novel, untested |
| **Level 4: Critical Risk** | Critical risk, constitutional relevance | Core model, critical data |

---

## SECTION 3: MODEL PROVENANCE

### 3.1 Provenance Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Source Verification** | Model source must be verified | Source verification record |
| **Training Data Provenance** | Training data must be traceable | Data provenance chain |
| **Training Process** | Training process must be documented | Training documentation |
| **Model Versioning** | Model versions must be tracked | Version history |
| **Deployment History** | Deployment history must be tracked | Deployment log |

### 3.2 Provenance Chain

| Phase | Component | Record |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Development** | Model development | Development logs |
| **Training** | Model training | Training logs, data provenance |
| **Validation** | Model validation | Validation results |
| **Deployment** | Model deployment | Deployment logs |
| **Operation** | Model operation | Operation logs |
| **Retirement** | Model retirement | Retirement logs |

### 3.3 Provenance Verification

| Verification | Method | Frequency |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Source Verification** | Verify source authenticity | Per component |
| **Data Verification** | Verify data provenance | Per dataset |
| **Integrity Verification** | Verify model integrity | Per model |
| **Chain Verification** | Verify provenance chain | Per model |

---

## SECTION 4: THIRD-PARTY ASSESSMENTS

### 4.1 Assessment Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Vendor Due Diligence** | Vendor must be vetted | Due diligence report |
| **Security Assessment** | Security posture must be assessed | Security assessment report |
| **IPS Assessment** | IPS risk must be assessed | IPS assessment report |
| **Compliance Assessment** | Compliance must be assessed | Compliance assessment report |
| **Contractual Controls** | Contracts must include governance provisions | Contract review |

### 4.2 Assessment Types

| Type | Description | Frequency |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Initial Assessment** | Assessment before onboarding | Per vendor |
| **Periodic Assessment** | Regular reassessment | Annual |
| **Event-Triggered Assessment** | Assessment after events | Per event |
| **Exit Assessment** | Assessment before offboarding | Per vendor |

### 4.3 Assessment Process

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Vendor Identification** | Identify potential vendor |
| **2** | **Due Diligence** | Conduct due diligence |
| **3** | **Risk Assessment** | Assess supply chain risk |
| **4** | **IPS Assessment** | Assess IPS risk |
| **5** | **Contract Negotiation** | Negotiate governance provisions |
| **6** | **Onboarding** | Onboard vendor |
| **7** | **Continuous Monitoring** | Monitor ongoing risk |

---

## SECTION 5: SUPPLIER IPS CLEARANCE

### 5.1 IPS Clearance Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **No IPS Capture** | Supplier must not enable IPS capture | O-N6 analysis |
| **No Network Benefit** | Supplier must not benefit specific networks | Network analysis |
| **No Shadow Value Flow** | Supplier must not enable shadow value flows | Value flow analysis |
| **No Informational Asymmetry** | Supplier must not create information asymmetry | Information flow analysis |

### 5.2 IPS Clearance Process

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Supplier Identification** | Identify supplier |
| **2** | **IPS Analysis** | Analyze IPS implications |
| **3** | **Clearance Decision** | Determine IPS clearance |
| **4** | **Clearance Recording** | Record clearance |
| **5** | **Continuous Monitoring** | Monitor IPS risk |

---

| Type | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Model Risk** | Risk from model vulnerabilities | Backdoors, bias, drift |
| **Data Risk** | Risk from data vulnerabilities | Poisoning, leakage, bias |
| **Library Risk** | Risk from library vulnerabilities | Vulnerabilities, backdoors |
| **Vendor Risk** | Risk from vendor vulnerabilities | Vendor compromise, vendor lock-in |
| **IPS Risk** | Risk from IPS infiltration | Capture, network benefit |

### 6.2 Risk Mitigation

| Risk Type | Mitigation | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Model Risk** | Model scanning, validation | AI Security Team |
| **Data Risk** | Data validation, provenance | Data Governance Team |
| **Library Risk** | Security scanning, updates | Security Team |
| **Vendor Risk** | Due diligence, monitoring | Supply Chain Team |
| **IPS Risk** | IPS screening, monitoring | IPS Analysis Team |

---

## SECTION 7: SUPPLY CHAIN GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Supply Chain Committee** | HAN, CISO, Supply Chain Lead | Oversee supply chain governance |
| **Supplier Review Board** | HAN, Supply Chain Team, IPS Analysis Team | Review supplier assessments |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Supply Chain Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Supply Chain Committee | Supply chain risk, supplier status |
| **Supply Chain Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Supply chain incidents |

---

## SECTION 8: SUPPLY CHAIN VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

A Supply Chain Governance deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Supply Chain Governance adopted** — Governance is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Component Types** — Component types and risk levels are defined.  
3. **Model Provenance** — Model provenance is operational.  
4. **Third-Party Assessments** — Third-party assessments are operational.  
5. **Supplier IPS Clearance** — Supplier IPS clearance is operational.  
6. **Risk Management** — Supply chain risk management is operational.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I5, I9, I10** | Constitutional parent. Governance enforces auditability, structural compliance, catastrophic risk, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Governance uses supplier IPS clearance. |
| **Skills & Plugins Governance** | Extension. Governance applies to skills and plugins. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Governance uses ADTEP for enforcement. |

## The One-Sentence Summary

# SKILLS & PLUGINS GOVERNANCE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Security/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Security Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

Skills & Plugins Governance establishes formal governance for AI "skills" and plugins—such as Claude Skills, GPT Store apps, and custom agentic extensions. It governs skills as software supply chain artifacts with security scanning and lifecycle management. This ensures that all AI extensions are governed with the same rigor as core AI systems.

Skills & Plugins Governance is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — skills must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — skills must be controllable  
- **ICC-10 I9** (Catastrophic Risk Invariance) — skills must not enable catastrophic risks  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — skills must resist IPS capture  
- **Supply Chain Governance** — supply chain security principles  
- **ADTEP** — technical enforcement

**Core insight:** Skills and plugins are the primary vector for AI extension and the primary vulnerability for AI compromise. They must be governed as software supply chain artifacts with formal security scanning, authorization, and lifecycle management.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Skill** | A discrete capability added to an AI agent (e.g., Claude Skill, GPT Store app). |
| **Plugin** | A software extension that adds functionality to an AI system. |
| **Skill Artifact** | The packaged skill or plugin code and metadata. |
| **Skill Registry** | A registry of all authorized skills and plugins. |
| **Skill Lifecycle** | The complete lifecycle from request to retirement. |
| **Skill Security Scan** | Automated security scanning of skill artifacts. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Skill and plugin governance | Core AI system governance (covered by AICA-5) |
| Skill security scanning | General software supply chain (covered by Supply Chain Governance) |
| Skill lifecycle management | Infrastructure security (covered by operations) |
| Skill authorization |  |
| Skill IPS screening |  |

---

## SECTION 2: SKILL CLASSIFICATION

### 2.1 Skill Types

| Type | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Internal Skill** | Developed internally for internal use | Internal automation, data processing |
| **Commercial Skill** | Commercially acquired skill | GPT Store app, marketplace skill |
| **Open Source Skill** | Open source skill | Community-developed skill |
| **Custom Skill** | Custom-developed for specific use | Client-specific skill |

### 2.2 Skill Risk Levels

| Level | Description | Criteria |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 1: Low Risk** | Minimal capability, low sensitivity | Read-only, no external access |
| **Level 2: Moderate Risk** | Moderate capability, moderate sensitivity | Write access, limited external access |
| **Level 3: High Risk** | High capability, high sensitivity | Autonomous action, external access |
| **Level 4: Critical Risk** | Critical capability, constitutional relevance | Constitutional authority, IPS relevance |

---

## SECTION 3: SKILL LIFECYCLE

### 3.1 Lifecycle Phases

| Phase | Activity | Responsible | Key Controls |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Request** | Skill requested | User | Request form, justification |
| **Review** | Skill reviewed | Skill Review Board | Security review, IPS screening |
| **Development** | Skill developed | Developer | Secure development, testing |
| **Security Scan** | Skill scanned | Security Team | Automated scanning, vulnerability assessment |
| **Authorization** | Skill authorized | HAN | Authorization decision |
| **Deployment** | Skill deployed | Operations | Controlled deployment |
| **Monitoring** | Skill monitored | AARM | Continuous monitoring, drift detection |
| **Update** | Skill updated | Developer | Version control, re-scanning |
| **Retirement** | Skill retired | Operations | Controlled retirement |

### 3.2 Lifecycle Controls

| Control | Phase | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Request Control** | Request | Ensure legitimate need |
| **Security Control** | Review, Security Scan | Ensure security compliance |
| **IPS Control** | Review | Ensure no IPS enablement |
| **Authorization Control** | Authorization | Ensure proper authorization |
| **Monitoring Control** | Monitoring | Ensure ongoing compliance |
| **Update Control** | Update | Ensure updates are secure |
| **Retirement Control** | Retirement | Ensure secure retirement |

| Result | Action | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |

| **Conditional Pass** | Skill approved with conditions | Security Team \+ HAN |
| **Fail** | Skill rejected | Security Team |
| **Critical Fail** | Skill blocked, investigation triggered | Security Team \+ HAN |

# MCP SERVER GOVERNANCE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Security/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Security Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

MCP Server Governance establishes a system of record for Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers. It registers, authorizes, and governs MCP servers as a new software supply chain risk. This ensures that MCP servers—which provide context and capabilities to AI agents—are governed with the same rigor as AI models and skills.

MCP Server Governance is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — MCP servers must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I4** (Control Invariance) — MCP servers must be controllable  
- **ICC-10 I9** (Catastrophic Risk Invariance) — MCP servers must not introduce catastrophic risks  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — MCP servers must resist IPS capture  
- **Supply Chain Governance** — supply chain security principles  
- **Skills & Plugins Governance** — extension governance

**Core insight:** MCP servers are the connective tissue of AI agent ecosystems. They provide context, tools, and capabilities to agents. If MCP servers are ungoverned, they become the primary vector for supply chain attacks, data leakage, and IPS infiltration.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **MCP Server** | A server implementing the Model Context Protocol, providing context and capabilities to AI agents. |
| **MCP Registry** | A system of record for all authorized MCP servers. |
| **MCP Authorization** | Formal authorization for an MCP server to operate. |
| **MCP Risk Level** | A classification of MCP server risk. |
| **MCP Lifecycle** | The complete lifecycle of an MCP server from registration to retirement. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| MCP server registration | MCP protocol implementation (covered by development) |
| MCP server authorization | MCP client governance (covered by client governance) |
| MCP server risk assessment | MCP network infrastructure (covered by operations) |
| MCP server lifecycle |  |
| MCP server IPS screening |  |

# RISK & COMPLIANCE LAYER


# SYSTEMIC RISK FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Risk Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Risk Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Systemic Risk Framework establishes a structured approach for managing systemic AI risks that can destabilize institutions. It classifies, assesses, and governs systemic risks like multi-agent interactions, large-scale hallucinations, and informal power structures (IPS). This framework ensures that risks with the potential to cascade across the entire AIGIS stack are identified, monitored, and mitigated with constitutional rigor.

The Systemic Risk Framework is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — systemic risks must not break structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — systemic risks must not threaten institutional continuity  
- **ICC-10 I9** (Catastrophic Risk Invariance) — catastrophic risks must be prevented  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — IPS is a systemic risk  
- **AICA-5 Co-N2** (Drift Detection) — systemic risk detection  
- **AICA-5 Ac-N4** (Rollback Pathways) — systemic risk remediation

**Core insight:** Systemic risks are not just larger versions of ordinary risks. They are risks that can cascade across the entire stack, breaking dependency chains and causing institutional collapse. They require distinct detection, assessment, and mitigation protocols.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Systemic Risk** | A risk that can cascade across the entire AIGIS stack, breaking dependency chains and causing institutional collapse. |
| **Cascade Failure** | A failure in one layer that triggers failures in downstream layers, per AICA-5 dependency chain. |
| **Systemic Risk Event** | An event that triggers systemic risk protocols. |
| **Systemic Risk Register** | A registry of all identified systemic risks. |
| **Systemic Risk Level** | A classification of systemic risk severity from Level 0 to Level 4\. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Systemic risk identification | Ordinary operational risks |
| Systemic risk classification | Individual agent risks |
| Systemic risk monitoring | Client-specific risks |
| Systemic risk mitigation |  |
| IPS as systemic risk |  |
| Cascade failure prevention |  |

---

## SECTION 2: SYSTEMIC RISK TYPES

### 2.1 Risk Types

| Risk Type | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Multi-Agent Interaction Risk** | Risks arising from interactions between multiple agents | Coalition drift, collective hallucination, agent collusion |
| **Cascade Failure Risk** | Risks of failures cascading across AICA-5 layers | Cognitive layer failure triggering execution layer failure |
| **Constitutional Drift Risk** | Risks of constitutional invariants weakening over time | I10 erosion, I3 degradation |
| **IPS Systemic Risk** | Risks from informal power structures achieving systemic capture | IPS Level 4 |
| **Infrastructure Failure Risk** | Risks from infrastructure failure | Compute failure, network failure |
| **Model Failure Risk** | Risks from model-level failures | Catastrophic hallucination, model collapse |

### 2.2 IPS as Systemic Risk

**Classification:** IPS Level 4 (Systemic) is classified as a systemic risk.

| IPS Level | Classification | Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0-2** | Not systemic | Ordinary IPS protocols apply |
| **Level 3: Confirmed** | High risk | Enhanced monitoring; escalation to HAN |
| **Level 4: Systemic** | Systemic risk | Full stack review; potential stack reset |

---

## SECTION 3: SYSTEMIC RISK DETECTION

### 3.1 Detection Signals

| Signal | Indicator | Threshold |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Cascade Pattern** | Failure patterns moving across layers | Failure crosses 2+ layers |
| **Concentration Risk** | Risk concentrated in critical nodes | \>40% risk in \<10% of nodes |
| **Network Centralization** | Risk networks centered on specific nodes | Centralization score \>0.7 |
| **Drift Acceleration** | Drift accelerating over time | Accelerating drift over 3+ periods |
| **IPS Systemic** | IPS achieving systemic capture | IPS Level 4 confirmed |

### 3.2 Detection Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Frequency |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Signal Monitoring** | Monitor for systemic risk signals | AICA-5 |
| **2** | **Signal Analysis** | Analyze signals for systemic patterns | Systemic Risk Team |
| **3** | **Classification** | Classify systemic risk level | Systemic Risk Team |
| **4** | **Escalation** | Escalate if Level 3+ | Systemic Risk Team → HAN |

---

## SECTION 4: SYSTEMIC RISK CLASSIFICATION

### 4.1 Risk Levels

| Level | Name | Description | Response |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Level 0** | No Risk | No systemic risk detected | Continue monitoring |
| **Level 1** | Low | Low systemic risk potential | Flag for observation |
| **Level 2** | Moderate | Moderate systemic risk potential | Enhanced monitoring |
| **Level 3** | High | High systemic risk potential | Escalation to HAN; mitigation planning |
| **Level 4** | Systemic | Systemic risk confirmed | Full stack review; potential stack reset |

### 4.2 Classification Criteria

| Criteria | Level 1 | Level 2 | Level 3 | Level 4 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Cascade Potential** | Low | Moderate | High | Critical |
| **Constitutional Impact** | Low | Moderate | High | Critical |
| **Continuity Impact** | Low | Moderate | High | Critical |
| **IPS Severity** | Level 1-2 | Level 2 | Level 3 | Level 4 |
| **Mitigation Feasibility** | High | Moderate | Low | Very Low |

---

## SECTION 5: SYSTEMIC RISK MITIGATION

### 5.1 Mitigation Tools

| Tool | Function | Applicability |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Dependency Chain Analysis** | Analyze and strengthen dependency chains | All levels |
| **Redundancy Implementation** | Add redundancy to critical nodes | Level 2+ |
| **Isolation** | Isolate at-risk components | Level 3+ |
| **Rollback** | Rollback to stable state | Level 3+ |
| **Stack Reset** | Full stack reconstruction | Level 4 |

### 5.2 Mitigation Protocol

| Phase | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Phase 1: Containment** | Prevent systemic risk from expanding | HAN \+ Systemic Risk Team | 24-72 hours |
| **Phase 2: Analysis** | Complete systemic risk analysis | Systemic Risk Team | 30 days |
| **Phase 3: Planning** | Develop mitigation strategy | HAN \+ Systemic Risk Team | 15 days |
| **Phase 4: Implementation** | Execute mitigation | HAN \+ Operational Teams | 90 days |
| **Phase 5: Monitoring** | Monitor for recurrence | Systemic Risk Team | Ongoing |

---

## SECTION 6: SYSTEMIC RISK REGISTER

### 6.1 Register Content

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **risk\_id** | String | Yes | Unique risk identifier |
| **risk\_type** | Enum | Yes | Risk type from Section 2.1 |
| **risk\_level** | Integer (0-4) | Yes | Systemic risk level |
| **description** | String | Yes | Risk description |
| **impact\_assessment** | Object | Yes | Impact on ICC-10 invariants |
| **detection\_timestamp** | DateTime | Yes | When risk was detected |
| **mitigation\_status** | Object | Yes | Mitigation status |
| **closure\_timestamp** | DateTime | Conditional | When risk was closed |

### 6.2 Register Update Protocol

| Trigger | Update Action | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **New Risk Detected** | Add to register | Systemic Risk Team |
| **Risk Escalation** | Update level | Systemic Risk Team → HAN |
| **Risk Mitigation** | Update status | Systemic Risk Team |
| **Risk Closure** | Update closure | Systemic Risk Team → HAN |

---

## SECTION 7: SYSTEMIC RISK GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Systemic Risk Committee** | HAN, CRO, Systemic Risk Team | Oversee systemic risk management |
| **Systemic Risk Review Board** | HAN, Board, External experts | Review systemic risk incidents |
| **Cascade Prevention Team** | AICA-5, ADTEP, Systemic Risk Team | Prevent cascade failures |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Systemic Risk Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Systemic Risk Committee | Systemic risk metrics, trends |
| **Systemic Risk Incident Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board, Regulators | Systemic risk incidents |
| **Systemic Risk Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Summary of systemic risk management |

---

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

1. **Systemic Risk Framework adopted** — Framework is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Systemic Risk Types** — All risk types are defined.  
3. **IPS as Systemic Risk** — IPS Level 4 is classified as systemic risk.  
4. **Detection Signals** — Detection signals are instrumented.  
5. **Risk Classification** — Risk classification levels are operational.  
6. **Mitigation Protocol** — Mitigation protocol is operational.  
7. **Systemic Risk Register** — Register is operational.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I5, I7, I9, I10** | Constitutional parent. Framework enforces structural compliance, continuity, catastrophic risk, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. IPS Level 4 is classified as systemic risk. |
| **AICA-5** | Control architecture. Framework uses Co-N2 and Ac-N4 for detection and remediation. |
| **ADTEP** | Technical enforcement. Framework uses ADTEP for containment. |

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Systemic Risk Framework classifies, detects, and mitigates systemic AI risks—including multi-agent interactions, cascade failures, constitutional drift, and IPS systemic capture—through four risk levels, seven risk types, a Systemic Risk Register, and full integration with ICC-10 I5, I7, I9, I10, AICA-5, ADTEP, and HAN."*

# MCR — MASTER CONTROL REGISTRY

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

The Master Control Registry (MCR) is a unified control set of the AIGIS Compliance Engine. It normalizes every AIGIS control into a single registry with consistent identifiers, descriptions, and regulatory mappings. The MCR ensures that all governance controls are traceable, auditable, and mappable to external regulatory frameworks.

The MCR is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — all controls must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — controls must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — controls must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — controls must resist IPS capture

**Core insight:** Governance without a unified control registry is unmanageable. The MCR ensures that every control is uniquely identified, consistently described, and traceable to both constitutional invariants and external regulatory requirements.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Control** | A specific governance requirement or safeguard enforced by the AIGIS stack. |
| **Control ID** | A unique identifier for each control in the registry. |
| **Control Description** | A clear, concise description of the control's purpose and function. |
| **Control Source** | The instrument or layer from which the control originates. |
| **Regulatory Mapping** | A mapping from AIGIS controls to external regulatory frameworks. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Control identification and registration | Operational execution of controls |
| Control description and documentation | Control implementation details |
| Regulatory mapping | Control performance metrics |
| IPS controls |  |
| I10 controls |  |

---

## SECTION 2: CONTROL REGISTRY STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Control ID Format

**Format:** `AIGIS-[Layer]-[Instrument]-[Number]`

| Component | Description | Example |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **AIGIS** | Stack identifier | AIGIS |
| **Layer** | AIGIS layer | CONST, CTRL, WF, OPS, SEC, EXT |
| **Instrument** | Instrument abbreviation | ICC, HOF, AICA, ADTEP, IPS |
| **Number** | Sequential number | 001, 002, 003 |

**Examples:**

- `AIGIS-CONST-ICC-001` — I1 Accountability Invariance  
- `AIGIS-CTRL-AICA-014` — O-N6 Shadow Network Detection  
- `AIGIS-WF-AWOF-008` — IPS Awareness Level 3 Requirement

### 2.2 Control Registry Fields

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **control\_id** | String | Yes | Unique control identifier |
| **control\_name** | String | Yes | Short control name |
| **control\_description** | String | Yes | Detailed control description |
| **control\_source** | String | Yes | Instrument or layer of origin |
| **control\_type** | Enum | Yes | Constitutional, Operational, Technical, Security |
| **icc\_invariant** | String | Yes | ICC-10 invariant enforced |
| **ips\_relevance** | Boolean | Yes | Whether control addresses IPS |
| **regulatory\_mappings** | Array | Yes | External regulatory mappings |
| **status** | Enum | Yes | Active, Pending, Deprecated |

5. **Regulatory Mapping** — Mappings to EU AI Act, NIST AI RMF, and ISO 42001 are operational.

# COMPLIANCE CROSSWALK ENGINE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

The Compliance Crosswalk Engine is the interactive mapping layer that translates AIGIS controls into the language of regulatory frameworks. It provides bidirectional mapping from control to clause and from clause to control. This ensures that AIGIS governance can be demonstrated as compliant with external regulatory requirements without subordinating institutional governance to any external framework.

The Compliance Crosswalk Engine is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — compliance must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — compliance must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — compliance must resist IPS capture  
- **ICC-10 Module 8** (Regulatory Correspondence Crosswalk) — translation layer  
- **MCR** (Master Control Registry) — unified control registry

**Core insight:** External compliance is not governance. Governance is constitutional. The Compliance Crosswalk Engine translates governance into compliance, not the reverse. It demonstrates that AIGIS constitutional requirements satisfy external regulatory requirements, not that external requirements govern AIGIS.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Bidirectional Mapping** | A mapping from AIGIS controls to regulatory frameworks and from regulatory frameworks to AIGIS controls. |
| **Direct Mapping** | A clear, unambiguous correspondence between an AIGIS control and a regulatory requirement. |
| **Partial Mapping** | A partial correspondence that may require additional controls to fully satisfy the regulatory requirement. |
| **Interpretive Mapping** | A mapping that requires interpretation to demonstrate equivalence. |
| **Control-to-Clause** | Mapping from AIGIS controls to regulatory clauses. |
| **Clause-to-Control** | Mapping from regulatory clauses to AIGIS controls. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Bidirectional mapping between AIGIS controls and regulatory frameworks | Regulatory interpretation (governed by legal) |
| Control-to-Clause mapping | Compliance implementation (governed by operational teams) |
| Clause-to-Control mapping | Compliance certification (governed by CGF) |
| Regulatory Overlay integration |  |
| I10 and IPS mapping |  |

---

## SECTION 2: MAPPING STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Mapping Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **mapping\_id** | String | Yes | Unique mapping identifier |
| **direction** | Enum | Yes | Control-to-Clause or Clause-to-Control |
| **aigis\_control\_id** | String | Yes | AIGIS control identifier (from MCR) |
| **framework** | Enum | Yes | EU AI Act, NIST AI RMF, ISO 42001 |
| **framework\_reference** | String | Yes | Framework-specific reference |
| **mapping\_type** | Enum | Yes | Direct, Partial, Interpretive |
| **confidence** | Enum | Yes | High, Medium, Low |
| **justification** | String | Yes | Justification for the mapping |
| **status** | Enum | Yes | Active, Pending Review, Deprecated |

### 2.2 Mapping Types

| Type | Definition | Example |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Direct** | Clear, unambiguous correspondence | AIGIS-CONST-ICC-001 ↔ EU AI Act Art. 14 |
| **Partial** | Partial correspondence requiring additional controls | AIGIS-CONST-ICC-010 ↔ EU AI Act Art. 9 (partial) |
| **Interpretive** | Interpretation required for equivalence | AIGIS-CONST-ICC-010 ↔ NIST AI RMF Govern/Manage (interpretive) |

# REGULATORY OVERLAYS

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

Regulatory Overlays are authoritative mappings between AIGIS controls and specific regulatory frameworks. They operationalize regulatory compliance within the AIGIS Compliance Engine by providing jurisdiction-aware, framework-specific mappings that translate AIGIS constitutional requirements into the language of external regulators.

Regulatory Overlays are derived from:

- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — compliance must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — compliance must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 Module 8** (Regulatory Correspondence Crosswalk) — translation layer  
- **MCR** (Master Control Registry) — unified control registry  
- **Compliance Crosswalk Engine** — bidirectional mapping

**Core insight:** Global AI governance is fragmented. Different jurisdictions have different regulatory philosophies, risk classifications, and enforcement mechanisms. AIGIS must be able to demonstrate compliance across all relevant frameworks without subordinating its constitutional governance to any single external framework.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Regulatory Overlay** | An authoritative mapping between AIGIS controls and a specific regulatory framework. |
| **Jurisdiction** | A geographic or political entity with its own AI governance requirements. |
| **Framework** | A specific regulatory instrument, standard, or guideline. |
| **Overlay Status** | The operational status of a regulatory overlay (Active, Pending, Deprecated). |
| **Jurisdictional Scope** | The jurisdictions within which a specific overlay applies. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Regulatory framework mappings | Legal interpretation (governed by legal) |
| Jurisdiction-aware compliance | Compliance implementation (governed by operational teams) |
| Framework-specific controls | Compliance certification (governed by CGF) |
| Overlay activation and deactivation |  |

## SECTION 3: OVERLAY STRUCTURE

### 3.1 Overlay Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **overlay\_id** | String | Yes | Unique overlay identifier |
| **framework\_name** | String | Yes | Name of the regulatory framework |
| **jurisdiction** | String | Yes | Applicable jurisdiction |
| **framework\_version** | String | Yes | Framework version |
| **effective\_date** | DateTime | Yes | Date overlay becomes effective |
| **deprecation\_date** | DateTime | Conditional | Date overlay is deprecated |
| **mapping\_status** | Enum | Yes | Active, Pending, Deprecated |
| **mappings** | Array | Yes | AIGIS control to framework clause mappings |

### 3.2 Overlay Components

| Component | Description | Required |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Control-to-Clause Mapping** | Maps AIGIS controls to framework requirements | Yes |
| **Clause-to-Control Mapping** | Maps framework requirements to AIGIS controls | Yes |
| **Gap Analysis** | Identifies framework requirements not covered by AIGIS controls | Yes |
| **Interpretive Guidance** | Guidance on interpreting mappings | Conditional |
| **Jurisdictional Notes** | Notes on jurisdiction-specific requirements | Conditional |
