## SECTION 14: REGULATORY OVERLAY VALIDATION

### 14.1 Validation Requirements

A Regulatory Overlay deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Regulatory Overlays adopted** — Overlays are integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Framework Coverage** — All applicable frameworks have overlays.  
3. **Jurisdictional Mapping** — Jurisdictional mappings are operational.  
4. **Control-to-Clause Mapping** — Control-to-Clause mappings are operational.  
5. **Clause-to-Control Mapping** — Clause-to-Control mappings are operational.  
6. **Gap Analysis** — Gap analysis is operational.  
7. **Overlay Status** — Overlay status is maintained.

---

## SECTION 15: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I6, I8, I10** | Constitutional parent. Overlays enforce transparency, external legibility, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Overlays map IPS controls to regulatory frameworks. |
| **MCR** | Control registry. Overlays use MCR for control identification. |
| **Compliance Crosswalk Engine** | Mapping. Overlays provide authoritative mappings for the Crosswalk. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. Overlays contribute compliance reporting to ERDP. |

> *"Regulatory Overlays provide authoritative mappings between AIGIS controls and 30+ regulatory frameworks across EU, US, China, Singapore, Japan, Middle East, African Union, Philippines, and ASEAN—including EU AI Act, NIST AI RMF, ISO 42001, China GenAI Interim Measures, Singapore Model AI Governance Framework for Agentic AI, Japan AI Guidelines v1.2, UAE Charter, SDAIA Principles, AU Continental AI Strategy, and Philippines National AI Strategy—ensuring jurisdiction-aware compliance without subordinating constitutional governance."*

# AUDIT EVIDENCE ENGINE

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

The Audit Evidence Engine is the automated evidence packaging system that extracts, organizes, and packages AIGIS governance artifacts into audit-ready evidence packets. It answers: *How does AIGIS prove compliance to auditors, regulators, and stakeholders without manual evidence collection?*

The Audit Evidence Engine is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — all actions must be reconstructable  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — evidence must be classifiable into disclosure tiers  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — evidence must be externally legible  
- **IMP** (Institutional Memory Protocol) — source of evidence artifacts  
- **ERDP** (External Reporting & Disclosure Protocol) — disclosure of evidence

**Core insight:** Audit readiness is not a manual process. It is a constitutional requirement. The Audit Evidence Engine ensures that every governance action produces durable, auditable evidence that can be packaged and presented to external auditors without disrupting operations.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Audit Evidence Packet** | A structured collection of evidence artifacts organized for a specific audit purpose. |
| **Evidence Artifact** | A specific piece of evidence (IMP object, log entry, report, etc.). |
| **Audit Trail** | A complete, chronological record of evidence artifacts. |
| **Evidence Chain** | A linked sequence of evidence artifacts demonstrating a governance process. |
| **Evidence Integrity** | The assurance that evidence has not been altered or tampered with. |
| **Evidence Timeliness** | The assurance that evidence is current and complete. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Evidence extraction and packaging | Evidence interpretation (governed by auditors) |
| Evidence organization and indexing | Evidence policy (governed by governance) |
| Evidence delivery and presentation | Evidence storage (governed by IMP) |
| Evidence integrity verification |  |

---

## SECTION 2: EVIDENCE SOURCES

### 2.1 Primary Evidence Sources

| Source | Description | Evidence Types |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IMP** | Institutional Memory Protocol | State objects, provenance chains, logs |
| **ADTEP** | Technical Enforcement Protocol | Session logs, escalation flags, suspension records |
| **HAN/HOF** | Human Authority | HAN decisions, scorecards, trigger register |
| **AICA-5** | Control Architecture | Control node logs, drift detection, health indicators |
| **IPS Framework** | Informal Power Structure | IPS detection logs, severity classifications, mitigation records |
| **AOBA** | AI Output Bias Audit | Bias audit logs, detection signals, severity classifications |
| **ABA** | Authority Bias Audit | Authority bias logs, detection signals, severity classifications |
| **CDF** | Capital Deployment | Capital deployment records, conditions verification |
| **COF** | Capital Orchestration | Capital orchestration decisions, feedback records |
| **CEF/CEF-IPS** | Client Engagement | Client IPS risk assessments, engagement records, mandate clauses |

### 2.2 Evidence Chain Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Completeness** | Evidence chains must be complete with no gaps | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Immutability** | Evidence chains must be cryptographically verifiable | IMP |
| **Traceability** | Evidence chains must trace to source and authorization | IMP |
| **Timeliness** | Evidence chains must include timestamps | Audit Evidence Engine |

---

## SECTION 3: EVIDENCE PACKET STRUCTURE

### 3.1 Packet Schema

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **packet\_id** | String | Yes | Unique packet identifier |
| **packet\_type** | Enum | Yes | Compliance, Regulatory, Internal, Incident |
| **purpose** | String | Yes | Purpose of the evidence packet |
| **scope** | String | Yes | Scope of the evidence packet |
| **time\_period** | Object | Yes | Time period covered |
| **evidence\_artifacts** | Array | Yes | List of evidence artifacts |
| **evidence\_chains** | Array | Yes | Linked evidence chains |
| **integrity\_hash** | String | Yes | Cryptographic hash of packet |
| **disclosure\_tier** | Enum | Yes | ERDP disclosure tier |

### 3.2 Packet Types

| Type | Description | Use Case |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Compliance Packet** | Evidence of constitutional compliance | Annual compliance reporting |
| **Regulatory Packet** | Evidence for regulatory submission | Regulatory filing, EU AI Act conformity |
| **Internal Packet** | Evidence for internal audit | Internal governance review |
| **Incident Packet** | Evidence for incident investigation | IPS incident, I9 violation |
| **Contestation Packet** | Evidence for contestation resolution | CEN-1 to CEN-7 contestations |

---

## SECTION 4: EVIDENCE PACKAGING PROCESS

### 4.1 Packaging Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Audit Request** | Request for audit evidence | Auditor / Regulator |
| **2** | **Scope Definition** | Define scope of evidence packet | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **3** | **Evidence Extraction** | Extract evidence from sources | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **4** | **Evidence Organization** | Organize evidence into chains | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **5** | **Packet Assembly** | Assemble evidence packet | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **6** | **Integrity Verification** | Verify cryptographic integrity | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **7** | **HAN Review** | HAN reviews packet | HAN |
| **8** | **Delivery** | Deliver packet to auditor/regulator | Audit Evidence Engine |

### 4.2 Evidence Extraction Rules

| Rule | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Complete Coverage** | Extract all evidence within scope | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Chain Preservation** | Preserve evidence chain integrity | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Source Verification** | Verify evidence source authenticity | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Timeliness** | Evidence must be current as of request | Audit Evidence Engine |

---

## SECTION 5: EVIDENCE INTEGRITY

### 5.1 Integrity Mechanisms

| Mechanism | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Cryptographic Hashing** | Evidence artifacts are cryptographically hashed | IMP |
| **Audit Trails** | All evidence access is logged | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Chain of Custody** | Evidence chain of custody is maintained | Audit Evidence Engine |
| **Verification** | Evidence integrity is verifiable by auditors | Audit Evidence Engine |

### 5.2 Integrity Verification

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Hash Verification** | Verify cryptographic hash |
| **2** | **Chain Verification** | Verify evidence chain |
| **3** | **Source Verification** | Verify source authenticity |
| **4** | **Timeliness Verification** | Verify evidence timeliness |

---

## SECTION 6: EVIDENCE DELIVERY

### 6.1 Delivery Formats

| Format | Description | Use Case |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **API Access** | Programmatic access to evidence | Automated compliance integration |
| **Dashboard View** | Visual evidence presentation | Internal audit review |
| **Report Package** | Structured report package | Regulatory submission |
| **Export Format** | Standard format (PDF, JSON, XML) | External audit review |

### 6.2 Delivery Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Format Selection** | Select delivery format |
| **2** | **Packet Preparation** | Prepare packet for delivery |
| **3** | **Delivery** | Deliver packet to recipient |
| **4** | **Verification** | Recipient verifies integrity |

---

## SECTION 7: AUDIT EVIDENCE ENGINE GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Audit Evidence Committee** | HAN, Audit Evidence Engine Admin, Compliance | Oversee audit evidence implementation |
| **Evidence Review Board** | HAN, External auditors | Review evidence quality and integrity |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Audit Evidence Health Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Audit Evidence Committee | Evidence coverage, integrity status |
| **Audit Evidence Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Summary of audit evidence |

---

## SECTION 8: AUDIT EVIDENCE ENGINE VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

An Audit Evidence Engine deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Audit Evidence Engine adopted** — Engine is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Evidence Sources** — All evidence sources are integrated.  
3. **Packet Structure** — Packet schema is operational.  
4. **Packaging Process** — Packaging sequence is operational.  
5. **Integrity Mechanisms** — Integrity mechanisms are operational.  
6. **Delivery Formats** — Delivery formats are operational.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I6, I8** | Constitutional parent. Audit Evidence Engine enforces auditability, transparency, and external legibility. |
| **IMP** | Evidence source. Audit Evidence Engine extracts evidence from IMP. |
| **ADTEP** | Evidence source. Audit Evidence Engine extracts enforcement logs. |
| **IPS Framework** | Evidence source. Audit Evidence Engine extracts IPS detection logs. |
| **AOBA** | Evidence source. Audit Evidence Engine extracts bias audit logs. |
| **ABA** | Evidence source. Audit Evidence Engine extracts authority bias logs. |
| **ERDP** | Disclosure. Audit Evidence Engine delivers evidence for ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Audit Evidence Engine is an automated evidence packaging system that extracts, organizes, and packages governance artifacts from IMP, ADTEP, IPS Framework, AOBA, ABA, CDF, and COF into audit-ready evidence packets—with cryptographic integrity verification, evidence chain preservation, and delivery in API, dashboard, report, and export formats."*

# COMPLIANCE DASHBOARD

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

The Compliance Dashboard is the central visualization and monitoring interface for the AIGIS Compliance Engine. It provides real-time visibility into compliance coverage, regulatory mapping, evidence status, and gaps. The dashboard transforms complex governance data into actionable intelligence for boards, executives, auditors, and regulators.

The Compliance Dashboard is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — compliance status must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — compliance must be externally legible  
- **MCR** (Master Control Registry) — control registry  
- **Compliance Crosswalk Engine** — regulatory mapping  
- **Audit Evidence Engine** — evidence status  
- **AICA-5 Measurement Framework** — governance health indicators

**Core insight:** Governance without visibility is theater. The Compliance Dashboard ensures that compliance status is visible, understandable, and actionable to all stakeholders with legitimate need.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Compliance Coverage** | The percentage of AIGIS controls that are operational and monitored. |
| **Regulatory Mapping Coverage** | The percentage of regulatory requirements mapped to AIGIS controls. |
| **Evidence Status** | The current status of evidence for each control. |
| **Compliance Gap** | A regulatory requirement not covered by an AIGIS control, or an AIGIS control not producing adequate evidence. |
| **Dashboard View** | A specific visualization of compliance data for a specific audience. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Compliance visualization | Compliance implementation |
| Regulatory mapping display | Evidence collection |
| Evidence status monitoring | Regulatory interpretation |
| Gap identification |  |
| Status reporting |  |

---

# AUTOMATED COMPLIANCE REMEDIATION

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

The Automated Compliance Remediation engine provides AI-driven recommendations for closing compliance gaps, prioritizing remediation actions, and automating the gap-to-remediation workflow. It answers: *How does AIGIS close the loop from detection to resolution, ensuring compliance gaps are not just identified but fixed?*

The Automated Compliance Remediation engine is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — remediation must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — remediation must preserve structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — remediation must preserve continuity  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — remediation must resist IPS capture  
- **Compliance Dashboard** — gap identification  
- **Systemic Risk Framework** — risk prioritization

**Core insight:** Identification without remediation is theater. The Automated Compliance Remediation engine ensures that gaps are not just identified but systematically addressed through prioritized, governed, and auditable remediation actions.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Compliance Gap** | A deviation from compliance requirements (regulatory, constitutional, or operational). |
| **Remediation Action** | A specific action taken to close a compliance gap. |
| **Remediation Priority** | A classification of remediation urgency based on risk and impact. |
| **Remediation Workflow** | The end-to-end process from gap identification to gap closure. |
| **Remediation Status** | The current status of a remediation action (Open, In Progress, Completed, Failed). |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Gap identification and classification | Gap policy (governed by governance) |
| Remediation recommendation generation | Remediation execution (governed by operational teams) |
| Remediation prioritization | Remediation verification (governed by audit) |
| Remediation workflow automation |  |
| IPS gap remediation |  |

---

## SECTION 2: GAP IDENTIFICATION AND CLASSIFICATION

### 2.1 Gap Types

| Gap Type | Description | Example |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Regulatory Gap** | Regulatory requirement not covered by AIGIS control | I10 not fully mapped to EU AI Act |
| **Constitutional Gap** | ICC-10 invariant not fully enforced | O-N6 not operational |
| **Operational Gap** | Control defined but not operational | AOBA pending IMP maturity |
| **Evidence Gap** | Control operational but evidence incomplete | Missing IPS detection logs |
| **IPS Gap** | IPS detection or mitigation incomplete | Capture point not closed |

### 2.2 Gap Classification

| Classification | Criteria | Response Time |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Critical** | Threatens ICC-10 invariants, systemic risk, or immediate regulatory violation | \<24 hours |
| **High** | Significant compliance or operational risk | \<7 days |
| **Medium** | Moderate compliance or operational risk | \<30 days |
| **Low** | Minor compliance or operational risk | \<90 days |

---

## SECTION 3: REMEDIATION RECOMMENDATION

### 3.1 Recommendation Generation

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Gap Analysis** | Analyze gap root cause |
| **2** | **Control Mapping** | Map to relevant controls |
| **3** | **Recommendation Generation** | Generate remediation recommendations |
| **4** | **Recommendation Validation** | Validate recommendation feasibility |

### 3.2 Recommendation Types

| Type | Description | Example |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Control Implementation** | Implement a new control | Deploy O-N6 |
| **Control Update** | Update an existing control | Update IMP schema for IPS objects |
| **Process Update** | Update a governance process | Add IPS screening to CEF Stage 1 |
| **Evidence Collection** | Collect missing evidence | Generate IPS detection logs |
| **Training** | Provide training | IPS awareness training |

---

## SECTION 4: REMEDIATION PRIORITIZATION

### 4.1 Prioritization Factors

| Factor | Weight | Description |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Risk Severity** | 30% | Impact on ICC-10 invariants and institutional risk |
| **Regulatory Urgency** | 25% | Regulatory deadline or enforcement risk |
| **Operational Impact** | 20% | Impact on operations and governance |
| **IPS Relevance** | 15% | Impact on IPS detection or mitigation |
| **Feasibility** | 10% | Ease and speed of remediation |

### 4.2 Prioritization Matrix

| Risk Severity | Regulatory Urgency | Priority |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Critical | High | P1 (Immediate) |
| Critical | Medium | P1 (Immediate) |
| High | High | P2 (7 Days) |
| High | Medium | P2 (7 Days) |
| Medium | High | P3 (30 Days) |
| Medium | Medium | P3 (30 Days) |
| Low | Any | P4 (90 Days) |

---

## SECTION 5: REMEDIATION WORKFLOW

### 5.1 Workflow Phases

| Phase | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Phase 1: Gap Identification** | Gap detected and classified | Compliance Dashboard | Continuous |
| **Phase 2: Recommendation** | Remediation recommendation generated | Automated Remediation Engine | Within 24 hours |
| **Phase 3: Prioritization** | Remediation prioritized | Automated Remediation Engine | Within 24 hours |
| **Phase 4: Approval** | HAN approves remediation plan | HAN | Within 7 days |
| **Phase 5: Execution** | Remediation executed | Operational Teams | Per priority |
| **Phase 6: Verification** | Remediation verified | Audit Team | Per priority |
| **Phase 7: Closure** | Gap closed | HAN \+ Compliance Team | Upon verification |

### 5.2 Workflow States

| State | Description | Transitions |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Open** | Gap identified, no action taken | In Progress |
| **In Progress** | Remediation underway | Completed, Failed |
| **Completed** | Remediation completed and verified | Closed |
| **Failed** | Remediation failed, needs review | Open, In Progress |
| **Closed** | Gap closed | N/A |

---

## SECTION 6: IPS GAP REMEDIATION

### 6.1 IPS Gap Types

| Gap Type | Description | Example |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **IPS Detection Gap** | IPS signals not instrumented | Missing behavioral signal detection |
| **IPS Escalation Gap** | IPS escalation path not defined | Level 3+ escalation undefined |
| **IPS Mitigation Gap** | IPS mitigation incomplete | Capture point not closed |
| **IPS Reporting Gap** | IPS reporting incomplete | Missing IPS disclosure in ERDP |

### 6.2 IPS Remediation Protocol

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **IPS Gap Identification** | IPS gap detected | O-N6 / IPS Analysis Team |
| **2** | **IPS Gap Classification** | Classify IPS gap severity | IPS Analysis Team |
| **3** | **IPS Remediation Recommendation** | Generate IPS remediation plan | Automated Remediation Engine |
| **4** | **HAN Approval** | HAN approves IPS remediation | HAN |
| **5** | **IPS Remediation Execution** | Execute IPS remediation | IPS Response Team |
| **6** | **IPS Remediation Verification** | Verify IPS remediation | IPS Analysis Team |
| **7** | **IPS Gap Closure** | IPS gap closed | HAN \+ IPS Analysis Team |

---

## SECTION 7: AUTOMATED COMPLIANCE REMEDIATION GOVERNANCE

### 7.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Remediation Committee** | HAN, CRO, Compliance Team | Oversee remediation implementation |
| **Remediation Review Board** | HAN, External auditors | Review remediation quality and integrity |

### 7.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Remediation Status Report** | Monthly | HAN, Remediation Committee | Gap status, remediation progress |
| **Remediation Completion Report** | Event-triggered | HAN, Board | Completed remediation actions |
| **Remediation Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Summary of remediation |

---

## SECTION 8: AUTOMATED COMPLIANCE REMEDIATION VALIDATION

### 8.1 Validation Requirements

An Automated Compliance Remediation deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Automated Compliance Remediation adopted** — Engine is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Gap Identification** — Gap identification and classification are operational.  
3. **Recommendation Generation** — Recommendation generation is operational.  
4. **Prioritization** — Prioritization is operational.  
5. **Workflow** — Remediation workflow is operational.  
6. **IPS Remediation** — IPS gap remediation is operational.

---

## SECTION 9: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I5, I7, I10** | Constitutional parent. Remediation enforces auditability, structural compliance, continuity, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. Remediation addresses IPS gaps. |
| **Compliance Dashboard** | Gap identification. Remediation uses Dashboard for gap identification. |
| **Systemic Risk Framework** | Prioritization. Remediation uses Systemic Risk Framework for prioritization. |
| **MCR** | Control registry. Remediation uses MCR for control mapping. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. Remediation requires HAN approval. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. Remediation records actions in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. Remediation contributes to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Automated Compliance Remediation provides AI-driven gap identification, recommendation generation, prioritization, and workflow automation—closing the loop from detection to resolution for regulatory, constitutional, operational, evidence, and IPS gaps—with critical remediation within 24 hours, high within 7 days, medium within 30 days, and low within 90 days."*

---



# REAL-TIME EVIDENCE STREAMING

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Compliance/Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Compliance Engine

---

## PREAMBLE

Real-Time Evidence Streaming transforms the Audit Evidence Engine from a batch-oriented system into a continuous, real-time evidence generation and streaming system. It enables continuous compliance monitoring, immediate gap detection, and real-time alerts. This ensures that evidence is not just collected periodically but streamed continuously to auditors, regulators, and stakeholders.

Real-Time Evidence Streaming is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — evidence must be continuous and complete  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — evidence must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — evidence must be externally legible  
- **Audit Evidence Engine** — evidence packaging  
- **Compliance Dashboard** — real-time monitoring

**Core insight:** Batch evidence is retrospective. Real-time evidence is proactive. Continuous streaming enables immediate detection of compliance drift, faster response to incidents, and real-time assurance for stakeholders.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Real-Time Evidence** | Evidence generated and streamed continuously, not collected in batches. |
| **Evidence Stream** | A continuous flow of evidence artifacts from sources to consumers. |
| **Streaming Latency** | The time between evidence generation and evidence delivery. |
| **Evidence Consumer** | Any system or stakeholder that consumes evidence streams. |
| **Stream Alert** | An automated notification triggered by evidence stream patterns. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Continuous evidence generation | Batch evidence collection (governed by Audit Evidence Engine) |
| Real-time evidence streaming | Evidence storage (governed by IMP) |
| Stream alerting | Evidence interpretation (governed by auditors) |

# GOVERNANCE & OVERSIGHT LAYER


# BOARD-LEVEL AI GOVERNANCE FRAMEWORK

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Governance Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Board-Level AI Governance Framework establishes a structured framework for boards and C-suites to articulate AI oversight to investors, regulators, and other stakeholders. It provides a Board AI Governance Charter, disclosure requirements, and reporting templates. This ensures that AI governance is not just an operational concern but a board-level strategic priority.

The Board-Level AI Governance Framework is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — board accountability for AI governance  
- **ICC-10 I6** (Transparency Gradient Invariance) — board disclosures must be transparent  
- **ICC-10 I8** (External Legibility Invariance) — board governance must be externally legible  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — boards must resist IPS capture  
- **HOF H2** (Board Reporting) — board oversight of HAN  
- **ERDP** (External Reporting & Disclosure) — board disclosures

**Core insight:** AI governance cannot succeed without board-level commitment. The Board-Level AI Governance Framework ensures that boards have the structure, tools, and accountability to oversee AI governance effectively.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Board AI Governance Charter** | A formal charter defining the board's AI governance responsibilities. |
| **Board AI Committee** | A board committee dedicated to AI governance oversight. |
| **Board Disclosure** | Formal disclosure of AI governance to stakeholders. |
| **Board AI Risk Appetite** | The board's declared appetite for AI-related risks. |
| **Board AI Oversight** | The board's oversight of AI governance and risk management. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Board AI governance structure | Operational AI governance (covered by operational instruments) |
| Board AI Committee | Day-to-day AI management |
| Board disclosures | AI development (covered by development) |
| Board risk appetite | AI deployment (covered by deployment) |

# EVOLUTIONARY LAYER


# DEP — DOCTRINE EXTENSION PROTOCOL

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Meta-Governance Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Evolutionary Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Doctrine Extension Protocol (DEP) is a meta-governance instrument that governs how the institutional framework stack may be extended, amended, superseded, or retired. It defines five signal types and four change classes, ensuring that all changes to the AIGIS stack are constitutionally governed, cohesively verified, and properly documented.

DEP is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I1** (Accountability Invariance) — changes must terminate at human accountability  
- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — changes must be reconstructable  
- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — changes must preserve MICOS-25 constraints  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — changes must preserve institutional continuity  
- **ICC-10 I10** (Anti-Shadow Invariance) — changes must not enable IPS capture

**Core insight:** The AIGIS stack must evolve, but evolution must be governed with the same constitutional rigor as operations. DEP ensures that changes are proposed, verified, approved, and implemented in a controlled manner, with full accountability and auditability.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Doctrine Extension** | Any change to an AIGIS instrument or the stack as a whole. |
| **Signal Type** | The nature of the proposed change. |
| **Change Class** | The scope and impact of the proposed change. |
| **Cohesion Impact Assessment** | Verification that changes do not break instrument dependencies. |
| **Change Proposal** | A formal proposal to extend, amend, supersede, or retire an instrument. |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Extensions to existing instruments | Operational changes (covered by operational protocols) |
| Amendments to existing instruments | Procedural changes (covered by procedures) |
| Supersession of existing instruments |  |
| Retirement of existing instruments |  |
| Cohesion Impact Assessment |  |
| Change approval and implementation |  |

---

## SECTION 2: SIGNAL TYPES

### 2.1 The Five Signal Types

| Signal | Name | Description | Example |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **S-1** | Extension Proposal | Proposal to add new content to an instrument | Adding a new invariant to ICC-10 |
| **S-2** | Amendment Proposal | Proposal to modify existing content | Updating HAN Scorecard metrics |
| **S-3** | Supersession Proposal | Proposal to replace an instrument with a new version | Replacing HOF v1.0 with HOF v2.0 |
| **S-4** | Retirement Proposal | Proposal to retire an instrument | Retiring a deprecated component |
| **S-5** | Cohesion Verification Request | Request to verify cohesion after changes | Verifying stack cohesion after amendments |

### 2.2 Signal Requirements

| Requirement | S-1 | S-2 | S-3 | S-4 | S-5 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Written Proposal** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Change Justification** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Impact Assessment** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Cohesion Assessment** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **HAN Approval** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Board Approval** | Yes (major) | Yes (major) | Yes | Yes | No |
| **IMP Recording** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |

---

## SECTION 3: CHANGE CLASSES

### 3.1 The Four Change Classes

| Class | Name | Description | Examples |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Class 1** | Minor | Non-material changes with no structural impact | Typos, clarifications, formatting |
| **Class 2** | Moderate | Material changes with limited structural impact | Metric updates, minor additions |
| **Class 3** | Major | Material changes with structural impact | New invariants, significant revisions |
| **Class 4** | Constitutional | Changes to constitutional instruments | ICC-10 amendments, HOF revisions |

### 3.2 Change Class Approval Requirements

| Requirement | Class 1 | Class 2 | Class 3 | Class 4 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **HAN Approval** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Board Approval** | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **Cohesion Impact Assessment** | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **CFL-V Verification** | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **Full Stack Review** | No | No | No | Yes |
| **External Review** | No | No | No | Yes |
| **IMP Recording** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **ERDP Disclosure** | No | No | Yes | Yes |

### 3.3 Change Class Documentation Requirements

| Documentation | Class 1 | Class 2 | Class 3 | Class 4 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Change Description** | Required | Required | Required | Required |
| **Change Justification** | Required | Required | Required | Required |
| **Impact Assessment** | Not Required | Required | Required | Required |
| **Cohesion Assessment** | Not Required | Required | Required | Required |
| **CFL-V Report** | Not Required | Not Required | Required | Required |
| **Full Stack Review** | Not Required | Not Required | Not Required | Required |
| **External Review** | Not Required | Not Required | Not Required | Required |

---

## SECTION 4: COHESION IMPACT ASSESSMENT

### 4.1 Assessment Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Verification |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Dependency Mapping** | Map all instruments affected by the change | Instrument dependency matrix |
| **Reference Impact** | Identify instruments referencing the changed instrument | Reference analysis |
| **Invariant Coverage** | Verify invariants still covered | ICC-10 coverage audit |
| **Terminology Consistency** | Verify terminology remains consistent | Terminology audit |
| **Implementation Impact** | Assess impact on operational implementation | Implementation review |

### 4.2 Assessment Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Change Proposal** | Propose change with justification | Proposer |
| **2** | **Dependency Mapping** | Map affected instruments | DEP Administrator |
| **3** | **Reference Impact** | Identify referencing instruments | DEP Administrator |
| **4** | **Invariant Coverage** | Verify invariant coverage | CFL-V |
| **5** | **Terminology Consistency** | Verify terminology consistency | DEP Administrator |
| **6** | **Assessment Report** | Generate cohesion assessment report | DEP Administrator |
| **7** | **HAN Review** | HAN reviews assessment | HAN |
| **8** | **Board Review** | Board reviews assessment (Class 3-4) | Board |

---

## SECTION 5: CHANGE IMPLEMENTATION PROTOCOL

### 5.1 Implementation Sequence

| Step | Activity | Responsible | Timeline |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Change Proposal** | Propose change | Proposer |
| **2** | **Impact Assessment** | Assess impact | DEP Administrator |
| **3** | **Cohesion Assessment** | Verify cohesion | DEP Administrator |
| **4** | **HAN Approval** | HAN approves | HAN |
| **5** | **Board Approval** | Board approves (Class 3-4) | Board |
| **6** | **Change Execution** | Execute change | Instrument Owner |
| **7** | **IMP Recording** | Record change in IMP | IMP |
| **8** | **Stack Version Map Update** | Update Stack Version Map | DEP Administrator |
| **9** | **ERDP Disclosure** | Disclose change (Class 3-4) | ERDP Administrator |

### 5.2 Implementation Verification

| Verification | Class 1 | Class 2 | Class 3 | Class 4 |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Change Executed** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **IMP Recorded** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Stack Version Map Updated** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **CFL-V Passed** | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **ERDP Disclosed** | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| **Full Stack Review Completed** | No | No | No | Yes |

---

## SECTION 6: DEP GOVERNANCE

### 6.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **DEP Committee** | DEP Administrator, HAN, Legal | Oversee DEP implementation |
| **Change Review Board** | HAN, Board, External reviewers | Review major and constitutional changes |
| **Cohesion Review Team** | Instrument owners, DEP Administrator | Conduct cohesion impact assessments |

### 6.2 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Change Log** | Quarterly | HAN, DEP Committee | All changes implemented |
| **Cohesion Assessment Report** | Per change | HAN, Board | Cohesion impact assessments |
| **DEP Annual Report** | Annual | Board, Regulators | Summary of DEP implementation |

---

## SECTION 7: DEP VALIDATION

### 7.1 Validation Requirements

A DEP deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **DEP Framework adopted** — DEP is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Signal Types** — Five signal types are defined and operational.  
3. **Change Classes** — Four change classes are defined and operational.  
4. **Cohesion Impact Assessment** — Cohesion Impact Assessment is operational.  
5. **Change Implementation** — Change implementation protocol is operational.  
6. **Governance Bodies** — Governance bodies are established.

---

## SECTION 8: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I1, I3, I5, I7, I10** | Constitutional parent. DEP enforces accountability, auditability, structural compliance, continuity, and anti-shadow invariants. |
| **IPS Framework** | Anti-shadow. DEP ensures changes do not enable IPS capture. |
| **Stack Version Map** | Versioning. DEP updates Stack Version Map. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. DEP requires HAN approval for changes. |
| **IMP** | Institutional memory. DEP records changes in IMP. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. DEP contributes change disclosure to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"DEP is a meta-governance instrument governing AIGIS stack evolution through five signal types (Extension, Amendment, Supersession, Retirement, Cohesion Verification) and four change classes (Minor, Moderate, Major, Constitutional)—with mandatory Cohesion Impact Assessment for all material changes, HAN approval, Board approval for major changes, and full IMP recording and ERDP disclosure."*

---



# STACK VERSION MAP

**Status:** Built — AIGIS Alpha  
**Type:** Governance/Reference Instrument  
**Parent Stack:** AIGIS Evolutionary Layer

---

## PREAMBLE

The Stack Version Map is a living record of the current canonical version of every instrument. It maintains governance version binding for all IMP objects, ensuring that every institutional state object is traceable to the version of the stack that governed its creation. This enables historical reconstruction, auditability, and continuity across stack versions.

The Stack Version Map is derived from:

- **ICC-10 I3** (Auditability Invariance) — version binding must be auditable  
- **ICC-10 I5** (Structural Compliance Invariance) — versioning must preserve structural constraints  
- **ICC-10 I7** (Continuity Invariance) — versioning must preserve continuity  
- **DEP** (Doctrine Extension Protocol) — versioning governance  
- **IMP** — version binding

**Core insight:** A governance stack without versioning is ungovernable. The Stack Version Map ensures that every instrument, every object, and every action is traceable to a specific version of the stack—enabling auditability, continuity, and evolution.

---

## SECTION 1: DEFINITIONS AND SCOPE

### 1.1 Core Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Stack Version** | A specific version of the AIGIS stack as a whole. |
| **Instrument Version** | A specific version of an individual instrument. |
| **Version Binding** | The association between an IMP object and the stack version that governed its creation. |
| **Version Map** | The complete record of all instrument versions and their relationships. |
| **Version Status** | The current status of a version (Active, Superseded, Deprecated). |

### 1.2 Scope

| In Scope | Out of Scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| Stack versioning | Instrument content (governed by instruments) |
| Instrument versioning | Implementation details |
| Version binding | Operational execution |
| Version map maintenance |  |

---

## SECTION 2: VERSION MAP STRUCTURE

### 2.1 Stack Version

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **stack\_version** | String | Yes | Unique stack version identifier |
| **release\_date** | DateTime | Yes | Stack release date |
| **status** | Enum | Yes | Alpha, Active, Superseded, Deprecated |
| **instruments** | Array | Yes | List of instrument versions included |
| **dependencies** | Array | Yes | Stack dependencies |
| **changelog** | Array | Yes | Summary of changes from previous version |

### 2.2 Instrument Version

| Field | Type | Required | Definition |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **instrument\_name** | String | Yes | Instrument name |
| **instrument\_version** | String | Yes | Instrument version |
| **status** | Enum | Yes | Active, Superseded, Deprecated |
| **stack\_version** | String | Yes | Stack version containing this instrument |
| **dependencies** | Array | Yes | Instrument dependencies |
| **changelog** | Array | Yes | Summary of changes |

## SECTION 4: VERSION BINDING

### 4.1 Binding Requirements

| Requirement | Description | Enforcement |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Object Binding** | Every IMP object must be bound to a stack version | IMP |
| **Version Traceability** | Objects must trace to the version that governed their creation | IMP |
| **Version Integrity** | Version bindings must be cryptographically verifiable | IMP |
| **Version Continuity** | Version bindings must preserve continuity | IMP |

### 4.2 Binding Process

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Object Creation** | IMP object created |
| **2** | **Version Retrieval** | Current stack version retrieved |
| **3** | **Version Binding** | Object bound to stack version |
| **4** | **Binding Record** | Binding recorded in object metadata |

---

## SECTION 5: VERSION GOVERNANCE

### 5.1 Governance Bodies

| Body | Composition | Function |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Version Committee** | HAN, DEP Administrator, Stack Version Map Admin | Oversee versioning |
| **Version Review Board** | HAN, External reviewers | Review version changes |

### 5.2 Version Update Process

| Step | Activity | Responsible |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **1** | **Change Proposal** | Change proposed |
| **2** | **DEP Review** | Change reviewed |
| **3** | **Impact Assessment** | Impact assessed |
| **4** | **Version Update** | Version map updated |
| **5** | **Communication** | Changes communicated |

### 5.3 Reporting and Disclosure

| Report | Frequency | Audience | Content |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Version Map Report** | Quarterly | HAN, Version Committee | Current version map |
| **Version Change Report** | Per change | HAN, Board | Version changes |

---

## SECTION 6: STACK VERSION MAP VALIDATION

### 6.1 Validation Requirements

A Stack Version Map deployment is compliant if and only if:

1. **Stack Version Map adopted** — Map is integrated into AIGIS operations.  
2. **Stack Version** — Stack version is defined.  
3. **Instrument Versions** — All instrument versions are defined.  
4. **Version Binding** — Version binding is operational.  
5. **Version Governance** — Version governance is operational.

---

## SECTION 7: RELATIONSHIP TO OTHER INSTRUMENTS

| Instrument | Relationship |
| :---- | :---- |
| **ICC-10 I3, I5, I7** | Constitutional parent. Map enforces auditability, structural compliance, and continuity. |
| **DEP** | Evolution. Map records DEP changes. |
| **IMP** | Version binding. Map binds objects to versions. |
| **HAN** | Human authority. HAN oversees version governance. |
| **ERDP** | External reporting. Map contributes version reporting to ERDP. |

---

## The One-Sentence Summary

> *"Stack Version Map is a living record of all instrument versions—maintaining version binding for all IMP objects, ensuring traceability, auditability, and continuity across stack versions, with governance through DEP and version binding requirements for every IMP object."*
